Biomass, Fisheries, PB/QB-Traits & Diet Pipeline

  • Show All Code
  • Hide All Code
  • View Source

Code documentation for the four EwE input-building scripts — biomass, fisheries, PB/QB & traits, diet — for any GFCM GSA subset or user-defined region in the Mediterranean

Published

September 17, 2026

1 Purpose of this document

This document describes a general-purpose R pipeline for turning MEDITS/ MEDIAS trawl-survey and catch data into input sheets for an Ecopath-with- Ecosim (EwE) model, for any region in the Mediterranean Sea — a single GFCM Geographical Sub-Area (GSA), any subset or the full set of GSAs, or a completely user-defined region (a bounding box or an arbitrary shapefile that doesn’t line up with GSA lines at all, e.g. a bay, an MPA, or a footprint spanning parts of several GSAs). It covers every script provided, what each one reads and writes, and every function in the shared library those scripts call.

WarningSome older sections may not reflect every recent change

This document has been updated for the 2026-09-17 per-block output CSV subfolder change and the new FG_References sheet (see the shared-library section below), and the top-level “Pipeline overview” and “Excel workbook functions” diagrams have been brought current. A handful of older sections elsewhere in the document may still describe an earlier script naming or workbook layout that predates those changes — if something here disagrees with a script’s own header comment or with medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd, the code and that methodology document are the source of truth.

The scripts as delivered are configured for the Western Mediterranean (GSA 1–11) as a worked example — this is what 01_biomass.R, export_gsa_boundaries.R and fig_WMed_basemap.R currently point at, and what a few reference tables (e.g. the MEDIAS Handbook area crosswalk) are currently populated for. The underlying mechanism — GSA-based selection via FILTER_AREAS, or an arbitrary region via 01_survey_density_custom.R — is not Western-Med-specific; see Adapting this pipeline to a different region below for exactly what to change to point the same pipeline at another part of the Mediterranean.

All scripts read from and write to one shared workbook, output/ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx, via two library functions designed for exactly this purpose:

  • upsert_workbook_sheets() — adds/replaces named sheets, leaves every other sheet in the workbook untouched. Safe to call from any script, in any order, any number of times.
  • finalize_workbook_sheet_order() — run last, after every upstream script has run at least once. Renames a couple of sheets (FG_lookup → FG, References → PB_QB_References_) and reorders every sheet into the final layout Ecopath/Ecosim expects.

Because of this design, the four numbered pipeline scripts are order-independent with respect to the shared workbook — except where a script’s own calculations literally need a file another script produced first (documented per-script below).

2 Data and code locations

  • Code — every script and library file documented here lives in the WestMed EwE GitHub repository (WMed_EwE, cloned locally to what the scripts call git_dir). All shared-library source() calls (lib_survey_fg_density_functions.R, lib_worms_taxonomy_lookup.R, lib_build_species_df_from_survey.R) resolve against this repository’s scripts/ folder.
  • Input data — every raw survey, catch and reference file each script reads (FG_WMed.xlsx, TM_list_(April_2019).xlsx, MEDITS TA.csv/ TB.csv, MEDIAS abundance.csv/biomass.csv, ASFIS_sp_2026.1.csv, the catchability CSV, GFCM capture/reference CSVs) is read from the WestMed EwE pCloud drive (EwE Western Med 2026, mounted/synced locally to what the scripts call pcloud_dir), not from the GitHub repository — the repository holds code only, no raw data.
  • Outputs — every CSV, plot, and the shared ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx workbook are written to a local out_dir (not synced back to pCloud or GitHub by the scripts themselves).

Throughout this document, <pcloud_dir> means the WestMed EwE pCloud drive and <git_dir> means the WestMed EwE GitHub repository — both are plain local paths the top of each script resolves (hardcoded for one user, or picked interactively via rstudioapi::selectDirectory() for anyone else), not fixed locations baked into the code. Retargeting the pipeline to a different region (see Adapting this pipeline to a different region) does not require moving off pCloud/GitHub — only pointing pcloud_dir/ git_dir at that region’s own drive/repository, or adding that region’s data alongside the Western Med data in the same drive/repository.

NoteA note on the plots and column tables in this document

This documentation was written without direct access to the WestMed EwE pCloud drive’s actual data files. The column-reference tables throughout are not guesses — they list exactly the column names each script’s own code reads (select()/merge()/setnames() calls, etc.), so they’re accurate regardless of the actual data values. The figures, however, are the pipeline’s own real output and only appear once you’ve generated them — see Populating the real figures below.

2.1 Populating the real figures

Several sections below embed a figure via a small safe_include_graphics() helper (defined in this document’s setup chunk). Each one checks for a file under figures/ (a folder that must sit next to this .Rmd) and shows the real image if present, or a plain “not yet available” message with the exact script/output path to copy from if not — so this document always knits cleanly either way. To populate them for real:

  1. Run the relevant script (named in each placeholder message) against the real WestMed EwE pCloud drive data.
  2. Copy the named .png from that script’s <out_dir>/plots/ into a figures/ folder next to this .Rmd, keeping the same filename.
  3. Re-knit.

Four other diagrams in this document (the pipeline flow, the FG-matching cascade, the workbook sheet assembly, and the PB/QB taxon dispatch diagrams) are a different kind of figure: Mermaid flowcharts, in the same style as medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd’s own diagrams (same flowchart LR layout, the same gray/purple/teal/coral classDef palette). They show the code’s own logic — which function calls which, which sheet gets written by what, which fallback branch runs when — built directly from the script and library contents described here, not from any survey or model output. They’re written as native Quarto ```{mermaid} code chunks, so Quarto renders them to an inline SVG at render time (no CDN, no external script tag, no internet connection needed to view the finished HTML) — unlike the four safe_include_graphics() placeholders above, they always render, with or without access to the pCloud drive.

3 Try it: pick your years and region

The parameters below (YEAR_ECOPATH, TS_YEARS, FILTER_AREAS/AREA_NAME/CUSTOM_BBOX, DROP_OUTLIERS, NORMALIZE_TS, STRATA) are the ones actually exposed as top-level variables in 01_biomass.R’s and 01_survey_density_custom.R’s own ## STEP 1: Configuration blocks. Adjust them here to get a ready-to-paste block for whichever script you’re using - it just mirrors the real variable names back at you, it doesn’t run anything.

NoteThis generates a config block, not a live result

This is a plain HTML/JS form, evaluated entirely in your browser when the rendered document is open - there’s no R behind it. It can’t execute the pipeline or show you an actual result; you still run the real .R script yourself, with the block below pasted in at its Configuration step.

The diagram below is a schematic of the actual script/function call structure documented in this document — which script produces which intermediate file, and which workbook sheets each one writes. It is not a plot of any survey data or model output (same diagram style as medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd’s “Pipeline flow” section).

Step 1 — pick ONE:

  • 01_biomass.R (MEDITS + MEDIAS, GFCM GSA areas), or
  • 01_survey_density_custom.R (MEDITS only, custom bbox/shapefile)

↓ produces species_density_regional_combined.csv, strata_area_by_area.csv, and sheets FG_spp_Ecopath, Ecopath, Ecosim, FG_spp_Ecosim, FG (renamed from FG_lookup), traits_ewe.

Step 2:

  • 02_fao_catches.R (reads Step 1’s strata_area_by_area.csv)

↓ produces sheets Catches_Ecopath, Catches_Ecosim.

Step 3 — independent, can run anytime before Step 4:

  • 03b_ecobase.R

↓ produces ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv (optional Step 4 input).

Step 4 — run last:

  • 03_pbqb-traits.R (needs Step 1’s species_density_regional_combined.csv; optionally reads Step 2’s fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv and Step 3’s ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv)

↓ produces sheets PB_QB, PB_QB_spp, Ecobase, References (renamed to PB_QB_References_).

Finally, finalize_workbook_sheet_order() — called from whichever script runs last — reorders every sheet into the final layout:

↓ output/ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx, final, ordered, ready for Ecopath import.

Two more files are not part of the numbered pipeline, but support it:

  • lib_survey_fg_density_functions.R and lib_worms_taxonomy_lookup.R and lib_build_species_df_from_survey.R — shared library files, source()’d by the numbered scripts, no top-level driver code of their own.
  • export_gsa_boundaries.R and fig_WMed_basemap.R — standalone cartography utilities, not required by the numbered pipeline. export_gsa_boundaries.R in particular produces the GeoJSON embedded in medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd’s interactive map.

5 Run order and dependencies

Step Script Depends on Must run before
1 01_biomass.R or 01_survey_density_custom.R (pick one) nothing 2, 4
2 02_fao_catches.R Step 1’s strata_area_by_area.csv —
2a 02a_fisheries_multisource.R (optional, alongside Step 2) Step 1’s strata_area_by_area.csv and FG reference; optionally Step 2’s fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv for the FAO-GFCM source 4 (optional input, via FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE)
3 03b_ecobase.R nothing (independent web query) 4 (optional input)
4 03_pbqb-traits.R Step 1’s species_density_regional_combined.csv; optionally Step 2’s/Step 2a’s fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv, Step 2a’s landings_by_species_gsa_year_*.csv, and Step 3’s ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv —

finalize_workbook_sheet_order() should be called at the end of whichever script ran last in a given session (each of the numbered scripts already does this itself at its own final step).

6 Script 1a — 01_biomass.R

Role: Pipeline Step 1 of 4, GFCM-GSA-based version — the general mechanism for any GSA subset anywhere in the Mediterranean, not just the Western Med. Run this or 01_survey_density_custom.R, not both, as Step 1. No dependency on the other numbered scripts; 02_fao_catches.R and 03_pbqb-traits.R both require this step’s output to exist first.

Runs two independent surveys over the same GSAs/FG scheme (MEDITS bottom trawl + MEDIAS acoustic) and combines both into one set of Ecopath/Ecosim sheets, rather than producing separate MEDIAS sheets. As delivered it’s configured for the Western Med (GSA 1–11, via FILTER_AREAS <- 1:11) as a worked example; retargeting it to a different GSA subset elsewhere in the Mediterranean — the Adriatic, the Aegean, the whole basin — is a matter of changing that one line and the region-specific reference tables listed in Adapting this pipeline to a different region.

6.1 Inputs

File Format Role
<pcloud_dir>/data/FG_WMed.xlsx, sheet 4 xlsx Species → Functional Group reference (dataframe2: ESPECIE→ScientificName, GF→FG_num, FG_name)
<pcloud_dir>/data/FG_WMed.xlsx, sheet traits_ewe xlsx Per-species life-history/ecology traits
<pcloud_dir>/.../TM_list_(April_2019).xlsx xlsx MEDITS species-code → scientific-name lookup
<pcloud_dir>/.../Demersal/TA.csv csv MEDITS haul/sample metadata (position, depth, gear, swept-area inputs)
<pcloud_dir>/.../Demersal/TB.csv csv MEDITS catch-per-haul data
<pcloud_dir>/.../Acoustic/abundance.csv, biomass.csv csv MEDIAS acoustic-survey raw records
<pcloud_dir>/data/ASFIS_sp_2026.1.csv csv FAO ASFIS species code list (MEDIAS species-code → scientific name)
<pcloud_dir>/data/catchability_factors_ecotrans_medits_2021_spp.csv csv (optional) Per-species catchability correction factors (q); skipped with a message if absent
GFCM GSA shapefile auto-downloaded zip from gfcmsitestorage.blob.core.windows.net/website/5.Data/ArcGIS/GFCM_GSA.zip Study-area polygons (area_shp); SMU_CODE 111/112 (W/E Sardinia) merged into GSA 11
NOAA bathymetry auto-downloaded via marmap::getNOAA.bathy() inside compute_strata_area_by_area() Depth-stratum area calculation

FILTER_AREAS <- 1:11 (this example’s Western Med GSAs — set to any GSA number or set of GSA numbers, anywhere in the GFCM area, to retarget the whole script to a different region), YEAR_ECOPATH <- 1994:1996, TS_YEARS <- 1995:2023, STRATA <- TRUE, DROP_OUTLIERS <- FALSE, NORMALIZE_TS <- TRUE, APPLY_AQUAMAPS_DEPTH_ADJUSTMENT <- FALSE (opt-in — see Step 7b below).

6.1.1 Step 7b — AquaMaps shallow/deep depth adjustment (opt-in)

MEDITS’ own 5 standard strata (MEDITS_STRATA) only cover 10–800 m. compute_strata_area_by_area()’s area-proportion weighting normalizes across just those 5 strata, so as delivered, this pipeline’s Biomass/ density figures implicitly treat the whole modeled area as if it were entirely 10–800 m seafloor — a species with real biomass shallower than 10 m or deeper than 800 m is invisible to these numbers, not just imprecisely measured. Setting APPLY_AQUAMAPS_DEPTH_ADJUSTMENT <- TRUE (default FALSE) adds genuine derived pseudo-strata: one shallow (0–10 m) plus, by default, the deep zone split into two sub-bands — 800–1000 m and 1000–2850 m — rather than one lumped 800–6000 m stratum (2850 m is a practical ceiling for the western Mediterranean’s own real depths specifically, not the Mediterranean-wide ~5267 m Calypso Deep, which sits in the eastern/Ionian basin outside these GSAs). Each pseudo-stratum gets its own real bathymetric area (the same compute_strata_area_by_area() machinery already used for the 5 real strata) and a density derived, not measured, anchored to the ONE real MEDITS stratum immediately adjacent to it — the shallowest real stratum (10–50 m) for the shallow pseudo-band, the deepest real stratum (500–800 m) for every deep pseudo-band — rather than an area-weighted average across all 5 real strata. Each pseudo-stratum’s density equals that adjacent stratum’s own measured density (compute_species_ anchor_stratum_density()), scaled by the ratio of AquaMaps mean suitability between the pseudo-band and that same adjacent stratum’s own depth range. A species at equal AquaMaps suitability in both bands gets a multiplier of exactly 1 — its pseudo-stratum density literally equals what MEDITS measured next door, with no distortion from averaging across strata the species may barely occupy. (An earlier version of this adjustment compared each pseudo-band’s suitability against the whole 10–800 m composite range instead — that systematically understated narrow pseudo-bands relative to the ~790 m-wide reference purely from the width mismatch, independent of actual suitability level; the anchor-stratum design replaces it.) The trade-off: a species with real catch elsewhere in 10–800 m but none specifically in the one adjacent anchor stratum gets no pseudo-stratum density at all for that area (flagged in the audit sheet), rather than being extrapolated from unrelated, non-adjacent strata. These pseudo-strata are folded into the exact same weight_by_strata()/weight_species_by_area() calls used for the 5 real strata — not a separate formula — so fg_index/ fg_index_regional/species_density_regional end up as one consistent area-weighted sum across all 8 strata (5 real + 3 derived), real and derived alike. The number and boundaries of deep sub-bands are controlled by apply_aquamaps_depth_adjustment()’s deep_ranges argument — a list of c(from, to) pairs — so this can be collapsed back to one lump (list(c(800, 6000))) or split further without changing anything else; all deep sub-bands anchor to the same deepest real stratum, they don’t chain off each other.

Core assumption, stated plainly rather than absorbed silently: a species’ relative density across depth is assumed proportional to AquaMaps’ relative occurrence-suitability across depth (its trapezoidal DepthMin/DepthPrefMin/DepthPrefMax/DepthMax envelope, per AquaMaps’ own “Algorithm and Data Sources” documentation). Occurrence suitability is not the same thing as density — this is a standard extrapolation approach when direct sampling doesn’t cover a species’ full depth range, but an approximation nonetheless. Every resulting multiplier is written to the AquaMaps_Depth_Adjustment sheet (species, its DepthMin/DepthPrefMin/DepthPrefMax/DepthMax, probability masses, resulting multiplier_shallow/multiplier_deep_800_1000/ multiplier_deep_1000_2850 (one column per configured deep sub-band, named by its own depth bounds), and a note explaining species that got NO adjustment for a given band — unmatched to AquaMaps, no depth envelope in am_hspen(), or essentially zero AquaMaps suitability in that band’s own adjacent real anchor stratum, making a ratio against that unstable) — reviewable per species, not a black box.

Requires the aquamapsdata R package (github.com/raquamaps/aquamapsdata), the only verified way to get AquaMaps’ per-species HSPEN depth-envelope table without scraping — its download_db() pulls a real, deliberate ~2GB (~10GB unpacked) local SQLite database once (ensure_aquamaps_db() uses force = FALSE, so this never re-downloads on subsequent runs). Implemented in lib_aquamaps_depth_extension.R (species → AquaMaps SpeciesID resolution, envelope fetch, envelope math, pseudo-strata construction) — see that file’s own header for the full mechanism. Requires STRATA <- TRUE (errors clearly if not — there’s no unstratified equivalent). Not yet wired into 01_survey_density_custom.R — only 01_biomass.R has this option so far.

Two outputs make the strata (real + derived) inspectable directly, independent of APPLY_AQUAMAPS_DEPTH_ADJUSTMENT — they cover whichever strata are actually in play, 5 or 8:

  • survey_fg_depth_strata_profile.png (Step 8, plot_strata_profile()) — a bar chart of raw per-sample density by stratum, faceted per FG, for sanity-checking that known deep-water FGs actually peak in the deep strata and vice versa. Reads weight_by_strata()’s per_stratum_raw attribute specifically (not per_stratum), so a stratum with real sample data but no computed area-proportion for a given AreaID still shows up here rather than silently vanishing.
  • FG_Density_by_Stratum sheet (Step 12c, build_fg_density_by_stratum_sheet()) — the same information as an Excel table: one row per FG, one column per stratum (region-wide, area-weighted, YEAR_ECOPATH-averaged), plus a Total_Density column that reproduces fg_index_regional’s own mean_density exactly, since each stratum column is that stratum’s own additive contribution to the total, not an independently-normalized figure.

MEDITS TA.csv (haul/sample metadata):

Column Used for
country, area, vessel, year, haul_number, month, day Built into SampleID (concatenated, _-separated)
distance Trawl distance towed → swept area
wing_opening Trawl wing opening (decimetres) → swept area
shooting_depth, hauling_depth Averaged into mean_depth
shooting_latitude, shooting_longitude DDMM.mmm format — converted to decimal degrees
shooting_quadrant Present but not used — flagged in the code as an unresolved caveat around hemisphere sign

MEDITS TB.csv (catch-per-haul data):

Column Used for
country, area, vessel, year, haul_number, month, day Built into SampleID, joined against TA.csv
genus, species Concatenated into species_code, joined against TM_list
ptot Total catch weight (grams; negative values treated as NA, converted to tonnes)

MEDIAS abundance.csv / biomass.csv (acoustic survey raw records):

Column Used for
country, year, species Grouping key for aggregation
area Text field containing a GSA number, extracted via regex (\d+)
sex Filtered to "C" (combined sexes) when present
lengthclass* (many columns) Summed row-wise into a single total_value (negative values treated as missing)

ASFIS_sp_2026.1.csv (FAO species reference list):

Column Used for
Alpha3_Code MEDIAS species code → joined against abundance/biomass
Scientific_Name Resolved scientific name
(also present: ISSCAAP_Group, Taxonomic_Code, English_name, …) Not read by this pipeline

Catchability CSV (catchability_factors_ecotrans_medits_2021_spp.csv, optional):

Column Renamed to Meaning
species ScientificName Species scientific name
q_FACTOR q Catchability correction factor

6.2 Processing steps

  1. Config — resolve out_dir/pcloud_dir/git_dir, download/cache the GSA shapefile, source lib_survey_fg_density_functions.R and lib_worms_taxonomy_lookup.R.

  2. Load MEDITS data — read TA.csv/TB.csv, compute swept area (distance × wing_opening_m / 1e6, wing opening in decimetres), convert DDMM.mmm lat/lon to decimal degrees, fix the Alboran Sea (GSA 1–3) longitude sign — a Western-Med-specific correction, harmless (a no-op) if your FILTER_AREAS doesn’t include GSA 1–3 — reshape into the standardized dataframe1/dataframe2 format. Runs validate_survey_data() and plot_sample_map() (→ survey_sample_coverage_map.png, survey_sample_coverage_map_by_year.png).

  3. FG matching (Steps 3–4) — prepare_fg_lookup() → match_species_to_fg() → match_nominate_subspecies_fg() → manual overrides (species codes absent from the MEDITS taxonomic list, e.g. ARGRACU→Argyropelecus aculeatus) → fallback_match_fg_by_taxonomy() (Genus→Family→Order→Class→Phylum) → apply_seed_fg_rules() (FGs with zero reference species, e.g. Holothuroidea→“Sea cucumbers”) → summarize_unresolved_species() → survey_unmatched_for_manual_review.csv.

  4. Densities (Step 5) — match_samples_to_area(), assign_depth_stratum(), compute_sample_densities(), restrict to FILTER_AREAS, apply_catchability_correction() (if the catchability CSV exists), remove_sample_outliers() (→ survey_outliers_flagged.csv if any flagged), compute_fg_densities_by_stratum().

  5. Strata/area weighting (Steps 6–7) — compute_strata_area_by_area() (cached to strata_area_by_area.csv), weight_by_strata(), weight_by_area(), compute_species_densities_by_stratum(), weight_species_by_area().

  6. MEDITS plots (Step 8) — plot_fg_timeseries_by_area(), plot_fg_timeseries_regional(), plot_strata_profile() → survey_fg_density_timeseries.png, survey_fg_density_timeseries_regional.png, survey_fg_depth_strata_profile.png.

  7. MEDIAS acoustic survey (Step 9, sub-steps 9a–9g) — load and collapse raw abundance/biomass records, map species codes via ASFIS, match to FG the same way as MEDITS, attach area (MEDIAS Handbook Table 1, hand-keyed MEDIAS_HANDBOOK_AREAS — as delivered this table only has Western Med entries, since that’s this example’s scope; extending to another region means adding that region’s own Handbook rows, with a bathymetry-derived 10–200 m fallback via compute_strata_area_by_area() covering any country/GSA combination the table doesn’t have a row for), compute region-wide FG and species density, then combine MEDITS + MEDIAS into one FG-level and one species-level table (averaged on Year/FG or Year/species overlap, overlaps flagged/printed). Outputs: medias_fg_annual_density_by_area.csv, medias_fg_annual_density_regional.csv, medias_fg_biomass_density_timeseries_regional.png, medias_fg_abundance_density_timeseries_regional.png, species_density_regional_combined.csv (contract file — read by lib_build_species_df_from_survey.R for 03_pbqb-traits.R).

  8. Excel export (Step 10) — survey_fg_annual_index.csv, survey_fg_annual_index_regional_combined.csv, then export_ecopath_ecosim_excel() writes sheets FG_spp_Ecopath, Ecopath, Ecosim, FG_spp_Ecosim into ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx. Also writes a PB_QB_spp scaffold — reusing FG_spp_Ecopath’s own Species/ FG_num/Density/prop_sp_fg (renamed to PB_QB_spp’s convention: Biomass, prop_biomass_FG), with PB/prop_biomass_PB/QB/ prop_biomass_QB all blank and a Note column — so PB_QB_spp exists in the workbook after this script alone, without needing 03_pbqb-traits.R (Step 4) to have run first. This function has no PB/QB computation of its own (that’s entirely Step 4’s, from life-history parameters, not survey density), so the scaffold’s numbers are genuinely blank, not estimated. Running Step 4 afterward replaces the scaffold with the real sheet automatically (upsert_workbook_sheets() always fully replaces a same-named sheet). Guarded against clobbering the other way: if PB_QB_spp already has real (non-NA) PB values — i.e. Step 4 already ran against this exact workbook — re-running this script leaves it untouched rather than overwriting it with a blank scaffold again.

  9. Complete FG_spp_Ecopath/FG_lookup (Step 11) — union of observed + reference-catalog species, zero-fill species with no base-year density, attach taxonomy, defensive check for FGs with no species at all. Written via upsert_workbook_sheets().

  10. traits_ewe sheet (Step 12) — parse FG_WMed.xlsx’s traits_ewe sheet (alternating “N: Group Name” header rows / species rows, fill-down logic), reconcile against dataframe2, flag naming-variant and missing-traits species. Written via upsert_workbook_sheets().

  11. Finalize sheet order (Step 13) — finalize_workbook_sheet_order().

6.3 Outputs

  • CSVs (in out_dir): survey_sample_coverage_map.png and other plot PNGs (in out_dir/plots), survey_unmatched_for_manual_review.csv, survey_outliers_flagged.csv, strata_area_by_area.csv, medias_fg_annual_density_by_area.csv, medias_fg_annual_density_regional.csv, species_density_regional_combined.csv, survey_fg_annual_index.csv, survey_fg_annual_index_regional_combined.csv.
  • Workbook ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx, sheets written/updated: FG_spp_Ecopath, Ecopath, Ecosim, FG_spp_Ecosim, FG_lookup (renamed to FG), traits_ewe, PB_QB_spp (scaffold, unless it already has real data from 03_pbqb-traits.R), AquaMaps_Depth_Adjustment (only when APPLY_AQUAMAPS_DEPTH_ADJUSTMENT <- TRUE — see Step 7b), and FG_Density_by_Stratum (only when STRATA <- TRUE — Step 12c; density by FG x depth stratum, region-wide, covering whichever strata are actually in play).

7 Script 1b — 01_survey_density_custom.R

Role: Alternative Step 1, for any study area anywhere in the Mediterranean that doesn’t line up with GSA boundaries — a single custom bounding box or shapefile (e.g. one EwE model’s own footprint, a bay, an MPA, or a region spanning parts of several GSAs, in any part of the basin). This is the general-purpose route for a fully user-defined region; Script 1a above is the general-purpose route for any GSA-aligned region instead. Run this instead of 01_biomass.R, never both. MEDITS-only — does not run the MEDIAS acoustic survey.

Structurally identical to 01_biomass.R for the MEDITS loading and FG-matching stages (Steps 1–4 use the exact same shared-library functions). What’s genuinely different:

  • AREA_NAME — a user-supplied label for this custom study area (typed interactively via rstudioapi::showPrompt(), or hardcoded for the daniel user). Used to build a dedicated output subfolder (out_dir/<AREA_NAME>/) and to suffix every output filename via helper functions af()/pf(), so two different custom-region runs (different AREA_NAMEs) never collide or overwrite each other’s outputs, and never collide with 01_biomass.R’s own out_dir.
  • Study-area definition (Step 1b) — CUSTOM_AREA_TYPE <- "bbox" (a rectangle defined by CUSTOM_BBOX <- c(xmin, xmax, ymin, ymax)), "shapefile" (an arbitrary sf polygon read via st_read()), or "gsa" (one or more GFCM GSAs, picked out of the same GSA shapefile 01_biomass.R downloads, via CUSTOM_GSA_IDS <- c(...) — e.g. a single GSA, or a different regional grouping than that script’s fixed 1:11 Western-Med range). For "bbox"/"shapefile" the result is a single-feature area_shp with AREA_ID_COL = "area_id", always area_id = 1 — there is no GSA sub-division inside a custom region unless the user builds one. For "gsa", area_shp can have one row per selected GSA, each keeping its own gsa_num as area_id (same convention as Script 1a), so a multi-GSA custom region is NOT forced down to a single area_id = 1.
  • AreaID is derived spatially for "bbox"/"shapefile", not read off MEDITS’ own area (GSA) column — every sample gets AreaID = 1, then filter_samples_by_area() drops everything outside the custom boundary. For "gsa", AreaID follows the selected GSA(s) directly, same as Script 1a.
  • Strata-area computation (Step 6) — compute_strata_area_by_area() called with area_ids = 1 for "bbox"/"shapefile" (a single custom polygon), or with the selected CUSTOM_GSA_IDS for "gsa" (one or more named GSAs) — the function itself is agnostic to this distinction either way.

Exception to the AREA_NAME filename-suffixing rule: three files stay at plain, unsuffixed names on purpose, because 02_fao_catches.R and 03_pbqb-traits.R read them by that exact fixed name regardless of which Step-1 script produced them: species_density_regional_combined.csv, ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx, strata_area_by_area.csv. The dedicated AREA_NAME subfolder is what keeps these three from colliding with another run’s copies.

7.1 Inputs

Same as 01_biomass.R minus the MEDIAS/ASFIS/GSA-shapefile inputs, plus (for CUSTOM_AREA_TYPE = "shapefile") a user-supplied boundary shapefile.

7.2 Processing steps

Steps 1–5 mirror 01_biomass.R (see above) using the custom area_shp. Steps 6–12 are the same Excel-export / FG_spp_Ecopath / traits_ewe / sheet-order-finalization logic, applied to a single-area result. (Since this script skips MEDIAS, its species_density_regional table is MEDITS-only — no MEDITS+MEDIAS averaging step.)

7.3 Outputs

Same file set and workbook sheets as 01_biomass.R (see above), written to out_dir/<AREA_NAME>/ with most filenames suffixed _<AREA_NAME>, except the three contract files listed above which stay unsuffixed.

8 Script 2 — 02_fao_catches.R

Role: Pipeline Step 2 of 4. Requires Step 1 to have already run — it reads strata_area_by_area.csv (written by Step 1) to convert catch totals into a t/km² density comparable to Biomass. Running this before Step 1 fails with a clear “file not found” error pointing back to Step 1.

Config-driven catch-data loader: one DATASETS registry entry per source format, so GFCM_2025 and (a currently-commented-out) FAO_2026 share one processing path (format = "gfcm_regional"), while FAO_2020 (format = "legacy_excel") is handled separately as a genuinely different file layout. DATASET_VERSION <- "GFCM_2025" is the active, confirmed, working source; FAO_2020 is present but its file location is unconfirmed on disk.

8.1 Inputs

File Format Role
<git_dir>/data/raw/FI_Regional_2025.1.0/GFCM_Capture_Quantity.csv csv Raw GFCM catch quantities
.../CL_FI_SPECIES_GROUPS.csv csv Species code → name reference (also auto-downloaded from FAO’s open-data catalog if not found under pcloud_dir)
.../CL_FI_COUNTRY_GROUPS.csv csv Country code → name reference
.../CL_FI_WATERAREA_DIVISION.csv csv FAO/GFCM division code → name reference; catches filtered to divisions 37.1.1/37.1.2/37.1.3
<pcloud_dir>/data/FG_WMed.xlsx, sheet 4 xlsx Species → FG reference, same as Script 1
strata_area_by_area.csv csv From Step 1. Total study-area km² for the density conversion

8.1.1 Column reference — key input files

GFCM_Capture_Quantity.csv (raw catch, <git_dir>/data/raw/FI_Regional_2025.1.0/):

Column Used for
SPECIES.ALPHA_3_CODE Joined against CL_FI_SPECIES_GROUPS.csv
COUNTRY.UN_CODE Joined against CL_FI_COUNTRY_GROUPS.csv

8.2 Processing steps

  1. Config + dataset loading — resolve directories, load_gfcm_regional() merges capture/species/country/area reference CSVs, filters to Western Med divisions, MEASURE == "Q_tlw", PERIOD >= 1995. Builds country_ts/species_ts (Year × Country/Species × Division × Catch).
  2. Plots + audit CSVs — country- and species-level catch time series (country_catch_timeseries.png, species_catch_timeseries.png), unmatched_species_FG_assignment.csv, westmed_species_timeseries.csv.
  3. Species → FG matching pipeline (inline, Steps 0–4, same safety philosophy as Script 1’s taxonomy fallback — a query is only auto-resolved if it maps to exactly one distinct FG, otherwise flagged ambiguous and never guessed):
    • Step 1: direct match (catch-record species text literally equals an FG_name).
    • Step 2: common name → scientific name via rfishbase::common_names() (queried for the known species list, not searched from all of FishBase — avoids cross-matching an unrelated species that happens to share a common name).
    • Step 3: FAO genus-level fallback for NEI/aggregate categories (e.g. “Sardinellas nei” → genus Sardinella).
    • Step 4: word-containment fallback (full token containment only).
    • Manual overrides (e.g. “Turbot” → Scophthalmus maximus, correcting a wrong FishBase common-name entry).
    • A catch-total sanity check confirms no species record is double-counted across matching stages.
    • Output: species_fg_matched.csv (one row per species, status = resolved/unresolved, match_method).
  4. FG-level Year timeseries — joins species_ts (has Year, no FG) against species_fg_matched.csv’s Species→FG mapping, re-aggregates to Year × FG. Output: fg_catch_timeseries_<DATASET_VERSION>.csv (landings-only, not confirmed to include discards — read by 03_pbqb-traits.R’s optional FG_YIELD_SOURCE = "fg_catch_csv" path).
  5. Add Catches_Ecopath/Catches_Ecosim to the workbook — reads strata_area_by_area.csv, sums to a total study-area km², calls add_catches_to_ecopath_workbook(). Known, documented limitation: GFCM Divisions 37.1.1–37.1.3 cover GSA 1–11 plus GSA 12 (Northern Tunisia), which the survey’s FILTER_AREAS does not include — the catch density is therefore a slight overestimate relative to the Biomass area (GFCM’s Division-resolution reporting can’t isolate GSA 12’s specific contribution to subtract it out).

Not implemented — no fleet or gear disaggregation anywhere in this script. GFCM_Capture_Quantity.csv (the actual, confirmed source above) resolves only to Country × Species × Division × Year — this is GFCM’s STATLANT-derived aggregate capture-production product, not its Operational-Unit-level data. GFCM does collect catch/effort broken down by Operational Unit (fleet segment × ISSCFG gear code × GSA), via the DCRF’s Task 2 (Catch)/Task 4 (Fleet)/Task 5 (Effort) and the Regional Fleet Register, submitted by national correspondents — but that’s a genuinely different GFCM product from this file, and isn’t confirmed to have the same kind of open bulk-download GFCM_Capture_Quantity.csv does (it feeds GFCM’s own stock-assessment working groups rather than being published as an open dataset the way STATLANT capture-production is). Getting real fleet/gear resolution into this pipeline means sourcing that Operational-Unit data through GFCM’s DCRF/SAC channels, or — for the EU-flagged portion of the fleet specifically — STECF’s FDI (Fisheries Dependent Information) database, which is the same source already flagged elsewhere in this pipeline as the likely next step for a discard-inclusive catch figure. Neither is currently plugged in anywhere; 03_pbqb-traits.R’s Fmort_species/Fmort_FG (below) inherit this same limitation, since they’re computed downstream of this file.

Single fleet by default; multi-fleet via an optional fleet_structure input. add_catches_to_ecopath_workbook() (lib_survey_fg_density_ functions.R) now takes an optional fleet_structure argument: a table of FG_num, Fleet, prop_catch (the share, 0–1, of each FG’s total catch attributed to each fleet). Left at its default (NULL, and that is still this script’s own default), the workbook is exactly what it always was — one implicit fleet, Catches_Ecopath’s combined Catch_<year> columns, Catches_Ecosim’s meta-rows with no Fleet field, no Fleet_Structure sheet. Point FLEET_STRUCTURE_PATH (near the top of this script) at a CSV in that FG_num,Fleet,prop_catch shape to split catches by fleet instead — any FG not listed there still defaults to a single implicit "Fleet_1", so a partial table (only the FGs you actually have a fleet split for) is fine. Once any FG resolves to more than one fleet, every FG row in Catches_Ecopath switches to fleet-suffixed columns (Catch_<year>_<Fleet>), Catches_Ecosim gets one column-set per FG × Fleet, and the resolved fleet table itself is written out as a new Fleet_Structure sheet for traceability.

This only splits catch after the fact — it does not source fleet/gear data itself (see the “not implemented” note above: GFCM_Capture_ Quantity.csv still carries no fleet/gear column), so the fleet_structure CSV has to come from somewhere else. Three ways to get one: (1) GFCM Operational Units or STECF FDI, assembled by hand; (2) a hand-built allocation; or (3) point FLEET_STRUCTURE_PATH at file.path(out_dir, "fleet_structure_from_sau.csv") — written automatically by Script 2a (02a_fisheries_multisource.R), country × gear proportions (e.g. Spain - bottom trawl, France - purse seine) derived from SAU’s own real fleet-level catch shares per FG. This is an approximation — it borrows SAU’s gear mix and applies it to this script’s own FAO-GFCM totals, two different catch reconstructions for the same species/FG, not a real fleet-resolved FAO-GFCM figure, since FAO-GFCM has no gear data of its own to resolve directly. If the fleet split should come from the same reconstruction as the catch totals, use SAU’s own Catches_by_Fleet sheet (written directly by Script 2a, see below) instead of routing through here. 03_pbqb-traits.R’s Fmort_species/Fmort_FG remain unaffected either way — they’re computed from the same un-split fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv regardless of whether Catches_Ecopath/Catches_Ecosim end up split by fleet.

CATCHES_DATA_SOURCE: pick which source feeds Catches_Ecopath/ Catches_Ecosim — without duplicating Script 2a’s own logic here. This selector (NULL | "SAU" | "SAU_no_unreported" | "FishMIP", near FLEET_STRUCTURE_PATH) just selects which of Script 2a’s already-written contract files to read — the same “point this script at 2a’s own output” pattern 03_pbqb-traits.R’s FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE already uses — it does not re-implement any of Script 2a’s SAU/FishMIP extraction. Defaults to "SAU" (not NULL) — discards/unreported/IUU catch and a real fleet × gear × country split are treated as always-wanted, not an occasional opt-in. When "SAU"/"SAU_no_unreported", this script reads Script 2a’s fg_catch_timeseries_<SOURCE>.csv for the catch total and fleet_structure_from_sau.csv for the fleet split — both from the same SAU reconstruction, so this is fully self-consistent, unlike routing SAU’s fleet shares through FLEET_STRUCTURE_PATH onto a FAO-GFCM total (that combo still works and is unaffected, but CATCHES_DATA_SOURCE’s own SAU fleet structure takes priority over FLEET_STRUCTURE_PATH whenever both are set). Set to "FishMIP", only the total is replaced — FishMIP’s catch side has no gear dimension, so there’s no fleet split to auto-apply; FLEET_STRUCTURE_PATH still works normally on top of it if you want one anyway. Set to NULL yourself to restore the original FAO-GFCM-only, landings-only, single-fleet figure for a specific run. "FAO_GFCM" is not a valid value (it would just mean reading back this script’s own output) and errors clearly if set. This script does not auto-run Script 2a for you — unlike Script 2a’s own AUTO_RUN_MISSING_SOURCES, which auto-runs SAU’s download and, separately, auto-runs this script for FAO-GFCM — because Script 2 auto-sourcing Script 2a would risk recursing straight back into Script 2. A missing contract file is therefore a clear stop() naming which script to run first, never a silent fallback to NULL’s FAO-GFCM behavior.

Why the FAO-GFCM auto-run (Script 2a’s own STEP 7) is safe from this new default: since CATCHES_DATA_SOURCE now defaults to "SAU", and Script 2a’s SAU step (STEP 5) runs before its FAO-GFCM step (STEP 7) in the same run, fg_catch_timeseries_SAU.csv already exists by the time STEP 7 auto-sources this script — so STEP 7 explicitly sets a global CATCHES_DATA_SOURCE <- NULL right before sourcing it, overriding this script’s own default (via the same “reuse if the caller already set it” pattern DATASET_VERSION uses). Without that override, the auto-run would silently hand back SAU’s total mislabeled as the FAO-GFCM figure that DataSources_Catch/FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE = "FAO_GFCM" depend on being genuinely FAO-GFCM-only.

8.3 Outputs

  • CSVs: unmatched_species_FG_assignment.csv, westmed_species_timeseries.csv, species_fg_matched.csv, fg_catch_timeseries_<DATASET_VERSION>.csv.
  • Plots: country_catch_timeseries.png, species_catch_timeseries.png.
  • Workbook ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx, sheets written: Catches_Ecopath, Catches_Ecosim, plus Fleet_Structure if FLEET_STRUCTURE_PATH was set and resolved to more than one fleet.

9 Script 2a — 02a_fisheries_multisource.R (optional, runs alongside Script 2)

Role: Optional additional Step 2 — requires Step 1 to have already run (reads strata_area_by_area.csv and the FG reference, same contract as Script 2/Script 4). Adds three genuinely different, already-assembled catch/effort sources on top of Script 2’s own FAO-GFCM source, so all four can be compared side by side and one of them selected to drive Fishing mortality (F) and PB = M + F downstream in Script 4 — without changing Script 4’s own F/PB math at all, only the input file it reads.

9.1 Why four sources, not one

None of the four is a strict upgrade over the others — each has real gaps of its own, which is why every one gets written into the workbook (DataSources_Catch) rather than silently picked for the user:

  • SAU (Sea Around Us) — reconstructed catch (adds back unreported/discarded/IUU catch on top of official landings), resolved to country × gear × species × year. The only source here with a real gear dimension, so Catches_by_Fleet (below) is built from SAU alone. Yield = SAU’s own reconstructed tonnes (already landings + discards + unreported/IUU, by SAU’s own definition).
  • SAU w/o unreported — the same SAU extract, filtered to reporting_status == "Reported". This is SAU’s own reporting-status split, not a clean “landings + discards” figure — kept as its own labeled source rather than mislabeled.
  • FishMIP — a SAU-derived calibration product (not independent of SAU), resolved to country × sector × functional group × year, with reported/IUU/discards already split out. No species dimension and no gear dimension on the catch side (gear only exists on FishMIP’s separate effort file — see Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet below). Yield = reported + discards (IUU deliberately excluded, kept as an audit column tonnes_incl_iuu, per the “landings + discards” definition used throughout this script). FishMIP’s own functional groups are not this pipeline’s FG scheme — see FISHMIP_FG_CROSSWALK_PATH below.
  • FAO-GFCM — this pipeline’s own existing source (Script 2’s output), passed through unchanged. Landings only (no discards), Division-resolution (not GSA), no fleet/gear dimension — same caveats as Script 2 itself.

Area scope caveat (same pattern as the GSA-12/Division mismatch already flagged for Script 2): SAU and FishMIP resolve to country/EEZ, not GSA. Restricting either one to “the same area as the survey” means restricting to the countries whose EEZs overlap that GSA range — an approximation, not an exact area match. A country with only a small coastal sliver inside the modeled GSA range still contributes its entire national catch/effort. This is unavoidable at these sources’ native resolution and is reported explicitly at runtime (TARGET_COUNTRIES_NOTE), not silently absorbed.

9.2 Inputs

File Format Notes
strata_area_by_area.csv csv From Step 1
<pcloud_dir>/data/FG_WMed.xlsx, sheet 4 xlsx Species → FG reference, same as Scripts 1/2
<pcloud_dir>/data/fisheries/sau_raw_combined_west_med.csv csv SAU raw extract (from sau_west_med_full.R/sau_west_med_analysis.Rmd) — country × gear × species × year, reporting_status column
<pcloud_dir>/data/fisheries/effort_histsoc_*.parquet parquet FishMIP effort — saup, sector, gear, f_group, nom_active (kW·days)
<pcloud_dir>/data/fisheries/calibration_catch_histsoc_*.parquet parquet FishMIP catch — saup, sector, f_group, reported, iuu, discards
fg_catch_timeseries_<FAO_GFCM_DATASET_VERSION>.csv csv Script 2’s own output, read unchanged
FISHMIP_FG_CROSSWALK_PATH (optional) csv User-supplied fishmip_f_group → FG_num crosswalk — without it, FishMIP is informational-only

Each source degrades gracefully if its input is missing — a clear console message explains what to run first, the script does not hard-fail.

9.3 Processing steps

  1. Configuration — TARGET_ISO3 (the 6-country Western Med default: Spain, France, Italy, Tunisia, Algeria, Morocco), START_YEAR/END_YEAR (1994–2019, matching SAU’s public reconstruction cutoff), N_TOP_GEARS (top 8 gears + “Other gear” bucketing, same convention as the attached SAU/FishMIP analysis scripts, to avoid a combinatorial explosion of near-empty FG × Fleet × Year rows).
  2. DATA_SOURCE <- "SAU" | "SAU_no_unreported" | "FishMIP" | "FAO_GFCM" — which source drives Script 4’s Yield/Fmort/PB = M+F chain. Does not change this script’s own output — every available source is still written to DataSources_Catch/Catches_by_Fleet/Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet regardless. 2b. AUTO_RUN_MISSING_SOURCES <- TRUE (default) — when SAU’s raw extract or Script 2’s fg_catch_timeseries_<FAO_GFCM_DATASET_VERSION>.csv is missing, this script now runs the upstream step itself instead of just telling you to go run something else by hand: SAU auto-downloads sau_raw_combined_west_med.csv straight from SAU’s API (per-EEZ, via the new lib_sau_extraction.R — the same extraction logic as sau_west_med_analysis.Rmd’s own “Part 0” chunk, lifted out so it doesn’t require knitting that Rmd by hand every time), and FAO-GFCM auto-sources Script 2 (02_fao_catches.R) itself, first setting a global DATASET_VERSION to match this script’s own FAO_GFCM_DATASET_VERSION so the auto-run can’t silently regenerate the wrong dataset version’s file. Both attempts are wrapped so a failure (offline, upstream file genuinely missing, etc.) degrades to the same “not found, skipping” message as before — auto-run is a shortcut, not a new failure mode. FishMIP is deliberately excluded — its two parquet files are a manual download from the FishMIP Input Explorer website, not something any R script here can generate, so a missing FishMIP file always just skips with a message pointing at that download page. Set AUTO_RUN_MISSING_SOURCES <- FALSE to go back to the old “message + skip” behavior for every source.
  3. Species → FG matching — same match_species_to_fg() → match_nominate_subspecies_fg() → fallback_match_fg_by_taxonomy() cascade Script 1/Script 2 use, so a species is matched (or left unresolved) consistently across the whole pipeline.
  4. SAU — year/country filter, species→FG match, builds country-year totals (both “SAU total” and “SAU w/o unreported”), FG-year totals, species-year totals (for landings_by_species_gsa_year_<SOURCE>.csv), and two fleet-level (country × top-N-gears, “Other gear” bucket) sheets: Catches_by_Fleet — wide, one row per FG, one column per Fleet (e.g. Spain - bottom trawl, France - longline, …), value = that FG × Fleet’s catch (t) averaged over YEAR_ECOPATH (a config constant in this script that must match Step 1’s own YEAR_ECOPATH) — same single-snapshot convention add_catches_to_ecopath_workbook() already uses for Catches_Ecopath, but built directly from SAU’s real per-fleet annual values rather than a static proportion split, so each fleet’s actual year-to-year share is reflected; and Catches_by_Fleet_AllYears — the same data in long form (one row per FG × Fleet × Year), kept for anyone who needs the full annual detail the wide sheet collapses out of. Both already include discards (+ unreported + IUU) — see “Why four sources, not one” above; there is no separate landings-only vs. discards-only split at fleet resolution, only SAU’s already-combined total.
  5. FishMIP (if the arrow package and both parquet files are available) — builds Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet (the only genuine effort data in this whole pipeline — SAU has no effort variable at all) and country-level catch totals; FG-level totals only if FISHMIP_FG_CROSSWALK_PATH is supplied.
  6. FAO-GFCM — reads Script 2’s own fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv unchanged.
  7. DataSources_Catch — every available source’s country-level and FG-level Yield, stacked with a source column, for side-by-side comparison regardless of which one is selected as DATA_SOURCE.
  8. Writes Catches_by_Fleet, Catches_by_Fleet_AllYears, Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet, DataSources_Catch into the shared workbook via upsert_workbook_sheets() (idempotent — re-running replaces these sheets, doesn’t duplicate them).
  9. Writes fg_catch_timeseries_<DATA_SOURCE>.csv (and, for SAU/SAU_no_unreported only, landings_by_species_gsa_year_<DATA_SOURCE>.csv) — the contract files Script 4’s FG_YIELD_SOURCE/YIELD_SOURCE mechanism reads. Wiring into Script 4: Script 4 has its own FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE selector (see Script 4 below) which, when set to match this script’s DATA_SOURCE, automatically points FG_CATCH_CSV_PATH/LANDINGS_CSV_PATH at these files — no manual path editing needed in Script 4 as long as the two DATA_SOURCE/ FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE values match.

9.4 Outputs

  • CSVs: fg_catch_timeseries_<DATA_SOURCE>.csv, and (SAU/SAU_no_unreported only) landings_by_species_gsa_year_<DATA_SOURCE>.csv.
  • Workbook ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx, sheets written: Catches_by_Fleet (wide — FG rows × Fleet columns, averaged over YEAR_ECOPATH) and Catches_by_Fleet_AllYears (the same data, long, one row per FG × Fleet × Year) — both SAU only, the only source with real gear × country × species resolution; Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet (FishMIP only — genuinely different fleet resolution than Catches_by_Fleet, never to be joined directly to it), DataSources_Catch (every available source, country- and FG-level, side by side).

10 Script 3 — 03b_ecobase.R

Role: Pipeline Step 3 of 4. Independent of Steps 1/2 — can run anytime before Step 4. Optional input to Step 4 for literature-based PB/QB gap-filling and comparison against the empirical formula estimates.

Queries EcoBase (ecobase.ecopath.org), an open-access repository of published Ecopath models, via its SOAP/XML web service — the official EcoBase R example ported from RCurl/XML to httr/xml2. The script author notes this was not tested against the live service from within the authoring sandbox (no network access to sirs.agrocampus-ouest.fr) — the diagnostics throughout are there specifically to surface an endpoint change, not just fail silently.

Fixed: merge(mediterranean_models, all_status, ...) — “x has duplicated column name [comments_objectives]”. Root cause traced to xml_node_to_dt_row() (formerly an inline anonymous function, at two call sites — the model-list parse and, in fetch_model_inputs(), the per-model group parse): when a single <model> (or <group>) XML node repeats a child tag — confirmed EcoBase’s own XML does this, e.g. a duplicated <comments_objectives> — setNames(as.list(xml_text(fields)), xml_name(fields)) produced a list with two entries sharing one name, and as.data.table() on that silently creates a data.table with two same-named columns. data.table allows that at creation time; it only rejects it the moment something needs unique names — which is why the error surfaced three functions and one rbindlist() away from where the duplicate actually originated, at the merge() call. Fix: both parse sites now go through one shared xml_node_to_dt_row() that detects a repeated tag name and collapses its values into ONE column, joined with "; " — nothing is dropped, there’s just no longer two columns fighting over one name.

10.1 Inputs (network)

Endpoint Purpose
sirs.agrocampus-ouest.fr/EcoBase/php/webser/soap-client_3.php Full model list
sirs.agrocampus-ouest.fr/EcoBase/php/webser/soap-client.php?no_model=<id> Per-model input values (Biomass/PB/QB per group)

pcloud_dir/git_dir are resolved for consistency with the other scripts but not actually used — this script is self-contained.

10.2 Processing steps

  1. Fetch the full EcoBase model list; save ecobase_model_list_full.csv.
  2. Filter geographically to the Mediterranean, using each model’s geographic_extent bounding box (not text/keyword matching on country/ecosystem names — more robust). Save ecobase_mediterranean_models.csv.
  3. Filter further to dissemination_allow == "true" — EcoBase’s own flag for which models actually have downloadable input data (per the site, only ~233 of ~500 listed models do; the rest are metadata/reference-only). KNOWN_RELEVANT_MODEL_IDS is the resulting query list.
  4. Fetch per-model group-level Biomass/PB/QB for each disseminable model (fetch_model_inputs()), explicitly detecting the “data not available despite the flag” case (confirmed against a real response for model 418). -9999 (Ecopath’s own sentinel for “not provided”) is converted to NA.
  5. Save a full status audit (ecobase_mediterranean_models_status.csv) and the failure-only subset (ecobase_models_without_data.csv).
  6. Compile the final summary table: FG_name, PB, QB, EwE_model, year, authors (model metadata columns matched by best-guess candidate names against model_list, since EcoBase’s exact field names aren’t confirmed).

10.3 Outputs

  • ecobase_model_list_full.csv, ecobase_mediterranean_models.csv, ecobase_mediterranean_models_status.csv, ecobase_models_without_data.csv, ecobase_literature_pb_qb_raw.csv, ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv (the file 03_pbqb-traits.R reads).
  • No workbook writes from this script directly — 03_pbqb-traits.R is what merges this CSV into the workbook’s Ecobase sheet.

10.3.1 Column reference — ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv

Column Meaning
FG_name EcoBase group name (matched to your own FG_name by text in 03_pbqb-traits.R — group naming won’t line up automatically across different published models)
PB Literature Production/Biomass (-9999, EcoBase’s own “not provided” sentinel, converted to NA)
QB Literature Consumption/Biomass (same -9999→NA handling)
EwE_model Source model name
year Source model’s reference year
authors Source model’s authors

11 Script 4 — 03_pbqb-traits.R

Role: Pipeline Step 4 of 4 — run last. Requires Step 1’s species_density_regional_combined.csv. Optionally uses Step 2’s fg_catch_timeseries_<DATASET_VERSION>.csv (FG_YIELD_SOURCE toggle) and Step 3’s ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv — both degrade gracefully with a clear message if missing, they don’t hard-fail this script.

Estimates Production/Biomass (PB) and Consumption/Biomass (QB) for every EwE Functional Group, following the taxon-specific methods in “Quick guide on how to calculate P/B and Q/B for EwE models” (Vilas, Coll, Piroddi, Steenbeek). Growth parameters, length-weight a/b, maturity, body weight, longevity, trophic level, temperature, depth and aspect ratio are all fetched automatically from FishBase + SeaLifeBase (rfishbase ≥ 4.0.0 required — the script hard-stops with install instructions if an old, broken pre-4.0 CRAN build, or a duckdbfs install missing duckdb_config, is detected).

11.1 Inputs

File Format Role
species_density_regional_combined.csv csv From Step 1. Reshaped via lib_build_species_df_from_survey.R::build_species_df_from_survey() into species_df (Species, FG, Biomass density, Yield=NA)
fg_catch_timeseries_GFCM_2025.csv (or fg_catch_timeseries_<FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE>.csv) csv (optional) From Step 2, or Step 2a if FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE is set. FG-level catch, used to compute a per-FG fishing-mortality rate Fmort_FG (FG_YIELD_SOURCE = "fg_catch_csv")
strata_area_by_area.csv csv From Step 1. Study area, needed to convert FG catch totals into a density comparable to Biomass
ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv csv (optional) From Step 3. Literature PB/QB for gap-filling/comparison
<pcloud_dir>/data/FG_WMed.xlsx, sheet fg_wmed_95 xlsx FG reference (loaded by explicit sheet name, since the workbook also has an unrelated fg_ebro delta_21 sheet)
landings_by_species_gsa_year.csv (or landings_by_species_gsa_year_<FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE>.csv) csv (optional; placeholder path when FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE is NULL) Species-level landings, for a species-level Fmort path (YIELD_SOURCE = "landings_csv") — real data when Step 2a’s FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE is set to "SAU"/"SAU_no_unreported"
FishBase / SeaLifeBase (rfishbase) web API Growth, length-weight, maturity, ecology/trophic level, swimming/aspect-ratio traits
WoRMS (worrms, via lib_worms_taxonomy_lookup.R) web API Taxonomic classification (Class → dispatch group: fish/mammal/seabird/phytoplankton/invertebrate)
FishLife R package local DB (FishBase_and_RAM$ParentChild_gz) Phylogenetically-imputed Loo/K/M/Winf, walking up the taxonomic tree when a direct match is missing
TropFishR R package local M_empirical() — Hoenig (1983), Then et al. (2015), Alverson & Carney (1975) natural-mortality estimators

11.1.1 Column reference — key input files

species_density_regional_combined.csv (from Step 1 — required columns, enforced by build_species_df_from_survey()):

FG_num, FG_name Functional Group id/name
ScientificName Species scientific name

Column Meaning

FG_num, FG_name Functional Group id/name
Catch_t Total landed catch, tonnes (region-wide, landings-only)

strata_area_by_area.csv (from Step 1):

Column Meaning

Stratum Depth-stratum number

landings_by_species_gsa_year.csv (placeholder path — not yet a real data source; schema below is what attach_yield_from_landings() expects if this is ever wired up):

Column Meaning
ScientificName Species scientific name
Year Landings year
AreaID GSA number
catch_t Landed catch, tonnes

Figure not yet available. Run 03_pbqb-traits.R against the real WestMed EwE pCloud drive data, then copy <out_dir>/plots/fish_PB_methods_comparison.png into a figures/ folder next to this .Rmd (as fish_PB_methods_comparison.png) and re-knit.

11.2 Processing steps

  1. Setup — package loading, rfishbase/duckdbfs version guard, GUI or console progress tracker across 13 named pipeline stages.

  2. Load species_df (SPECIES_DF_SOURCE = "survey", the real path) via lib_build_species_df_from_survey.R.

  3. Fishing mortality attachment — two independent attachment points, both driven by a single FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE selector (set near out_dir/pcloud_dir/git_dir at the top of this script): NULL | "SAU" | "SAU_no_unreported" | "FishMIP" | "FAO_GFCM", matching Script 2a’s own DATA_SOURCE values one-to-one. When set, it automatically points LANDINGS_CSV_PATH/FG_CATCH_CSV_PATH at the _<FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE>.csv files Script 2a writes, and flips YIELD_SOURCE/FG_YIELD_SOURCE on to match — no manual path editing needed as long as this matches Script 2a’s DATA_SOURCE for the run that produced those files. Defaults to "SAU" (not NULL) — species-level resolution and discards/unreported/IUU catch are treated as always-wanted for this chain, matching 02_fao_catches.R’s own CATCHES_DATA_SOURCE default (also "SAU", same reasoning). Set to NULL yourself to restore the original hardcoded placeholder behavior from before Script 2a existed. Every source’s own catch/effort still appears in the workbook’s DataSources_Catch/Catches_by_Fleet/Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet sheets (written by Script 2a) regardless of this setting — only this script’s own Fmort/PB = M+F chain follows whichever source is selected here.

      1. species-level, via attach_yield_from_landings() — active only for FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE %in% c("SAU", "SAU_no_unreported") (the only two sources with species-level resolution); inactive (YIELD_SOURCE = "none") for NULL/"FishMIP"/"FAO_GFCM".
      1. FG-level, via fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv — active (FG_YIELD_SOURCE = "fg_catch_csv") for any non-NULL FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE — computed as Yield_FG / Biomass_FG per FG, applied to fish PB inside calc_fish() as a fallback when species-level Yield is absent (species-level always takes priority when both exist). Known, unresolved caveat (same root cause as Catches_Ecopath/ Catches_Ecosim below): Yield_FG’s denominator (area_total_km2, summed from strata_area_by_area.csv) is the survey’s GSA-filtered area, while its numerator (fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv’s Catch_t) is filtered by GFCM Division, which for the Western Med example covers GSA 1–11 plus GSA 12 — so Yield_FG, and therefore Fmort_FG and the fish PB it feeds, is a slight overestimate wherever this path is enabled. Not fixable from this side (GFCM’s catch data can’t be split below Division resolution); see the same caveat under Script 2 — 02_fao_catches.R for the full explanation. Also not implemented — no fleet or gear disaggregation: both Fmort_species (path A, inactive) and Fmort_FG (path B) are a single Yield/Biomass ratio lumped across the whole region and every gear/fleet that landed that species/FG — neither the landings_by_species_gsa_year.csv placeholder schema nor fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv carries a fleet or gear column, so there’s nothing here that could produce a fleet- or gear-specific F even once a real landings file is plugged in. See the matching note under Script 2 — 02_fao_catches.R for what a real fix would require (GFCM’s Operational-Unit data or STECF’s FDI database). Fmort itself stays single-fleet even where Catches_ sheets are split: unlike Catches_Ecopath/Catches_Ecosim (which can now be split by fleet via fleet_structure — see the “Single fleet by default; multi-fleet via an optional fleet_structure input” note under Script 2 — 02_fao_catches.R), this script never sees fleet_structure and computes Fmort_species/Fmort_FG from the same un-split fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv regardless. A genuinely fleet-specific F would need Yield split by fleet before this ratio, not just the Catches_ sheets split after the fact.

  4. Taxonomic classification — worms_taxonomy_lookup() for every species in species_df, dispatch-classified into fish / mammal / seabird / phytoplankton / invertebrate (default) by WoRMS Class, with a synonym→accepted-name resolution fallback for species WoRMS returns no Class for.

  5. Trait fetching (Step 2a–2f) — select_best_rows() picks the best population-specific study per species from FishBase/SeaLifeBase multi-record tables, tiered: Western-Med locality keywords → broader Mediterranean keywords → most recent year → first available. extract_provenance() captures Locality/Year/Reference citation for each selected row (used later for the References sheet/CSV).

  6. Gap-filling — fill_by_taxonomic_proximity(): local within-dataset donors first (Genus→Family→Order→Class, restricted to directly-computed values), then, for methods in EXTERNAL_FORMULA_REGISTRY (fish/mammal/ general Gascuel formulas plus raw-trait identity mappings), an external FishBase/SeaLifeBase donor lookup — querying other species sharing the same taxonomic rank, not limited to species already in species_df.

  7. Taxon-specific PB/QB calculation (dispatch by group) — the diagram below shows the actual dispatch and method-cascade logic:

Code 
%%| fig-cap: "PB/QB taxon dispatch and gap-fill cascade - which calc_*() function runs per taxonomic Class, and how results are aggregated to FG-level and gap-filled from EcoBase."
   %%{init: {'flowchart': {'padding': 20, 'nodeSpacing': 60, 'rankSpacing': 90}, 'themeVariables': {'fontSize': '18px'}}}%%
   flowchart LR
       T["worms_taxonomy_lookup()&lt;br/&gt;WoRMS Class"] --> F["calc_fish()&lt;br/&gt;PB: 6 M methods + Fmort&lt;br/&gt;QB: 4 methods"]
       T --> M["calc_mammal()&lt;br/&gt;PB: Siler surv. to Gascuel&lt;br/&gt;QB: Innes&amp;Trites to Q/P=3"]
       T --> S["calc_seabird()&lt;br/&gt;PB: Gascuel only&lt;br/&gt;QB: Nilsson-Nilsson (no Q/P=3)"]
       T --> I["calc_invertebrate() (default)&lt;br/&gt;PB: Tumbiolo/Brey to Gascuel&lt;br/&gt;QB: Q/P=3 only"]
       F --> D["fill_by_taxonomic_proximity()&lt;br/&gt;local then external donors"]
       M --> D
       S --> D
       I --> D
       D --> AGG["FG-level aggregation&lt;br/&gt;biomass-weighted mean"]
       AGG --> OUT["Ecobase gap-fill -&gt; Ecopath_PBQB&lt;br/&gt;(PB_QB/PB_QB_spp/Ecobase CSVs)"]
   
       classDef gray fill:#F1EFE8,stroke:#5F5E5A,color:#2C2C2A
       classDef purple fill:#EEEDFE,stroke:#534AB7,color:#26215C
       classDef teal fill:#E1F5EE,stroke:#0F6E56,color:#04342C
       classDef coral fill:#FAECE7,stroke:#993C1D,color:#4A1B0C
       class T gray
       class F,M,S,I purple
       class D teal
       class AGG,OUT coral

:::

Phytoplankton is flagged by worms_taxonomy_lookup() but not computed (needs separate production-sampling data, out of scope for this pipeline) — not shown above.

  • calc_fish() — PB from 6 independent M methods (Pauly 1980, FishLife, Gascuel 2008, Hoenig 1983, Then et al. 2015, Alverson & Carney 1975) + Fmort; QB from 4 methods (Palomares & Pauly 1998 Eq.27/Eq.26, Christensen & Pauly 1992, Q/P=3 fallback). Chosen value via priority order or mean (PB_QB_SELECTION_MODE).
  • calc_mammal() — PB via Siler-survivorship (Barlow & Boveng 1991), fallback Gascuel (2008); QB via Innes & Trites (1997), fallback Q/P=3.
  • calc_seabird() — PB via Gascuel (2008) only; QB via Nilsson & Nilsson (1976), no Q/P=3 fallback (that heuristic is fish/ectotherm-derived and would badly underestimate endotherm QB).
  • calc_invertebrate() — PB via Tumbiolo & Downing (1994), Brey (1999), fallback Gascuel (2008); QB via Q/P=3 only (Brey’s 2012 ANN model flagged as non-automatable — top-10 highest-biomass invertebrates exported instead for manual cross-check).
  • Phytoplankton — flagged, not computed (needs separate production- sampling data, out of scope for this pipeline).
  1. FG-level aggregation — biomass-weighted mean PB/QB per FG, with coverage/count audit columns (n_species_with_PB, biomass_coverage_PB, etc.).
  2. EcoBase literature merge (if ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv exists) — averages multiple matching literature models per FG_name, flags >2×/<0.5× divergence from the empirical estimate, gap-fills only where the empirical value is NA. Writes sheet Ecobase (one row per FG, averaged PB/QB across every matching model, plus a References column listing each contributing model) and Ecobase_by_Model (one row per FG × individual published model — FG_num, FG_name, EwE_model, year, authors, PB, QB — the un-averaged detail behind Ecobase’s own per-FG mean, since a single FG typically has several different published models backing it and the averaged sheet alone loses which model said what).
  3. FG-level F top-up (if FG_YIELD_SOURCE = "fg_catch_csv") — applied only to FGs with no species-level F already baked in (mainly mammal/ seabird/invertebrate FGs), to avoid double-counting against fish’s own species-level Fmort.
  4. Workbook write — add_pbqb_to_ecopath_workbook() writes sheet PB_QB (one row per FG — the biomass-weighted rollup), and, since results (the species-level table) is passed in as its species_pb_qb argument, also writes sheet PB_QB_spp (one row per species, mirroring FG_spp_Ecopath‘s shape — every species that went into computing PB_FG/QB_FG, with three proportion columns: prop_biomass_FG — share of the FG’s total biomass, regardless of whether that species got a PB/QB; prop_biomass_PB/prop_biomass_QB — that species’ actual weight inside PB_FG‘s/QB_FG’s own biomass-weighted mean, NA if that species’ own PB/QB didn’t compute. This is the sheet to check which species and how much of each FG’s biomass a given PB_FG/QB_FG number was built from — without it, that detail only existed outside the workbook, in species_pb_qb_by_taxon_group.csv). If 01_survey_density_*.R already wrote a PB_QB_spp scaffold into this workbook (Species/FG_num/ Biomass/prop_biomass_FG only, no real PB/QB yet), this call replaces it wholesale with the full sheet — upsert_workbook_sheets() always fully overwrites a same-named sheet, so no separate merge step is needed. A separate upsert_workbook_sheets() call writes sheet References (later renamed PB_QB_References_ by finalize_workbook_sheet_order(), run from Script 1).
  5. Diagnostic plots — per-method PB/QB comparison plots for fish, plus paginated species-level and FG-level PB/QB comparison plots across every dispatch group (auto-paginated when a plot would exceed ggsave’s 50-inch height limit).
  6. Ecopath-ready CSV export — ecopath_ready_PB_QB.csv, formatted to match a real Ecopath Basic Input CSV’s exact column layout and European comma-decimal number formatting (verified against a real exported file, westernmed90s-Basic_input.csv).

11.3 Outputs

  • CSVs (2026-09-17: all under output/pbqb-traits/, not the top-level output/): invertebrates_for_brey_manual_check.csv, species_pb_qb_by_taxon_group.csv, fg_pb_qb_weighted.csv, phytoplankton_needs_separate_method.csv, fg_pb_qb_weighted_with_ecobase.csv, fg_pb_qb_weighted_with_F.csv, species_parameter_references.csv (species-level parameter provenance — not the same table as the workbook’s FG_References sheet, see below), ecopath_ready_PB_QB.csv, native PB_QB.csv/PB_QB_spp.csv.
  • Plots: fish_PB_methods_comparison.png, fish_QB_methods_comparison.png, species_PB_QB_comparison[_pageN].png, FG_PB_QB_comparison[_pageN].png.
  • Workbook ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx, final sheets written: Ecopath_PBQB, Ecopath_traits. (PB_QB/PB_QB_spp/Ecobase are native/intermediate CSVs only now, not workbook sheets — see “Excel workbook functions” in the shared-library section below.)

12 Shared library — lib_survey_fg_density_functions.R

Sourced by all four numbered scripts; has no top-level driver code of its own — every symbol below is a function definition only.

12.1 FG-matching functions

Every observed species runs through this cascade, in order, until it resolves to a Functional Group or falls through to manual review — the diagram shows the actual fallback order implemented in the code below (same style as medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd’s @sec-fg-matching diagram; each stage only attempts species the previous one left unmatched).

Code 
%%{init: {'flowchart': {'padding': 20, 'nodeSpacing': 60, 'rankSpacing': 90}, 'themeVariables': {'fontSize': '18px'}}}%%
flowchart LR
    A1["match_species_to_fg()&lt;br/&gt;direct ScientificName match"] --> A2["match_nominate_subspecies_fg()&lt;br/&gt;strip repeated trinomial word, retry"]
    A2 --> A3["apply_seed_fg_rules()&lt;br/&gt;manual rules, zero-reference FGs"]
    A3 --> A4["fallback_match_fg_by_taxonomy()&lt;br/&gt;Genus to Phylum, exactly-one-FG rule"]
    A4 --> A5["summarize_unresolved_species()&lt;br/&gt;manual review"]

    classDef purple fill:#EEEDFE,stroke:#534AB7,color:#26215C
    classDef gray fill:#F1EFE8,stroke:#5F5E5A,color:#2C2C2A
    class A1,A2,A3,A4 purple
    class A5 gray

Function Purpose Returns
validate_survey_data(dataframe1, dataframe2, strata = TRUE) Fail-fast schema/uniqueness check on the two standardized input tables (required columns present, SampleID unique per survey) invisible(TRUE)
prepare_fg_lookup(dataframe2) Deduplicates species→FG reference; keeps genuine stanza splits (a species exclusively mapped to ≥2 FGs), excludes/flags ambiguous multi-FG species deduped data.table(ScientificName, FG_num, FG_name)
match_species_to_fg(dataframe1, fg_lookup_safe) Direct left-merge by ScientificName merged dt
match_nominate_subspecies_fg(dt, fg_lookup_safe) Strips a repeated trinomial word (e.g. “Diplodus sargus sargus” → “Diplodus sargus”) and retries the match dt with newly resolved rows
fetch_taxonomy(species_names, taxonomy_source = "worms") Dispatches to fetch_taxonomy_worms(), fetch_taxonomy_fishbase(), or both data.table(ScientificName, Genus, Family, Order, Class, Phylum)
fallback_match_fg_by_taxonomy(dt, fg_lookup_safe, taxonomy_source = "worms", rank_levels = c("Genus","Family","Order","Class","Phylum")) Data-driven rank fallback — a rank value is used only if it maps to exactly one FG among reference species dt, with attr(dt,"fetched_taxonomy") and attr(dt,"still_unresolved_taxonomy") attached
apply_seed_fg_rules(dt, dataframe2, seed_rules, species_exceptions = NULL) Explicit small rule table for FGs with zero reference species (nothing for the automatic fallback to learn from); species-level exceptions applied first dt
summarize_unresolved_species(dt, taxonomy = NULL) Mean biomass + appearance count per still-unmatched species, sorted by appearance count summary data.table

12.2 Area / stratum / density functions

Function Purpose Returns
match_samples_to_area(dt, area_shp, area_id_col) Spatial join (sf::st_join) of sample points to polygons; no-op if AreaID already fully populated dt
assign_depth_stratum(dt, strata_def) Bins Depth into strata via findInterval(); out-of-range depths → Stratum = NA dt
filter_samples_by_area(dt, area_filter, filter_type = c("shapefile","bbox"), points_crs = 4326) Custom sub-region filter, independent of GSA/AreaID dt filtered to in-area SampleIDs
compute_sample_densities(dt) Density := Biomass / Effort dt
apply_catchability_correction(dt, catchability_table, default_q = 1, species_taxonomy = NULL, exempt_fg_names = NULL, exempt_species = NULL) 5-tier resolution: direct species → genus “spp” entries → explicit broad-rank entries → taxonomic-proximity fallback → default_q; exemptions override last. Divides Density/Biomass by resolved q dt
remove_sample_outliers(dt, threshold = 70, min_samples = 5, drop_outliers = TRUE, log_scale = TRUE) Per-species/per-area robust z-score (median/MAD) on log1p(Density); sets flagged Density/Biomass to NA if drop_outliers = TRUE dt, with attr(dt,"flagged_outliers")
compute_fg_densities_by_stratum(dt, strata = TRUE) Sums species density within FG per sample, then across samples by area/year/[stratum]/FG per_group_fg (density_sum)
compute_strata_area_by_area(area_ids, area_shp, area_id_col, strata_def, cache_path = NULL, resolution = 1) Downloads NOAA bathymetry per area, bins cell area into depth strata (latitude-corrected); optional CSV cache strata_area_by_area table
weight_by_strata(per_group_fg, n_samples_by_stratum, strata_area_by_area) Core strata-weighted estimator: D_area = Σ_h (density_sum_h/n_samples_h) · area_prop_h fg_index, with attr(fg_index,"per_stratum")
simple_area_density(per_group_fg, n_samples_by_area) Unstratified path (strata = FALSE) data.table
weight_by_area(fg_index_stratified) Region-wide weighting from absolute stratum areas (per_stratum attribute) — requires the stratified path upstream fg_index_regional
compute_species_densities_by_stratum(dt, strata = TRUE) / weight_species_by_area(per_group_sp, n_samples_by_stratum, strata_area_by_area) Species-level analogs of the two functions above, feeding the FG_spp sheets data.table(Year, FG_num, FG_name, ScientificName, mean_density)
compute_cv_log_by_fg(value_dt, value_col, min_replicates = 2) cv_log = sd(log1p(x))/mean(log1p(x)) per FG/Year, averaged across years — feeds the Ecosim “Weight” row data.table(FG_num, FG_name, cv_log, n_years)

12.4 Excel workbook functions (2026-09-17 revision)

As of the 2026-09-17 CSV-native refactor, ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx carries only the 10 final target sheets — every native/intermediate table each block used to also keep as a workbook sheet is now CSV-only (see “Per-block output CSV subfolders” and “Final target sheet order” below). The table below reflects the current function signatures, not the older ones this document used to describe.

Function Purpose
upsert_workbook_sheets(sheets, out_path) Adds/replaces the named sheets in out_path, leaves every other sheet untouched. Creates the workbook if it doesn’t exist yet. Safe to call repeatedly, from any script, in any order.
write_native_sheet_csv(dt, name, out_dir) / write_native_sheets_csv(sheets, out_dir) / read_native_sheet_csv(name, out_dir) Generic CSV read/write for a block’s own native/intermediate tables — out_dir is always passed explicitly by the caller (the block’s own subfolder), never assumed
read_existing_ts_years(out_path, sheet_name, csv_dir = NULL) Reads year values from column 1 of an existing native CSV sheet (e.g. Ecosim), so Catches_Ecosim’s years stay aligned with it. csv_dir defaults to dirname(out_path) for back-compat; the numbered scripts pass the correct block’s subfolder explicitly
read_full_fg_reference(out_path, csv_dir = NULL) Reads FG_lookup.csv (a biomass-block output) — the full FG_num/FG_name reference, used to expand PB_QB/FG_References/etc. to every model FG, not just the ones with data this run
export_ecopath_ecosim_excel(fg_index_regional, species_density_regional, n_samples_by_area_year, dataframe2, year_ecopath, ts_years, out_path, species_taxonomy = NULL, fg_cv_log = NULL, normalize_ts = TRUE, csv_out_dir = NULL) Writes the final Ecopath_B sheet directly to the workbook, plus native/intermediate CSVs (FG_spp_Ecopath, FG_lookup, Ecosim, FG_spp_Ecosim, a PB_QB_spp scaffold) into csv_out_dir (01_biomass.R’s own output/biomass/ subfolder)
add_catches_to_ecopath_workbook(fg_catch, fg_lookup, out_path, year_ecopath, area_km2, ts_years = NULL, fleet_structure = NULL, csv_out_dir = NULL, biomass_csv_dir = NULL) Converts raw catch tonnes to t/km² density; writes native CSVs Catches_Ecopath/Catches_Ecosim/Fleet_Structure into csv_out_dir (02_fisheries.R’s output/fisheries/). Reads Ecosim.csv from biomass_csv_dir when ts_years isn’t passed explicitly
add_pbqb_to_ecopath_workbook(fg_weighted, out_path, species_pb_qb = NULL, csv_out_dir = NULL, biomass_csv_dir = NULL) Writes native CSVs PB_QB/PB_QB_spp into csv_out_dir (03_pbqb-traits.R’s output/pbqb-traits/). Reads FG_lookup.csv from biomass_csv_dir to expand to every model FG
finalize_ecopath_ecosim_summary_sheets(out_path, year_ecopath, biomass_csv_dir = NULL, fisheries_csv_dir = NULL) Builds the final Ecosim_ts sheet by combining native CSVs from two blocks — Ecosim (biomass) plus Catches_Discards_FG_ts/Catches_Ecosim/Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet (fisheries) — hence the two separate directory arguments
build_fg_references_sheet(out_path, biomass_csv_dir = NULL, fisheries_csv_dir = NULL, pbqb_csv_dir = NULL, diet_csv_dir = NULL) Builds the final FG_References sheet — one row per FG, consolidating which data source fed each block (see “The FG_References sheet” below)
trim_workbook_to_final_sheets(out_path) Run last, by every numbered script. Reduces the workbook to exactly the 10 final target sheets (see below), dropping any native/intermediate sheet a much older run of this workbook might still be carrying

The diagram below shows which script/function writes which sheet into the one shared workbook, and the final trim that runs after each script.
