12.5 Per-block output CSV subfolders (2026-09-17)

Every native/intermediate CSV a block writes now lands in that block’s own subfolder under the shared out_dir, instead of a single flat directory shared by all four scripts:

  • 01_biomass.R → output/biomass/
  • 02_fisheries.R → output/fisheries/
  • 03_pbqb-traits.R → output/pbqb-traits/
  • 04_diets.R → output/diet/

ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx itself stays at the top-level output/ — all four scripts read and write it, so it has no single owning block. Each script defines its own csv_out_dir near the top and passes it (or a *_csv_dir pointing at a different block’s subfolder, for the handful of cross-block reads — species density, strata area, FG_lookup.csv, Ecosim.csv, biomass_proportion_by_species_fg.csv, catch time series) into the library functions above. Every one of those new directory arguments defaults to dirname(out_path) (the workbook’s own top-level folder) when omitted, so nothing breaks if some other caller doesn’t pass them.

12.6 Final target sheet order (10 sheets)

target_order, used by trim_workbook_to_final_sheets() and run after every numbered script:

  1. info — run metadata (areas, countries, YEAR_ECOPATH, TS_YEARS, …)
  2. FG_spp — FG × species × taxonomy list, written by 01_biomass.R
  3. Ecopath_B — FG-level biomass (t/km²), YEAR_ECOPATH-averaged
  4. Ecopath_L — FG × Fleet landings, written by 02_fisheries.R
  5. Ecopath_Di — FG × Fleet discards, written by 02_fisheries.R
  6. Ecopath_PBQB — FG-level PB/QB, written by 03_pbqb-traits.R
  7. Ecopath_traits — FG_name/species-level traits, written by 01_biomass.R
  8. Ecopath_diet — predator FG × prey FG diet matrix, written by 04_diets.R
  9. Ecosim_ts — combined B/L/Di/Effort time series (see finalize_ecopath_ecosim_summary_sheets())
  10. FG_References — one row per FG, the data-source/citation summary (see below) — added 2026-09-17

Every native/intermediate table (Ecopath, Catches_Ecopath, PB_QB, Ecosim, Catches_Ecosim, Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet, FG_spp_Ecopath, FG_spp_Ecosim, FG_lookup, traits_ewe, fg_traits_weighted, PB_QB_spp, References [species-parameter-level, 03_pbqb-traits.R’s own audit CSV — a different table from the FG_References workbook sheet above, despite the similar name], Ecobase, Catches_Discards_FG_ts, diet_references_by_fg, …) lives only as CSV, in its own block’s subfolder, never as a workbook sheet — trim_workbook_to_final_sheets() drops anything else it finds regardless.

12.7 The FG_References sheet

Added 2026-09-17, built by build_fg_references_sheet() (lib_survey_fg_density_functions.R), run by 04_diets.R after every other script has had a chance to run. One row per FG, with columns:

Column What it summarizes Read from
FG_num, FG_name The full FG reference FG_lookup.csv (output/biomass/)
ref_B Which biomass source (MEDITS/MEDIAS survey vs. stock assessment) fed Ecopath_B/Ecosim_ts’s B block for this FG biomass_source in survey_fg_annual_index_regional_combined.csv (output/biomass/)
ref_fisheries Which catch/discard source (GFCM/FDI/SAU-derived vs. stock assessment) fed Ecopath_L/Ecopath_Di/Ecosim_ts’s catch time series for this FG catch_source in Catches_Discards_FG_ts.csv (output/fisheries/)
ref_pbqb_traits Whether PB_FG/QB_FG came from the empirical species-level calculation or an EcoBase literature gap-fill PB_source/QB_source in PB_QB.csv (output/pbqb-traits/), falling back to a generic “empirical” note when EcoBase gap-filling didn’t run
ref_diet Which diet-study citations (the metaweb’s own Reference column) fed this FG’s row/columns in Ecopath_diet diet_references_by_fg.csv (output/diet/), rolled up from build_species_diet()’s per-predator citations via the same species→FG mapping the diet matrix itself uses
ref_methods The calculation METHOD/literature behind PB_FG/QB_FG (as opposed to ref_pbqb_traits, which is about the data, not the method) dispatch_group in PB_QB_spp.csv (output/pbqb-traits/), mapped to a literature citation
ImportantThis is a summary, not a full audit trail

Each column is a best-effort summary (distinct values " | "-joined when a FG drew on more than one source across years/species/studies) — the individual block’s own CSV named in the table above has the complete detail. A column is left blank for a FG when the block behind it hasn’t run yet against this workbook, with a message saying so rather than an error; running build_fg_references_sheet() again later (as 04_diets.R does every run) fills in whatever is available by then.

13 Other library files

13.1 lib_worms_taxonomy_lookup.R

Library file, no top-level driver code — source()’d automatically by the numbered scripts. Defines a single function:

worms_taxonomy_lookup(names_vector) — the single taxonomy source used everywhere in the pipeline (so genus/family comparisons are always apples-to-apples, never a FishBase-derived family compared against a WoRMS-derived one for the same actual family). Strips "Genus spp." suffixes so genus-level placeholders still resolve, chunks the query list (50 names/chunk) against worrms::wm_records_names(), picks the best (status == "accepted" preferred) match per name, and returns data.table(original_name, search_term, AphiaID, matched_name, rank, status, phylum, class, order, family, genus). Explicitly namespaces purrr::map() rather than calling map() bare — the maps package (loaded elsewhere in this pipeline for basemap plotting) also exports an unrelated map(), and whichever package attached most recently silently wins an unqualified call.

13.2 lib_build_species_df_from_survey.R

Library file — bridges 01_biomass.R’s survey output and 03_pbqb-traits.R’s PB/QB estimation. Defines:

build_species_df_from_survey(survey_csv_path, year_ecopath_range, out_rds_path = NULL) — reads species_density_regional_combined.csv, restricts to YEAR_ECOPATH’s snapshot years (the same years used for the Ecopath sheet’s Biomass column, so PB/QB weighting and the reported Biomass describe the same time snapshot), collapses to one mean-density Biomass value per species (stops with an error if any species maps to more than one FG within that year range — shouldn’t happen given upstream FG-matching logic), sets Yield := NA (this survey pipeline has no catch/landings data of its own — F = Yield/Biomass isn’t computable from it directly). Optionally saves the result as an .rds. Returns species_df(Species, FG, FG_name, Biomass, Yield).

14 Standalone cartography utilities (not part of the numbered pipeline)

14.1 export_gsa_boundaries.R

Standalone — does not need 01_biomass.R or the shared library sourced first. Downloads the same GFCM GSA shapefile via the same URL and GSA-numbering fix (SMU_CODE 111/112 → 11). Western-Med-specific as delivered: restricts to GSA 1–11 — change the gsa_num %in% 1:11 filter to any other GSA range to export a different region’s boundaries. Simplifies the result for web embedding via rmapshaper::ms_simplify(keep = 0.05, keep_shapes = TRUE) (keeps small polygons like Alboran Island, GSA 2, from being deleted outright), and writes gsa_boundaries.geojson — the file embedded in medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd’s interactive Leaflet map.

14.2 fig_WMed_basemap.R

Standalone map-generation script (not sourced by anything else), purely cartographic — not required by the numbered pipeline at all. Western-Med-specific as delivered: downloads the GFCM GSA shapefile, splits polygons into Western Med (GSA 1–11, the target study area) vs. context GSAs (12, 16 — Northern Tunisia, South of Sicily) and draws the Sicily Channel divide (Cape Bon–Cape Lilibeo) as the Western/Central Mediterranean boundary; all three of those choices would need editing to produce an equivalent basemap for a different region. The general technique — label placement via the “pole of inaccessibility” algorithm (polylabelr, more robust than st_point_on_surface() for narrow, coastline-hugging GSAs) plus a high-resolution (rnaturalearthhires) coastline — is reusable as-is for any region. Output: output/plots/westmed_gsa_map.png.

15 Adapting this pipeline to a different region

Everything below applies whether the target region is a single GSA, an arbitrary GSA subset, the whole Mediterranean GSA-by-GSA, or a fully custom (non-GSA) boundary.

15.1 Preferred method: source the scripts with pre-set options

01_biomass.R, 02_fisheries.R, 03_pbqb-traits.R, and 04_diets.R are source-able: every region/year/method knob each one uses (FILTER_AREAS, TARGET_COUNTRIES, YEAR_ECOPATH, START_YEAR/END_YEAR, TS_YEARS, DATASET_VERSION, FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE, OUTLIER_METHOD, PB_QB_SELECTION_MODE, out_dir/pcloud_dir/git_dir, and a handful more — each script’s own “Configuration” section, under a comment block headed SOURCE-ABLE SCRIPT, lists exactly which variables it recognizes) is wrapped in if (!exists("VAR", envir = .GlobalEnv, inherits = FALSE)) VAR <- <default>. That means:

  • If a variable is already set in the calling environment before the script is source()-d, the script uses that value and skips its own default entirely.
  • If it is not set, the script falls back to its original Western Med default (GSA 1–11, 1994–2023) — running any of the three scripts on its own, exactly as before, is completely unchanged.

run_pipeline_demo.R is a driver script demonstrating this: it sets out_dir/pcloud_dir/git_dir once, then source()s 01_biomass.R → 02_fisheries.R → 03_pbqb-traits.R → 04_diets.R in that dependency order (01 must run first — see Adapting this pipeline to a different region below and each script’s own header comment for why; 04 specifically needs 01’s FG_spp_Ecopath sheet in the shared workbook for its species’ share-of-FG-biomass values, and is otherwise optional — diet composition isn’t needed for every run). It includes two ready-to-run examples, switched via a RUN_MODE flag at the top:

  • RUN_MODE <- "westmed_default" — sets nothing beyond the three paths; every script uses its built-in West Med default.
  • RUN_MODE <- "custom_example" — sets FILTER_AREAS <- 17:18, TARGET_COUNTRIES <- c("Italy", "Croatia", "Slovenia"), YEAR_ECOPATH <- 2005:2007, TS_YEARS <- 2000:2020, and START_YEAR/END_YEAR <- 2000/2020 as a worked Adriatic-GSA example — copy and adapt this block for any other region/year range, setting only the variables that need to differ from the West Med default.

YEAR_ECOPATH in particular is read by all three scripts and must stay consistent across them (it defines the single Biomass snapshot the PB/QB weights are computed against) — setting it once in the driver script before any of the three are sourced keeps them in sync automatically, which is easy to forget when editing each script’s hardcoded copy by hand.

This sourcing approach and the manual per-script editing approach described in the rest of this section are not mutually exclusive — the if (!exists(...)) guards mean a variable can still be hardcoded by editing the script directly (equivalent to always setting it), or left alone to pick up a caller’s override, on a variable-by-variable basis.

To model a different GSA or set of GSAs (Adriatic, Ionian, Aegean, Levant, or the whole basin), starting from 01_biomass.R as a template:

  • FILTER_AREAS — set to the target GSA number(s). No other GSA-based code in lib_survey_fg_density_functions.R hardcodes a Western-Med-only assumption — compute_strata_area_by_area(), weight_by_strata(), weight_by_area() etc. are all region-agnostic and operate on whatever area_ids/area_shp they’re given.
  • ALBORAN_GSAS/fix_alboran_longitude_sign() — a Western-Med-only correction (GSA 1–3); harmless no-op elsewhere, delete if not needed.
  • MEDIAS_HANDBOOK_AREAS/GSA_AREA_CROSSWALK — only populated for Western Med GSAs; add the target region’s own rows from the MEDIAS Handbook (or rely entirely on the bathymetry-derived 10–200 m fallback if MEDIAS data or handbook-reported areas aren’t available for that region).
  • out_dir/pcloud_dir/git_dir paths, and the raw MEDITS/MEDIAS/FG- reference file paths under them — point at the target region’s own data.
  • export_gsa_boundaries.R/fig_WMed_basemap.R — optional cartography only; edit the GSA-range filters if you want an equivalent map for the new region, otherwise skip both entirely (nothing downstream needs them).

To model a fully custom (non-GSA) region anywhere in the Mediterranean, use 01_survey_density_custom.R directly: set AREA_NAME, CUSTOM_AREA_TYPE ("bbox" or "shapefile"), and CUSTOM_BBOX/CUSTOM_SHAPEFILE_PATH to the target boundary — no GSA-specific code path is involved at all in this script (the Alboran-sign fix is present but is a documented no-op outside GSA 1–3).

Everything downstream of Step 1 — 02_fao_catches.R, 03b_ecobase.R, 03_pbqb-traits.R, and the shared library’s Excel-export/PB-QB functions — is already region-agnostic: they operate on whatever strata_area_by_area.csv and species_density_regional_combined.csv Step 1 produced, with no Western-Med-specific logic beyond 02_fao_catches.R’s area_filter_values (the FAO/GFCM division codes 37.1.1–37.1.3, which likewise need changing to match the target region’s own FAO division codes) and 03b_ecobase.R’s MED_LON_MIN/MAX/MED_LAT_MIN/MAX bounding box (already covers the full Mediterranean by default — Gibraltar to the Levant — so it does not need editing for a sub-region; narrow it only if you specifically want to exclude literature models from elsewhere in the basin).

16 Script 5 — 04_diets.R

Role: Pipeline Step 5, optional — builds an EwE diet-composition matrix (predator FG × prey FG) from the Mediterranean trophic metaweb database (data_entry_metaweb_empty.xlsx - currently an empty template, no DATA_ENTRY rows yet), collapsed from species-level predator-prey records up to the same Functional Group scheme used everywhere else in the pipeline. Auto-runs on source(), same convention as 01_biomass.R/02_fisheries.R/03_pbqb-traits.R — no separate function call needed afterward.

Dependency: must run after 01_biomass.R has written to the same ECOPATH_WORKBOOK_PATH — it reads biomass_proportion_by_species_fg.csv (01_biomass.R’s own native CSV output, output/biomass/ — not a workbook sheet) for each species’ share of its Functional Group’s total biomass (“proportion B”), which weights that species’ own diet more heavily within its FG’s combined diet the larger a share of the FG’s biomass it represents. If that CSV isn’t there yet (01 hasn’t run against this workbook), it falls back to SPECIES_BIOMASS_IN_FG_CSV_PATH (a small manual CSV, same schema: species/fg_name/biomass_proportion) for whichever species×FG pairs are still missing, and finally to an equal split among an FG’s remaining mapped species for anything neither source covers.

16.1 Inputs

File Format Role
<pcloud_dir>/data/Complementary data/data_entry_metaweb_empty.xlsx xlsx Mediterranean trophic metaweb — predator-prey diet records (DATA_ENTRY), taxonomic code lookup (Taxonomic_codes), generic/non-taxonomic prey-category lookup (Non_taxonomic_groups)
<pcloud_dir>/data/FG_WMed.xlsx, sheet 4 xlsx Species → Functional Group reference — same file 01_biomass.R uses, so every species already assigned an FG there needs no manual entry here
output/biomass/biomass_proportion_by_species_fg.csv csv Written by 01_biomass.R — species’ share of biomass within their FG (“proportion B”), the preferred source
output/biomass/FG_lookup.csv csv Full FG_num/FG_name reference, used to resolve FG_num for the new diet_references_by_fg.csv output below
<pcloud_dir>/data/species_to_fg_missing.csv csv Manual species→FG fallback, only for species this run needs that aren’t in FG_WMed.xlsx at all
<pcloud_dir>/data/species_biomass_in_fg_missing.csv csv Manual biomass-share fallback, only for species×FG pairs missing from biomass_proportion_by_species_fg.csv
<pcloud_dir>/data/ewe_group_table.csv csv EwE group numbering (group_number/group_name/is_predator) — defines row/column order and which FGs get a predator column in the exported matrix

16.2 Outputs

  • output/diet/diet_composition_ewe.csv (2026-09-17: under output/diet/, not the top-level output/) — EwE’s own plain-text diet-composition import format: blank + “Prey \ predator” header, predator columns by group number (is_predator groups only), prey rows by group number + name (full group list), decimal-comma quoted values (unquoted would silently split into extra columns, since the delimiter is also a comma), Import/Sum/1-Sum rows at the bottom.
  • output/diet/diet_references_by_fg.csv — added 2026-09-17 — one row per FG, the distinct diet-study citations (the metaweb’s own Reference column) behind every species that maps into that FG as a predator. Read back by build_fg_references_sheet() to populate the final workbook’s FG_References sheet’s ref_diet column (see the shared-library section below).
  • output/ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx, sheet Ecopath_diet — the same predator-FG × prey-FG matrix, written into the shared workbook as plain numeric values (no decimal-comma/quoting — that convention is specific to EwE’s plain-text import, not needed inside an Excel sheet). Columns: Prey_FG_num, Prey_FG_name, then one Predator_<num>_<name> column per predator FG.
  • output/ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx, sheet FG_References — built here too (04_diets.R runs last), after Ecopath_diet — see “The FG_References sheet” in the shared-library section below.

16.3 Key functions

Function Role
read_metaweb(path) Reads DATA_ENTRY/Taxonomic_codes/Non_taxonomic_groups from the metaweb workbook
build_code_lookup(tax_codes, nontax_groups) Builds one code→name lookup from both tables (Taxonomic_codes wins on a code collision)
resolve_codes(codes, lookup) Predator/prey code → scientific name or generic-category label
pick_diet_metric(dt) Picks one diet-proportion value per DATA_ENTRY row via DIET_METRIC_PRIORITY (IRI preferred; Presence-only records get an equal split among a study’s present prey)
build_species_diet(data_entry, lookup) Species-level predator→prey diet proportions, weighted by study sample size and normalized per predator
read_species_to_fg_from_reference(path, sheet) Reads FG_WMed.xlsx’s species→FG assignment
build_species_to_fg(needed_species, reference_path, missing_csv_path) FG_WMed.xlsx first, manual CSV fallback only for genuinely missing species
read_biomass_proportion_from_workbook(workbook_path, sheet = "FG_spp_Ecopath") Reads species’ share of FG biomass from 01_biomass.R’s own output — the preferred “proportion B” source
build_biomass_in_fg(needed_species_fg, workbook_path, fallback_csv_path) Combines the workbook source with the manual fallback CSV, reporting coverage counts at each tier
build_fg_diet(species_diet, species_to_fg, biomass_in_fg) Expands species-level diet to FG×FG, biomass-weighting each predator species’ contribution within its FG, then collapses to one number per (predator FG, prey FG)
export_ewe_matrix_csv(fg_diet, group_table, out_path) Writes the EwE plain-text import CSV
build_ecopath_diet_sheet(fg_diet, group_table) Builds the same matrix as a plain numeric data.table for the Ecopath_diet sheet
run_pipeline(...) Runs all of the above end to end; called unconditionally at the bottom of the script

17 Methodology document — medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd

A Quarto methodology write-up (format: html, all R chunks eval: false — illustrative, not executed at render time) documenting lib_survey_fg_density_functions.R and the two Step-1 driver scripts. Key features:

  • Embedded interactive map — the actual gsa_boundaries.geojson (from export_gsa_boundaries.R) is inlined as a JSON blob and rendered via Leaflet.js. Lets a reader toggle between the GSA-based and custom-region workflows, live-preview FILTER_AREAS/CUSTOM_BBOX, and generate a ready-to-paste R config block — all client-side JavaScript, no R executes behind it.
  • Section structure: Overview (with mermaid diagrams for the pipeline, FG-matching sub-flow, and density/weighting sub-flow) → Input files → Interactive configuration → Functional Group matching (worked examples of each matching stage) → Study-area definition (GSA vs. custom) → Per-haul density and corrections (swept-area formula, depth strata, catchability, outliers) → Strata-weighted density → Region-wide aggregation → MEDIAS acoustic survey → Validation plots → Sample coverage map → Outputs → a full ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx deep-dive, including the same final sheet-order table reproduced above.
  • Real output files are copied into figures//downloads/ folders next to the rendered document (via safe_include_graphics()/safe_download_link() helpers) so the rendered HTML is portable off the author’s own machine, since out_dir is otherwise an absolute local path.

```{r setup, include=FALSE, eval=TRUE}
# figure chunks below override eval/echo individually so this document can
# show real pipeline output when it's available; everything else in the
# document stays non-executed (eval/echo defaults come from the execute:
# key in the YAML header above, same as medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd).
# NOTE: this setup chunk itself needs an explicit eval=TRUE, unlike in the
# .Rmd version — Quarto's top-level `execute: eval: false` applies to every
# chunk including this one, whereas knitr::opts_chunk$set(eval = FALSE)
# only affected chunks *after* it ran. Without this override,
# safe_include_graphics() below is never defined and every figure chunk
# that calls it fails with "could not find function".

# safe_include_graphics(): shows the real figure if it's been copied into
# figures/ next to this .Rmd (see "Populating the real figures" below);
# otherwise prints a plain placeholder message instead of erroring, so this
# document always knits cleanly whether or not the pipeline has been run
# yet against the real WestMed EwE pCloud drive data.
safe_include_graphics <- function(path, script, out_dir_note) {
  if (file.exists(path)) {
    knitr::include_graphics(path)
  } else {
    cat(
      "*Figure not yet available.* Run **", script, "**",
      " against the real WestMed EwE pCloud drive data, then copy\n`",
      out_dir_note, "`\ninto a `figures/` folder next to this `.Rmd` ",
      "(as `", basename(path), "`) and re-knit.",
      sep = ""
    )
  }
}
```

# Purpose of this document

This document describes a general-purpose R pipeline for turning MEDITS/
MEDIAS trawl-survey and catch data into input sheets for an Ecopath-with-
Ecosim (EwE) model, for **any region in the Mediterranean Sea** — a single
GFCM Geographical Sub-Area (GSA), any subset or the full set of GSAs, or a
completely user-defined region (a bounding box or an arbitrary shapefile
that doesn't line up with GSA lines at all, e.g. a bay, an MPA, or a
footprint spanning parts of several GSAs). It covers every script provided,
what each one reads and writes, and every function in the shared library
those scripts call.

:::: {.callout-warning}
## Some older sections may not reflect every recent change

This document has been updated for the 2026-09-17 per-block output CSV
subfolder change and the new `FG_References` sheet (see the shared-library
section below), and the top-level "Pipeline overview" and "Excel workbook
functions" diagrams have been brought current. A handful of older
sections elsewhere in the document may still describe an earlier script
naming or workbook layout that predates those changes — if something here
disagrees with a script's own header comment or with
`medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd`, the code and that methodology document
are the source of truth.
::::

The scripts as delivered are configured for the **Western Mediterranean**
(GSA 1–11) as a **worked example** — this is what `01_biomass.R`,
`export_gsa_boundaries.R` and `fig_WMed_basemap.R` currently point at, and
what a few reference tables (e.g. the MEDIAS Handbook area crosswalk) are
currently populated for. The underlying mechanism — GSA-based selection via
`FILTER_AREAS`, or an arbitrary region via `01_survey_density_custom.R` — is
not Western-Med-specific; see [Adapting this pipeline to a different region]
below for exactly what to change to point the same pipeline at another part
of the Mediterranean.

All scripts read from and write to **one shared workbook**,
`output/ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx`, via two library functions designed for
exactly this purpose:

* `upsert_workbook_sheets()` — adds/replaces named sheets, leaves every other
  sheet in the workbook untouched. Safe to call from any script, in any order,
  any number of times.
* `finalize_workbook_sheet_order()` — run **last**, after every upstream
  script has run at least once. Renames a couple of sheets (`FG_lookup` →
  `FG`, `References` → `PB_QB_References_`) and reorders every sheet into the
  final layout Ecopath/Ecosim expects.

Because of this design, the four numbered pipeline scripts are
**order-independent with respect to the shared workbook** — except where a
script's own calculations literally need a file another script produced
first (documented per-script below).

# Data and code locations

* **Code** — every script and library file documented here lives in the
  **WestMed EwE GitHub repository** (`WMed_EwE`, cloned locally to what the
  scripts call `git_dir`). All shared-library `source()` calls
  (`lib_survey_fg_density_functions.R`, `lib_worms_taxonomy_lookup.R`,
  `lib_build_species_df_from_survey.R`) resolve against this repository's
  `scripts/` folder.
* **Input data** — every raw survey, catch and reference file each script
  reads (`FG_WMed.xlsx`, `TM_list_(April_2019).xlsx`, MEDITS `TA.csv`/
  `TB.csv`, MEDIAS `abundance.csv`/`biomass.csv`, `ASFIS_sp_2026.1.csv`,
  the catchability CSV, GFCM capture/reference CSVs) is read from the
  **WestMed EwE pCloud drive** (`EwE Western Med 2026`, mounted/synced
  locally to what the scripts call `pcloud_dir`), **not** from the GitHub
  repository — the repository holds code only, no raw data.
* **Outputs** — every CSV, plot, and the shared `ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx`
  workbook are written to a local `out_dir` (not synced back to pCloud or
  GitHub by the scripts themselves).

Throughout this document, `<pcloud_dir>` means *the WestMed EwE pCloud
drive* and `<git_dir>` means *the WestMed EwE GitHub repository* — both are
plain local paths the top of each script resolves (hardcoded for one user,
or picked interactively via `rstudioapi::selectDirectory()` for anyone
else), not fixed locations baked into the code. Retargeting the pipeline to
a different region (see [Adapting this pipeline to a different region])
does not require moving off pCloud/GitHub — only pointing `pcloud_dir`/
`git_dir` at that region's own drive/repository, or adding that region's
data alongside the Western Med data in the same drive/repository.

:::: {.callout-note}
## A note on the plots and column tables in this document

This documentation was written without direct access to the WestMed EwE
pCloud drive's actual data files. The **column-reference tables**
throughout are not guesses — they list exactly the column names each
script's own code reads (`select()`/`merge()`/`setnames()` calls, etc.), so
they're accurate regardless of the actual data values. The **figures**,
however, are the pipeline's own real output and only appear once you've
generated them — see [Populating the real figures] below.
::::

## Populating the real figures

Several sections below embed a figure via a small `safe_include_graphics()`
helper (defined in this document's setup chunk). Each one checks for a file
under `figures/` (a folder that must sit next to this `.Rmd`) and shows the
real image if present, or a plain "not yet available" message with the
exact script/output path to copy from if not — so this document always
knits cleanly either way. To populate them for real:

1. Run the relevant script (named in each placeholder message) against the
   real WestMed EwE pCloud drive data.
2. Copy the named `.png` from that script's `<out_dir>/plots/` into a
   `figures/` folder next to this `.Rmd`, keeping the same filename.
3. Re-knit.

Four **other** diagrams in this document (the pipeline flow, the
FG-matching cascade, the workbook sheet assembly, and the PB/QB taxon
dispatch diagrams) are a different kind of figure: **Mermaid flowcharts**,
in the same style as `medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd`'s own diagrams
(same `flowchart LR` layout, the same gray/purple/teal/coral
`classDef` palette). They show the code's own logic — which function
calls which, which sheet gets written by what, which fallback branch runs
when — built directly from the script and library contents described
here, not from any survey or model output. They're written as native
Quarto ` ```{mermaid} ` code chunks, so Quarto renders them to an inline
SVG at render time (no CDN, no external script tag, no internet
connection needed to view the finished HTML) — unlike the four
`safe_include_graphics()` placeholders above, they always render, with
or without access to the pCloud drive.

# Try it: pick your years and region {#sec-try-it}

The parameters below (`YEAR_ECOPATH`, `TS_YEARS`, `FILTER_AREAS`/`AREA_NAME`/`CUSTOM_BBOX`, `DROP_OUTLIERS`, `NORMALIZE_TS`, `STRATA`) are the ones actually exposed as top-level variables in `01_biomass.R`'s and `01_survey_density_custom.R`'s own `## STEP 1: Configuration` blocks. Adjust them here to get a ready-to-paste block for whichever script you're using - it just mirrors the real variable names back at you, it doesn't run anything.

::: {.callout-note}
### This generates a config block, not a live result
This is a plain HTML/JS form, evaluated entirely in your browser when the rendered document is open - there's no R behind it. It can't execute the pipeline or show you an actual result; you still run the real `.R` script yourself, with the block below pasted in at its Configuration step.
:::

::: {.callout-note}
### Both modes are now accurate, live map previews
The **custom-region bounding box** updates the map live and exactly - it's just four numbers drawn as a rectangle.

The **GSA boundaries** below are the real, official GFCM Geographical Sub-Area polygons - downloaded directly from GFCM's own data (`gfcmsitestorage.blob.core.windows.net/.../GFCM_GSA.zip`, the same source `01_biomass.R` itself uses), simplified for web display (`rmapshaper::ms_simplify()`, 5% vertex retention) and coordinate precision rounded to ~11m (4 decimal places) - both changes purely to keep the embedded file size reasonable; neither affects what's visible at this map's zoom level. Selected GSAs (from the field below) highlight in blue; unselected GSAs shown in grey for context.
:::

```{=html}
</script>
```

```{=html}
<script>
function qs(id) { return document.getElementById(id); }

document.querySelectorAll('input[name="script-type"]').forEach(function(radio) {
  radio.addEventListener('change', toggleScriptFields);
});

// --- live map preview -----------------------------------------------------
var configMap = L.map('config-map', { scrollWheelZoom: false }).setView([39, 5], 5);
L.tileLayer('https://{s}.tile.openstreetmap.org/{z}/{x}/{y}.png', {
  attribution: '&copy; OpenStreetMap contributors',
  maxZoom: 10
}).addTo(configMap);

var bboxRect = null;
var gsaLayer = null;
var gsaGeojsonData = null;
try {
  gsaGeojsonData = JSON.parse(document.getElementById('gsa-geojson-data').textContent);
} catch (e) {
  console.warn('Could not parse embedded GSA GeoJSON:', e);
}

function selectedGsaNumbers() {
  return qs('filter-areas').value.split(',')
    .map(function(s) { return parseFloat(s.trim()); })
    .filter(function(n) { return !isNaN(n); });
}

function styleGsaFeature(feature) {
  var selected = selectedGsaNumbers();
  var isSelected = selected.indexOf(feature.properties.gsa_num) !== -1;
  return {
    color: isSelected ? '#0d6efd' : '#888888',
    weight: isSelected ? 2 : 1,
    fillColor: isSelected ? '#0d6efd' : '#cccccc',
    fillOpacity: isSelected ? 0.35 : 0.12
  };
}

function updateGsaLayer() {
  if (gsaLayer) { configMap.removeLayer(gsaLayer); gsaLayer = null; }
  if (!gsaGeojsonData) return;
  gsaLayer = L.geoJSON(gsaGeojsonData, {
    style: styleGsaFeature,
    onEachFeature: function(feature, layer) {
      layer.bindTooltip('GSA ' + feature.properties.gsa_num +
                        (feature.properties.SMU_NAME ? ' - ' + feature.properties.SMU_NAME : ''),
                        { sticky: true });
    }
  }).addTo(configMap);
}

function updateMapPreview() {
  var isCustom = qs('script-custom').checked;

  if (bboxRect) { configMap.removeLayer(bboxRect); bboxRect = null; }

  if (isCustom) {
    if (gsaLayer) { configMap.removeLayer(gsaLayer); gsaLayer = null; }
    var xmin = parseFloat(qs('bbox-xmin').value);
    var xmax = parseFloat(qs('bbox-xmax').value);
    var ymin = parseFloat(qs('bbox-ymin').value);
    var ymax = parseFloat(qs('bbox-ymax').value);
    if ([xmin, xmax, ymin, ymax].every(function(v) { return !isNaN(v); }) && xmax > xmin && ymax > ymin) {
      bboxRect = L.rectangle([[ymin, xmin], [ymax, xmax]], {
        color: '#0d6efd', weight: 2, fillColor: '#0d6efd', fillOpacity: 0.15
      }).addTo(configMap);
      configMap.fitBounds(bboxRect.getBounds(), { padding: [30, 30] });
    }
  } else {
    updateGsaLayer();
    if (gsaLayer) {
      configMap.fitBounds(gsaLayer.getBounds(), { padding: [20, 20] });
    } else {
      configMap.setView([39, 5], 5);   // embedded data failed to parse - fall back to a plain view
    }
  }
}

['bbox-xmin', 'bbox-xmax', 'bbox-ymin', 'bbox-ymax'].forEach(function(id) {
  qs(id).addEventListener('input', updateMapPreview);
});
qs('filter-areas').addEventListener('input', function() {
  if (!qs('script-custom').checked) updateGsaLayer();   // re-style only, don't re-fit bounds on every keystroke
});

function toggleScriptFields() {
  var isCustom = qs('script-custom').checked;
  qs('westmed-fields').style.display = isCustom ? 'none' : 'block';
  qs('custom-fields').style.display = isCustom ? 'block' : 'none';
  updateMapPreview();
}

function rYears(a, b) {
  a = parseInt(a, 10); b = parseInt(b, 10);
  return a + ':' + b;
}

function generateConfig() {
  var isCustom = qs('script-custom').checked;
  var yearEcopath = rYears(qs('year-ecopath-start').value, qs('year-ecopath-end').value);
  var tsYears = rYears(qs('ts-years-start').value, qs('ts-years-end').value);
  var strata = qs('strata').checked ? 'TRUE' : 'FALSE';
  var dropOutliers = qs('drop-outliers').checked ? 'TRUE' : 'FALSE';
  var normalizeTs = qs('normalize-ts').checked ? 'TRUE' : 'FALSE';

  var lines = [];

  if (!isCustom) {
    var filterAreas = qs('filter-areas').value.split(',').map(function(s) { return s.trim(); }).filter(Boolean);
    lines.push('## --- generated by the "Try it" section - 01_biomass.R ---');
    lines.push('FILTER_AREAS  <- c(' + filterAreas.join(', ') + ')');
    lines.push('STRATA        <- ' + strata + '                       # function attribute: strata');
    lines.push('YEAR_ECOPATH  <- ' + yearEcopath + '                   # function attribute: year_ecopath');
    lines.push('TS_YEARS      <- ' + tsYears + '                        # function attribute: ts_years');
    lines.push('DROP_OUTLIERS <- ' + dropOutliers + '                        # remove flagged observations (TRUE) or only report them (FALSE)');
    lines.push('NORMALIZE_TS  <- ' + normalizeTs + '   # TRUE = Ecosim series rescaled to first value = 1; FALSE = raw density');
  } else {
    var areaName = qs('area-name').value.trim() || 'custom_region';
    var xmin = qs('bbox-xmin').value, xmax = qs('bbox-xmax').value;
    var ymin = qs('bbox-ymin').value, ymax = qs('bbox-ymax').value;
    lines.push('## --- generated by the "Try it" section - 01_survey_density_custom.R ---');
    lines.push('AREA_NAME     <- "' + areaName + '"');
    lines.push('STRATA        <- ' + strata);
    lines.push('YEAR_ECOPATH  <- ' + yearEcopath);
    lines.push('TS_YEARS      <- ' + tsYears);
    lines.push('DROP_OUTLIERS <- ' + dropOutliers);
    lines.push('NORMALIZE_TS  <- ' + normalizeTs);
    lines.push('CUSTOM_BBOX   <- c(xmin = ' + xmin + ', xmax = ' + xmax + ', ymin = ' + ymin + ', ymax = ' + ymax + ')');
  }

  qs('config-preview').textContent = lines.join('\n');
}

function copyConfig() {
  var text = qs('config-preview').textContent;
  if (!text || text.indexOf('Click "Generate') !== -1) { generateConfig(); text = qs('config-preview').textContent; }
  navigator.clipboard.writeText(text).then(function() {
    var btn = event.target;
    var original = btn.textContent;
    btn.textContent = 'Copied!';
    setTimeout(function() { btn.textContent = original; }, 1200);
  });
}

function downloadConfig() {
  var text = qs('config-preview').textContent;
  if (!text || text.indexOf('Click "Generate') !== -1) { generateConfig(); text = qs('config-preview').textContent; }
  var isCustom = qs('script-custom').checked;
  var filename = isCustom ? 'custom_config_block.R' : 'biomass_config_block.R';
  var blob = new Blob([text], { type: 'text/plain' });
  var link = document.createElement('a');
  link.href = URL.createObjectURL(blob);
  link.download = filename;
  document.body.appendChild(link);
  link.click();
  document.body.removeChild(link);
}

toggleScriptFields();
</script>
```

| Parameter | Script variable | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| Ecopath base years | `YEAR_ECOPATH` | Which years get averaged into the `Ecopath`/`FG_spp_Ecopath` base-year snapshot |
| Ecosim time-series range | `TS_YEARS` | Which years appear in the `Ecosim`/`FG_spp_Ecosim` time series |
| Strata weighting | `STRATA` | Bathymetry-weighted density estimator (see "Strata-weighted density" in `medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd`) vs. a simple mean |
| Drop outliers | `DROP_OUTLIERS` | Whether flagged observations are actually removed, or only reported in `survey_outliers_flagged.csv` |
| Normalize Ecosim series | `NORMALIZE_TS` | Whether `Ecosim`'s series is a relative index (first value = 1) or raw density |
| GSA numbers (biomass script only) | `FILTER_AREAS` | Which GFCM GSAs are included in the analysis (see "Study area definition", Approach A, in `medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd`) |
| Area name (custom only) | `AREA_NAME` | Output subfolder name and file-naming prefix - keeps runs for different custom regions from overwriting each other |
| Bounding box (custom only) | `CUSTOM_BBOX` | The study area's outer boundary, decimal-degree WGS84 (see "Study area definition", Approach B, in `medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd`) |

# Pipeline overview

The diagram below is a schematic of the actual script/function call
structure documented in this document — which script produces which
intermediate file, and which workbook sheets each one writes. It is not a
plot of any survey data or model output (same diagram style as
`medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd`'s "Pipeline flow" section).


**Step 1 — pick ONE:**

* `01_biomass.R`
  (MEDITS + MEDIAS, GFCM GSA areas), **or**
* `01_survey_density_custom.R`
  (MEDITS only, custom bbox/shapefile)

&#8595; produces `species_density_regional_combined.csv`,
`strata_area_by_area.csv`, and sheets `FG_spp_Ecopath`, `Ecopath`, `Ecosim`,
`FG_spp_Ecosim`, `FG` (renamed from `FG_lookup`), `traits_ewe`.

**Step 2:**

* `02_fao_catches.R`
  (reads Step 1's `strata_area_by_area.csv`)

&#8595; produces sheets `Catches_Ecopath`, `Catches_Ecosim`.

**Step 3 — independent, can run anytime before Step 4:**

* `03b_ecobase.R`

&#8595; produces `ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv` (optional Step 4 input).

**Step 4 — run last:**

* `03_pbqb-traits.R`
  (needs Step 1's `species_density_regional_combined.csv`; optionally reads
  Step 2's `fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv` and Step 3's
  `ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv`)

&#8595; produces sheets `PB_QB`, `PB_QB_spp`, `Ecobase`, `References` (renamed
to `PB_QB_References_`).

**Finally**, `finalize_workbook_sheet_order()` — called from whichever
script runs last — reorders every sheet into the final layout:

&#8595; `output/ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx`, final, ordered, ready for
Ecopath import.

Two more files are **not** part of the numbered pipeline, but support it:

* `lib_survey_fg_density_functions.R` and `lib_worms_taxonomy_lookup.R` and
  `lib_build_species_df_from_survey.R` — shared library files, `source()`'d
  by the numbered scripts, no top-level driver code of their own.
* `export_gsa_boundaries.R` and `fig_WMed_basemap.R` — standalone cartography
  utilities, not required by the numbered pipeline. `export_gsa_boundaries.R`
  in particular produces the GeoJSON embedded in `medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd`'s
  interactive map.

# Run order and dependencies

| Step | Script | Depends on | Must run before |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `01_biomass.R` **or** `01_survey_density_custom.R` (pick one) | nothing | 2, 4 |
| 2 | `02_fao_catches.R` | Step 1's `strata_area_by_area.csv` | — |
| 2a | `02a_fisheries_multisource.R` (optional, alongside Step 2) | Step 1's `strata_area_by_area.csv` and FG reference; optionally Step 2's `fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv` for the FAO-GFCM source | 4 (optional input, via `FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE`) |
| 3 | `03b_ecobase.R` | nothing (independent web query) | 4 (optional input) |
| 4 | `03_pbqb-traits.R` | Step 1's `species_density_regional_combined.csv`; optionally Step 2's/Step 2a's `fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv`, Step 2a's `landings_by_species_gsa_year_*.csv`, and Step 3's `ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv` | — |

`finalize_workbook_sheet_order()` should be called at the end of whichever
script ran *last* in a given session (each of the numbered scripts already
does this itself at its own final step).

---

# Script 1a — `01_biomass.R`

**Role:** Pipeline Step 1 of 4, GFCM-GSA-based version — the general
mechanism for **any GSA subset anywhere in the Mediterranean**, not just the
Western Med. Run this **or** `01_survey_density_custom.R`, not both, as Step
1. No dependency on the other numbered scripts; `02_fao_catches.R` and
`03_pbqb-traits.R` both require this step's output to exist first.

Runs two independent surveys over the same GSAs/FG scheme (MEDITS bottom
trawl + MEDIAS acoustic) and combines both into one set of Ecopath/Ecosim
sheets, rather than producing separate MEDIAS sheets. As delivered it's
configured for the Western Med (GSA 1–11, via `FILTER_AREAS <- 1:11`) as a
worked example; retargeting it to a different GSA subset elsewhere in the
Mediterranean — the Adriatic, the Aegean, the whole basin — is a matter of
changing that one line and the region-specific reference tables listed in
[Adapting this pipeline to a different region].

## Inputs

| File | Format | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/FG_WMed.xlsx`, sheet 4 | xlsx | Species → Functional Group reference (`dataframe2`: `ESPECIE`→`ScientificName`, `GF`→`FG_num`, `FG_name`) |
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/FG_WMed.xlsx`, sheet `traits_ewe` | xlsx | Per-species life-history/ecology traits |
| `<pcloud_dir>/.../TM_list_(April_2019).xlsx` | xlsx | MEDITS species-code → scientific-name lookup |
| `<pcloud_dir>/.../Demersal/TA.csv` | csv | MEDITS haul/sample metadata (position, depth, gear, swept-area inputs) |
| `<pcloud_dir>/.../Demersal/TB.csv` | csv | MEDITS catch-per-haul data |
| `<pcloud_dir>/.../Acoustic/abundance.csv`, `biomass.csv` | csv | MEDIAS acoustic-survey raw records |
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/ASFIS_sp_2026.1.csv` | csv | FAO ASFIS species code list (MEDIAS species-code → scientific name) |
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/catchability_factors_ecotrans_medits_2021_spp.csv` | csv (optional) | Per-species catchability correction factors (q); skipped with a message if absent |
| GFCM GSA shapefile | auto-downloaded zip from `gfcmsitestorage.blob.core.windows.net/website/5.Data/ArcGIS/GFCM_GSA.zip` | Study-area polygons (`area_shp`); `SMU_CODE` 111/112 (W/E Sardinia) merged into GSA 11 |
| NOAA bathymetry | auto-downloaded via `marmap::getNOAA.bathy()` inside `compute_strata_area_by_area()` | Depth-stratum area calculation |

`FILTER_AREAS <- 1:11` (this example's Western Med GSAs — set to any GSA
number or set of GSA numbers, anywhere in the GFCM area, to retarget the
whole script to a different region), `YEAR_ECOPATH <- 1994:1996`,
`TS_YEARS <- 1995:2023`, `STRATA <- TRUE`, `DROP_OUTLIERS <- FALSE`,
`NORMALIZE_TS <- TRUE`, `APPLY_AQUAMAPS_DEPTH_ADJUSTMENT <- FALSE` (opt-in —
see Step 7b below).

### Step 7b — AquaMaps shallow/deep depth adjustment (opt-in)

MEDITS' own 5 standard strata (`MEDITS_STRATA`) only cover 10–800 m.
`compute_strata_area_by_area()`'s area-proportion weighting normalizes
across just those 5 strata, so as delivered, this pipeline's Biomass/
density figures implicitly treat the whole modeled area as if it were
entirely 10–800 m seafloor — a species with real biomass shallower than
10 m or deeper than 800 m is invisible to these numbers, not just
imprecisely measured. Setting `APPLY_AQUAMAPS_DEPTH_ADJUSTMENT <- TRUE`
(default `FALSE`) adds genuine **derived** pseudo-strata: one shallow
(0–10 m) plus, by default, the deep zone split into **two** sub-bands —
800–1000 m and 1000–2850 m — rather than one lumped 800–6000 m stratum
(2850 m is a practical ceiling for the western Mediterranean's own real
depths specifically, not the Mediterranean-wide ~5267 m Calypso Deep,
which sits in the eastern/Ionian basin outside these GSAs). Each
pseudo-stratum gets its own real bathymetric area (the same
`compute_strata_area_by_area()` machinery already used for the 5 real
strata) and a density **derived, not measured**, anchored to the ONE
real MEDITS stratum immediately adjacent to it — the shallowest real
stratum (10–50 m) for the shallow pseudo-band, the deepest real stratum
(500–800 m) for every deep pseudo-band — rather than an area-weighted
average across all 5 real strata. Each pseudo-stratum's density equals
that adjacent stratum's own **measured** density (`compute_species_
anchor_stratum_density()`), scaled by the ratio of AquaMaps mean
suitability between the pseudo-band and that same adjacent stratum's own
depth range. A species at equal AquaMaps suitability in both bands gets
a multiplier of exactly 1 — its pseudo-stratum density literally equals
what MEDITS measured next door, with no distortion from averaging across
strata the species may barely occupy. (An earlier version of this
adjustment compared each pseudo-band's suitability against the whole
10–800 m composite range instead — that systematically understated
narrow pseudo-bands relative to the ~790 m-wide reference purely from
the width mismatch, independent of actual suitability level; the
anchor-stratum design replaces it.) The trade-off: a species with real
catch elsewhere in 10–800 m but none specifically in the one adjacent
anchor stratum gets no pseudo-stratum density at all for that area
(flagged in the audit sheet), rather than being extrapolated from
unrelated, non-adjacent strata. These pseudo-strata are folded into the
exact same `weight_by_strata()`/`weight_species_by_area()` calls used
for the 5 real strata — not a separate formula — so `fg_index`/
`fg_index_regional`/`species_density_regional` end up as one consistent
area-weighted sum across all 8 strata (5 real + 3 derived), real and
derived alike. The number and boundaries of deep sub-bands are
controlled by `apply_aquamaps_depth_adjustment()`'s `deep_ranges`
argument — a list of `c(from, to)` pairs — so this can be collapsed back
to one lump (`list(c(800, 6000))`) or split further without changing
anything else; all deep sub-bands anchor to the same deepest real
stratum, they don't chain off each other.

**Core assumption, stated plainly rather than absorbed silently**: a
species' relative density across depth is assumed proportional to
AquaMaps' relative occurrence-suitability across depth (its trapezoidal
`DepthMin`/`DepthPrefMin`/`DepthPrefMax`/`DepthMax` envelope, per
AquaMaps' own "Algorithm and Data Sources" documentation). Occurrence
suitability is not the same thing as density — this is a standard
extrapolation approach when direct sampling doesn't cover a species' full
depth range, but an approximation nonetheless. Every resulting multiplier
is written to the `AquaMaps_Depth_Adjustment` sheet (species, its
`DepthMin`/`DepthPrefMin`/`DepthPrefMax`/`DepthMax`, probability masses,
resulting `multiplier_shallow`/`multiplier_deep_800_1000`/
`multiplier_deep_1000_2850` (one column per configured deep sub-band,
named by its own depth bounds), and a `note` explaining species that got
NO adjustment for a given band — unmatched to AquaMaps, no depth
envelope in `am_hspen()`, or essentially zero AquaMaps suitability in
that band's own adjacent real anchor stratum, making a ratio against
that unstable) — reviewable per species, not a black box.

Requires the `aquamapsdata` R package (github.com/raquamaps/aquamapsdata),
the only verified way to get AquaMaps' per-species HSPEN depth-envelope
table without scraping — its `download_db()` pulls a real, deliberate
**~2GB (~10GB unpacked) local SQLite database once** (`ensure_aquamaps_db()`
uses `force = FALSE`, so this never re-downloads on subsequent runs).
Implemented in `lib_aquamaps_depth_extension.R` (species → AquaMaps
`SpeciesID` resolution, envelope fetch, envelope math, pseudo-strata
construction) — see that file's own header for the full mechanism.
Requires `STRATA <- TRUE` (errors clearly if not — there's no unstratified
equivalent). **Not yet wired into `01_survey_density_custom.R`** — only
`01_biomass.R` has this option so far.

Two outputs make the strata (real + derived) inspectable directly,
independent of `APPLY_AQUAMAPS_DEPTH_ADJUSTMENT` — they cover whichever
strata are actually in play, 5 or 8:

- **`survey_fg_depth_strata_profile.png`** (Step 8, `plot_strata_profile()`)
  — a bar chart of raw per-sample density by stratum, faceted per FG, for
  sanity-checking that known deep-water FGs actually peak in the deep
  strata and vice versa. Reads `weight_by_strata()`'s `per_stratum_raw`
  attribute specifically (not `per_stratum`), so a stratum with real
  sample data but no computed area-proportion for a given AreaID still
  shows up here rather than silently vanishing.
- **`FG_Density_by_Stratum`** sheet (Step 12c, `build_fg_density_by_stratum_sheet()`)
  — the same information as an Excel table: one row per FG, one column
  per stratum (region-wide, area-weighted, `YEAR_ECOPATH`-averaged), plus
  a `Total_Density` column that reproduces `fg_index_regional`'s own
  `mean_density` exactly, since each stratum column is that stratum's own
  additive contribution to the total, not an independently-normalized
  figure.

Columns as actually referenced by the code (from the `<pcloud_dir>` files

| Column | Used for |

```{r sample-coverage-fig, echo=FALSE, eval=TRUE, results="asis", fig.cap="plot_sample_map() output — MEDITS sample coverage by area."}
safe_include_graphics(
  "figures/survey_sample_coverage_map.png",
```
