## Processing steps

1. **Config** — resolve `out_dir`/`pcloud_dir`/`git_dir`, download/cache the GSA
   shapefile, source `lib_survey_fg_density_functions.R` and
   `lib_worms_taxonomy_lookup.R`.
2. **Load MEDITS data** — read `TA.csv`/`TB.csv`, compute swept area
   (`distance × wing_opening_m / 1e6`, wing opening in decimetres), convert
   DDMM.mmm lat/lon to decimal degrees, fix the Alboran Sea (GSA 1–3)
   longitude sign — a Western-Med-specific correction, harmless (a no-op) if
   your `FILTER_AREAS` doesn't include GSA 1–3 — reshape into the
   standardized `dataframe1`/`dataframe2` format. Runs `validate_survey_data()`
   and `plot_sample_map()` (→ `survey_sample_coverage_map.png`,
   `survey_sample_coverage_map_by_year.png`).
3. **FG matching (Steps 3–4)** — `prepare_fg_lookup()` →
   `match_species_to_fg()` → `match_nominate_subspecies_fg()` → manual
   overrides (species codes absent from the MEDITS taxonomic list, e.g.
   `ARGRACU`→*Argyropelecus aculeatus*) → `fallback_match_fg_by_taxonomy()`
   (Genus→Family→Order→Class→Phylum) → `apply_seed_fg_rules()` (FGs with zero
   reference species, e.g. Holothuroidea→"Sea cucumbers") →
   `summarize_unresolved_species()` → `survey_unmatched_for_manual_review.csv`.
4. **Densities (Step 5)** — `match_samples_to_area()`,
   `assign_depth_stratum()`, `compute_sample_densities()`, restrict to
   `FILTER_AREAS`, `apply_catchability_correction()` (if the catchability CSV
   exists), `remove_sample_outliers()` (→ `survey_outliers_flagged.csv` if
   any flagged), `compute_fg_densities_by_stratum()`.
5. **Strata/area weighting (Steps 6–7)** — `compute_strata_area_by_area()`
   (cached to `strata_area_by_area.csv`), `weight_by_strata()`,
   `weight_by_area()`, `compute_species_densities_by_stratum()`,
   `weight_species_by_area()`.
6. **MEDITS plots (Step 8)** — `plot_fg_timeseries_by_area()`,
   `plot_fg_timeseries_regional()`, `plot_strata_profile()` →
   `survey_fg_density_timeseries.png`,
   `survey_fg_density_timeseries_regional.png`,
   `survey_fg_depth_strata_profile.png`.

   ```{r fg-timeseries-fig, echo=FALSE, eval=TRUE, results="asis", fig.cap="plot_fg_timeseries_by_area() output — MEDITS trawl survey density by FG and area (strata-weighted)."}
   safe_include_graphics(
     "figures/survey_fg_density_timeseries.png",
     script = "01_biomass.R (or 01_survey_density_custom.R)",
     out_dir_note = "<out_dir>/plots/survey_fg_density_timeseries.png"
   )
   ```

   ```{r strata-profile-fig, echo=FALSE, eval=TRUE, results="asis", fig.cap="plot_strata_profile() output — raw per-sample density across depth strata, by FG."}
   safe_include_graphics(
     "figures/survey_fg_depth_strata_profile.png",
     script = "01_biomass.R (or 01_survey_density_custom.R)",
     out_dir_note = "<out_dir>/plots/survey_fg_depth_strata_profile.png"
   )
   ```
7. **MEDIAS acoustic survey (Step 9, sub-steps 9a–9g)** — load and collapse
   raw abundance/biomass records, map species codes via ASFIS, match to FG
   the same way as MEDITS, attach area (MEDIAS Handbook Table 1, hand-keyed
   `MEDIAS_HANDBOOK_AREAS` — as delivered this table only has Western Med
   entries, since that's this example's scope; extending to another region
   means adding that region's own Handbook rows, with a bathymetry-derived
   10–200 m fallback via `compute_strata_area_by_area()` covering any
   country/GSA combination the table doesn't have a row for), compute
   region-wide FG and species density, then
   **combine MEDITS + MEDIAS** into one FG-level and one species-level table
   (averaged on Year/FG or Year/species overlap, overlaps flagged/printed).
   Outputs: `medias_fg_annual_density_by_area.csv`,
   `medias_fg_annual_density_regional.csv`,
   `medias_fg_biomass_density_timeseries_regional.png`,
   `medias_fg_abundance_density_timeseries_regional.png`,
   `species_density_regional_combined.csv` (**contract file** — read by
   `lib_build_species_df_from_survey.R` for `03_pbqb-traits.R`).
8. **Excel export (Step 10)** — `survey_fg_annual_index.csv`,
   `survey_fg_annual_index_regional_combined.csv`, then
   `export_ecopath_ecosim_excel()` writes sheets `FG_spp_Ecopath`, `Ecopath`,
   `Ecosim`, `FG_spp_Ecosim` into `ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx`. **Also writes
   a `PB_QB_spp` scaffold** — reusing `FG_spp_Ecopath`'s own `Species`/
   `FG_num`/`Density`/`prop_sp_fg` (renamed to `PB_QB_spp`'s convention:
   `Biomass`, `prop_biomass_FG`), with `PB`/`prop_biomass_PB`/`QB`/
   `prop_biomass_QB` all blank and a `Note` column — so `PB_QB_spp` exists in
   the workbook after this script alone, without needing `03_pbqb-traits.R`
   (Step 4) to have run first. This function has no PB/QB computation of its
   own (that's entirely Step 4's, from life-history parameters, not survey
   density), so the scaffold's numbers are genuinely blank, not estimated.
   Running Step 4 afterward replaces the scaffold with the real sheet
   automatically (`upsert_workbook_sheets()` always fully replaces a
   same-named sheet). Guarded against clobbering the other way: if
   `PB_QB_spp` already has real (non-`NA`) `PB` values — i.e. Step 4 already
   ran against this exact workbook — re-running this script leaves it
   untouched rather than overwriting it with a blank scaffold again.
9. **Complete `FG_spp_Ecopath`/`FG_lookup` (Step 11)** — union of observed +
   reference-catalog species, zero-fill species with no base-year density,
   attach taxonomy, defensive check for FGs with no species at all. Written
   via `upsert_workbook_sheets()`.
10. **`traits_ewe` sheet (Step 12)** — parse `FG_WMed.xlsx`'s `traits_ewe`
    sheet (alternating "N: Group Name" header rows / species rows,
    fill-down logic), reconcile against `dataframe2`, flag naming-variant and
    missing-traits species. Written via `upsert_workbook_sheets()`.
11. **Finalize sheet order (Step 13)** — `finalize_workbook_sheet_order()`.

## Outputs

* **CSVs** (in `out_dir`): `survey_sample_coverage_map.png` and other plot
  PNGs (in `out_dir/plots`), `survey_unmatched_for_manual_review.csv`,
  `survey_outliers_flagged.csv`, `strata_area_by_area.csv`,
  `medias_fg_annual_density_by_area.csv`,
  `medias_fg_annual_density_regional.csv`,
  `species_density_regional_combined.csv`, `survey_fg_annual_index.csv`,
  `survey_fg_annual_index_regional_combined.csv`.
* **Workbook** `ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx`, sheets written/updated:
  `FG_spp_Ecopath`, `Ecopath`, `Ecosim`, `FG_spp_Ecosim`, `FG_lookup`
  (renamed to `FG`), `traits_ewe`, `PB_QB_spp` (scaffold, unless it
  already has real data from `03_pbqb-traits.R`), `AquaMaps_Depth_Adjustment`
  (only when `APPLY_AQUAMAPS_DEPTH_ADJUSTMENT <- TRUE` — see Step 7b), and
  `FG_Density_by_Stratum` (only when `STRATA <- TRUE` — Step 12c; density
  by FG x depth stratum, region-wide, covering whichever strata are
  actually in play).

---

# Script 1b — `01_survey_density_custom.R`

**Role:** Alternative Step 1, for **any study area anywhere in the
Mediterranean that doesn't line up with GSA boundaries** — a single custom
bounding box or shapefile (e.g. one EwE model's own footprint, a bay, an
MPA, or a region spanning parts of several GSAs, in any part of the basin).
This is the general-purpose route for a fully user-defined region; Script 1a
above is the general-purpose route for any GSA-aligned region instead. Run
this **instead of** `01_biomass.R`, never both. MEDITS-only —
does not run the MEDIAS acoustic survey.

Structurally identical to `01_biomass.R` for the MEDITS
loading and FG-matching stages (Steps 1–4 use the exact same shared-library
functions). What's genuinely different:

* **`AREA_NAME`** — a user-supplied label for this custom study area (typed
  interactively via `rstudioapi::showPrompt()`, or hardcoded for the
  `daniel` user). Used to build a **dedicated output subfolder**
  (`out_dir/<AREA_NAME>/`) and to suffix every output filename via helper
  functions `af()`/`pf()`, so two different custom-region runs (different
  `AREA_NAME`s) never collide or overwrite each other's outputs, and never
  collide with `01_biomass.R`'s own `out_dir`.
* **Study-area definition (Step 1b)** — `CUSTOM_AREA_TYPE <- "bbox"` (a
  rectangle defined by `CUSTOM_BBOX <- c(xmin, xmax, ymin, ymax)`),
  `"shapefile"` (an arbitrary `sf` polygon read via `st_read()`), or **`"gsa"`**
  (one or more GFCM GSAs, picked out of the same GSA shapefile
  `01_biomass.R` downloads, via `CUSTOM_GSA_IDS <- c(...)` —
  e.g. a single GSA, or a different regional grouping than that script's
  fixed `1:11` Western-Med range). For `"bbox"`/`"shapefile"` the result is a
  single-feature `area_shp` with `AREA_ID_COL = "area_id"`, always
  `area_id = 1` — there is no GSA sub-division inside a custom region unless
  the user builds one. For `"gsa"`, `area_shp` can have **one row per
  selected GSA**, each keeping its own `gsa_num` as `area_id` (same
  convention as Script 1a), so a multi-GSA custom region is NOT forced down
  to a single `area_id = 1`.
* **`AreaID` is derived spatially** for `"bbox"`/`"shapefile"`, not read off
  MEDITS' own `area` (GSA) column — every sample gets `AreaID = 1`, then
  `filter_samples_by_area()` drops everything outside the custom boundary.
  For `"gsa"`, `AreaID` follows the selected GSA(s) directly, same as Script
  1a.
* **Strata-area computation (Step 6)** — `compute_strata_area_by_area()`
  called with `area_ids = 1` for `"bbox"`/`"shapefile"` (a single custom
  polygon), or with the selected `CUSTOM_GSA_IDS` for `"gsa"` (one or more
  named GSAs) — the function itself is agnostic to this distinction either
  way.

**Exception to the `AREA_NAME` filename-suffixing rule:** three files stay at
plain, unsuffixed names on purpose, because `02_fao_catches.R` and
`03_pbqb-traits.R` read them by that exact fixed name regardless of which
Step-1 script produced them: `species_density_regional_combined.csv`,
`ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx`, `strata_area_by_area.csv`. The dedicated
`AREA_NAME` subfolder is what keeps these three from colliding with another
run's copies.

## Inputs

Same as `01_biomass.R` minus the MEDIAS/ASFIS/GSA-shapefile
inputs, plus (for `CUSTOM_AREA_TYPE = "shapefile"`) a user-supplied boundary
shapefile.

## Processing steps

Steps 1–5 mirror `01_biomass.R` (see above) using the custom
`area_shp`. Steps 6–12 are the same Excel-export / `FG_spp_Ecopath` /
`traits_ewe` / sheet-order-finalization logic, applied to a single-area
result. (Since this script skips MEDIAS, its `species_density_regional`
table is MEDITS-only — no MEDITS+MEDIAS averaging step.)

## Outputs

Same file set and workbook sheets as `01_biomass.R`
(see above), written to `out_dir/<AREA_NAME>/` with most filenames suffixed
`_<AREA_NAME>`, except the three contract files listed above which stay
unsuffixed.

---

# Script 2 — `02_fao_catches.R`

**Role:** Pipeline Step 2 of 4. **Requires** Step 1 to have already run — it
reads `strata_area_by_area.csv` (written by Step 1) to convert catch totals
into a t/km² density comparable to Biomass. Running this before Step 1 fails
with a clear "file not found" error pointing back to Step 1.

Config-driven catch-data loader: one `DATASETS` registry entry per source
format, so `GFCM_2025` and (a currently-commented-out) `FAO_2026` share one
processing path (`format = "gfcm_regional"`), while `FAO_2020`
(`format = "legacy_excel"`) is handled separately as a genuinely different
file layout. `DATASET_VERSION <- "GFCM_2025"` is the active, confirmed,
working source; `FAO_2020` is present but its file location is
**unconfirmed** on disk.

## Inputs

| File | Format | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `<git_dir>/data/raw/FI_Regional_2025.1.0/GFCM_Capture_Quantity.csv` | csv | Raw GFCM catch quantities |
| `.../CL_FI_SPECIES_GROUPS.csv` | csv | Species code → name reference (also auto-downloaded from FAO's open-data catalog if not found under `pcloud_dir`) |
| `.../CL_FI_COUNTRY_GROUPS.csv` | csv | Country code → name reference |
| `.../CL_FI_WATERAREA_DIVISION.csv` | csv | FAO/GFCM division code → name reference; catches filtered to divisions `37.1.1`/`37.1.2`/`37.1.3` |
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/FG_WMed.xlsx`, sheet 4 | xlsx | Species → FG reference, same as Script 1 |
| `strata_area_by_area.csv` | csv | **From Step 1.** Total study-area km² for the density conversion |

### Column reference — key input files

**`GFCM_Capture_Quantity.csv`** (raw catch, `<git_dir>/data/raw/FI_Regional_2025.1.0/`):

| Column | Used for |
|---|---|
| `SPECIES.ALPHA_3_CODE` | Joined against `CL_FI_SPECIES_GROUPS.csv` |
| `COUNTRY.UN_CODE` | Joined against `CL_FI_COUNTRY_GROUPS.csv` |
| `DIVISION.CODE` (`area_code_col`) | Joined against `CL_FI_WATERAREA_DIVISION.csv`; filtered to `37.1.1`/`37.1.2`/`37.1.3` (Western Med divisions — change for a different region) |
| `MEASURE` | Filtered to `"Q_tlw"` (live-weight tonnes) |
| `PERIOD` | Year |
| `VALUE` | Catch quantity |

**Reference CSVs** (same folder):

| File | Columns used |
|---|---|
| `CL_FI_SPECIES_GROUPS.csv` | `3A_Code` (species code) → `Name_En` (species name) |
| `CL_FI_COUNTRY_GROUPS.csv` | `UN_Code` → `Name_En` (country name) |
| `CL_FI_WATERAREA_DIVISION.csv` | `Code` (`area_join_col`) → `Name_En` (division name) |

**`FAO_2020` legacy Excel** (`format = "legacy_excel"`, alternate/unconfirmed
source):

| Column | Renamed to |
|---|---|
| `Species (scientific name)` | `Species` |
| `Area (FAO division)` | `Division` |
| `Area (FAO subarea)` | `Subarea` — filtered to `"Western Med (37.1)"` |
| `Year`, `Quantity`, `Country` | used as-is |

## Processing steps

1. **Config + dataset loading** — resolve directories, `load_gfcm_regional()`
   merges capture/species/country/area reference CSVs, filters to Western
   Med divisions, `MEASURE == "Q_tlw"`, `PERIOD >= 1995`. Builds
   `country_ts`/`species_ts` (Year × Country/Species × Division × Catch).
2. **Plots + audit CSVs** — country- and species-level catch time series
   (`country_catch_timeseries.png`, `species_catch_timeseries.png`),
   `unmatched_species_FG_assignment.csv`, `westmed_species_timeseries.csv`.
3. **Species → FG matching pipeline** (inline, Steps 0–4, same safety
   philosophy as Script 1's taxonomy fallback — a query is only
   auto-resolved if it maps to exactly **one** distinct FG, otherwise flagged
   ambiguous and never guessed):
   - Step 1: direct match (catch-record species text literally equals an
     `FG_name`).
   - Step 2: common name → scientific name via `rfishbase::common_names()`
     (queried **for** the known species list, not searched from all of
     FishBase — avoids cross-matching an unrelated species that happens to
     share a common name).
   - Step 3: FAO genus-level fallback for NEI/aggregate categories (e.g.
     "Sardinellas nei" → genus *Sardinella*).
   - Step 4: word-containment fallback (full token containment only).
   - Manual overrides (e.g. "Turbot" → *Scophthalmus maximus*, correcting a
     wrong FishBase common-name entry).
   - A catch-total sanity check confirms no species record is double-counted
     across matching stages.
   - Output: `species_fg_matched.csv` (one row per species, `status` =
     resolved/unresolved, `match_method`).
4. **FG-level Year timeseries** — joins `species_ts` (has Year, no FG) against
   `species_fg_matched.csv`'s Species→FG mapping, re-aggregates to Year × FG.
   Output: `fg_catch_timeseries_<DATASET_VERSION>.csv` (landings-only, **not**
   confirmed to include discards — read by `03_pbqb-traits.R`'s optional
   `FG_YIELD_SOURCE = "fg_catch_csv"` path).
5. **Add `Catches_Ecopath`/`Catches_Ecosim` to the workbook** — reads
   `strata_area_by_area.csv`, sums to a total study-area km², calls
   `add_catches_to_ecopath_workbook()`. **Known, documented limitation:**
   GFCM Divisions 37.1.1–37.1.3 cover GSA 1–11 **plus** GSA 12 (Northern
   Tunisia), which the survey's `FILTER_AREAS` does not include — the catch
   density is therefore a slight overestimate relative to the Biomass area
   (GFCM's Division-resolution reporting can't isolate GSA 12's specific
   contribution to subtract it out).

**Not implemented — no fleet or gear disaggregation anywhere in this
script.** `GFCM_Capture_Quantity.csv` (the actual, confirmed source above)
resolves only to Country × Species × Division × Year — this is GFCM's
STATLANT-derived aggregate capture-production product, not its
Operational-Unit-level data. GFCM *does* collect catch/effort broken down
by **Operational Unit** (fleet segment × ISSCFG gear code × GSA), via the
DCRF's Task 2 (Catch)/Task 4 (Fleet)/Task 5 (Effort) and the Regional
Fleet Register, submitted by national correspondents — but that's a
genuinely different GFCM product from this file, and isn't confirmed to
have the same kind of open bulk-download `GFCM_Capture_Quantity.csv` does
(it feeds GFCM's own stock-assessment working groups rather than being
published as an open dataset the way STATLANT capture-production is).
Getting real fleet/gear resolution into this pipeline means sourcing that
Operational-Unit data through GFCM's DCRF/SAC channels, or — for the
EU-flagged portion of the fleet specifically — STECF's FDI (Fisheries
Dependent Information) database, which is the same source already
flagged elsewhere in this pipeline as the likely next step for a
discard-inclusive catch figure. Neither is currently plugged in anywhere;
`03_pbqb-traits.R`'s `Fmort_species`/`Fmort_FG` (below) inherit this same
limitation, since they're computed downstream of this file.

**Single fleet by default; multi-fleet via an optional `fleet_structure`
input.** `add_catches_to_ecopath_workbook()` (`lib_survey_fg_density_
functions.R`) now takes an optional `fleet_structure` argument: a table of
`FG_num`, `Fleet`, `prop_catch` (the share, 0–1, of each FG's total catch
attributed to each fleet). Left at its default (`NULL`, and that is still
this script's own default), the workbook is exactly what it always was —
one implicit fleet, `Catches_Ecopath`'s combined `Catch_<year>` columns,
`Catches_Ecosim`'s meta-rows with no `Fleet` field, no `Fleet_Structure`
sheet. Point `FLEET_STRUCTURE_PATH` (near the top of this script) at a CSV
in that `FG_num,Fleet,prop_catch` shape to split catches by fleet instead —
any FG *not* listed there still defaults to a single implicit `"Fleet_1"`,
so a partial table (only the FGs you actually have a fleet split for) is
fine. Once any FG resolves to more than one fleet, **every** FG row in
`Catches_Ecopath` switches to fleet-suffixed columns
(`Catch_<year>_<Fleet>`), `Catches_Ecosim` gets one column-set per FG ×
Fleet, and the resolved fleet table itself is written out as a new
`Fleet_Structure` sheet for traceability.

This only splits catch *after the fact* — it does not source fleet/gear
data itself (see the "not implemented" note above: `GFCM_Capture_
Quantity.csv` still carries no fleet/gear column), so the `fleet_structure`
CSV has to come from somewhere else. **Three ways to get one:** (1) GFCM
Operational Units or STECF FDI, assembled by hand; (2) a hand-built
allocation; or (3) point `FLEET_STRUCTURE_PATH` at
`file.path(out_dir, "fleet_structure_from_sau.csv")` — written automatically
by Script 2a (`02a_fisheries_multisource.R`), country × gear proportions
(e.g. `Spain - bottom trawl`, `France - purse seine`) derived from SAU's own
real fleet-level catch shares per FG. This is an **approximation** — it
borrows SAU's gear mix and applies it to this script's own FAO-GFCM totals,
two different catch reconstructions for the same species/FG, not a real
fleet-resolved FAO-GFCM figure, since FAO-GFCM has no gear data of its own
to resolve directly. If the fleet split should come from the *same*
reconstruction as the catch totals, use SAU's own `Catches_by_Fleet` sheet
(written directly by Script 2a, see below) instead of routing through here.
`03_pbqb-traits.R`'s `Fmort_species`/`Fmort_FG` remain unaffected either way —
they're computed from the same un-split `fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv`
regardless of whether `Catches_Ecopath`/`Catches_Ecosim` end up split by
fleet.

**`CATCHES_DATA_SOURCE`: pick which source feeds `Catches_Ecopath`/
`Catches_Ecosim` — without duplicating Script 2a's own logic here.** This
selector (`NULL | "SAU" | "SAU_no_unreported" | "FishMIP"`, near
`FLEET_STRUCTURE_PATH`) just **selects** which of Script 2a's already-written
contract files to read — the same "point this script at 2a's own output"
pattern `03_pbqb-traits.R`'s `FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE` already uses — it does
**not** re-implement any of Script 2a's SAU/FishMIP extraction. **Defaults to
`"SAU"`** (not `NULL`) — discards/unreported/IUU catch and a real fleet ×
gear × country split are treated as always-wanted, not an occasional opt-in.
When `"SAU"`/`"SAU_no_unreported"`, this script reads Script 2a's
`fg_catch_timeseries_<SOURCE>.csv` for the catch **total** *and*
`fleet_structure_from_sau.csv` for the fleet **split** — both from the *same*
SAU reconstruction, so this is fully self-consistent, unlike routing SAU's
fleet shares through `FLEET_STRUCTURE_PATH` onto a FAO-GFCM total (that combo
still works and is unaffected, but `CATCHES_DATA_SOURCE`'s own SAU fleet
structure takes priority over `FLEET_STRUCTURE_PATH` whenever both are set).
Set to `"FishMIP"`, only the total is replaced — FishMIP's catch side has no
gear dimension, so there's no fleet split to auto-apply; `FLEET_STRUCTURE_PATH`
still works normally on top of it if you want one anyway. Set to `NULL`
yourself to restore the original FAO-GFCM-only, landings-only, single-fleet
figure for a specific run. `"FAO_GFCM"` is **not** a valid value (it would
just mean reading back this script's own output) and errors clearly if set.
**This script does not auto-run Script 2a for you** — unlike Script 2a's own
`AUTO_RUN_MISSING_SOURCES`, which auto-runs SAU's download and, separately,
auto-runs *this* script for FAO-GFCM — because Script 2 auto-sourcing Script
2a would risk recursing straight back into Script 2. A missing contract file
is therefore a clear `stop()` naming which script to run first, never a
silent fallback to `NULL`'s FAO-GFCM behavior.

**Why the FAO-GFCM auto-run (Script 2a's own STEP 7) is safe from this new
default:** since `CATCHES_DATA_SOURCE` now defaults to `"SAU"`, and Script
2a's SAU step (STEP 5) runs *before* its FAO-GFCM step (STEP 7) in the same
run, `fg_catch_timeseries_SAU.csv` already exists by the time STEP 7
auto-sources this script — so STEP 7 explicitly sets a global
`CATCHES_DATA_SOURCE <- NULL` right before sourcing it, overriding this
script's own default (via the same "reuse if the caller already set it"
pattern `DATASET_VERSION` uses). Without that override, the auto-run would
silently hand back SAU's total mislabeled as the FAO-GFCM figure that
`DataSources_Catch`/`FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE = "FAO_GFCM"` depend on being
genuinely FAO-GFCM-only.

## Outputs

* **CSVs:** `unmatched_species_FG_assignment.csv`,
  `westmed_species_timeseries.csv`, `species_fg_matched.csv`,
  `fg_catch_timeseries_<DATASET_VERSION>.csv`.
* **Plots:** `country_catch_timeseries.png`, `species_catch_timeseries.png`.
* **Workbook** `ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx`, sheets written: `Catches_Ecopath`,
  `Catches_Ecosim`, plus `Fleet_Structure` if `FLEET_STRUCTURE_PATH` was set
  and resolved to more than one fleet.

---

# Script 2a — `02a_fisheries_multisource.R` (optional, runs alongside Script 2)

**Role:** Optional additional Step 2 — **requires** Step 1 to have already
run (reads `strata_area_by_area.csv` and the FG reference, same contract as
Script 2/Script 4). Adds **three genuinely different, already-assembled**
catch/effort sources on top of Script 2's own FAO-GFCM source, so all four
can be compared side by side and one of them selected to drive Fishing
mortality (F) and PB = M + F downstream in Script 4 — **without changing
Script 4's own F/PB math at all**, only the input file it reads.

## Why four sources, not one

None of the four is a strict upgrade over the others — each has real gaps of
its own, which is why every one gets written into the workbook
(`DataSources_Catch`) rather than silently picked for the user:

* **SAU (Sea Around Us)** — reconstructed catch (adds back
  unreported/discarded/IUU catch on top of official landings), resolved to
  **country × gear × species × year**. The only source here with a real gear
  dimension, so `Catches_by_Fleet` (below) is built from SAU alone. Yield =
  SAU's own reconstructed `tonnes` (already landings + discards +
  unreported/IUU, by SAU's own definition).
* **SAU w/o unreported** — the same SAU extract, filtered to
  `reporting_status == "Reported"`. This is SAU's own reporting-status
  split, not a clean "landings + discards" figure — kept as its own labeled
  source rather than mislabeled.
* **FishMIP** — a **SAU-derived calibration product** (not independent of
  SAU), resolved to country × sector × functional group × year, with
  reported/IUU/discards already split out. No species dimension and no gear
  dimension on the catch side (gear only exists on FishMIP's separate
  *effort* file — see `Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet` below). Yield = `reported +
  discards` (IUU deliberately excluded, kept as an audit column
  `tonnes_incl_iuu`, per the "landings + discards" definition used
  throughout this script). FishMIP's own functional groups are **not** this
  pipeline's FG scheme — see `FISHMIP_FG_CROSSWALK_PATH` below.
* **FAO-GFCM** — this pipeline's own existing source (Script 2's output),
  passed through unchanged. Landings only (no discards), Division-resolution
  (not GSA), no fleet/gear dimension — same caveats as Script 2 itself.

**Area scope caveat** (same pattern as the GSA-12/Division mismatch already
flagged for Script 2): SAU and FishMIP resolve to **country/EEZ**, not GSA.
Restricting either one to "the same area as the survey" means restricting to
the *countries* whose EEZs overlap that GSA range — an approximation, not an
exact area match. A country with only a small coastal sliver inside the
modeled GSA range still contributes its entire national catch/effort. This
is unavoidable at these sources' native resolution and is reported
explicitly at runtime (`TARGET_COUNTRIES_NOTE`), not silently absorbed.

## Inputs

| File | Format | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `strata_area_by_area.csv` | csv | From Step 1 |
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/FG_WMed.xlsx`, sheet 4 | xlsx | Species → FG reference, same as Scripts 1/2 |
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/fisheries/sau_raw_combined_west_med.csv` | csv | SAU raw extract (from `sau_west_med_full.R`/`sau_west_med_analysis.Rmd`) — country × gear × species × year, `reporting_status` column |
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/fisheries/effort_histsoc_*.parquet` | parquet | FishMIP effort — `saup`, `sector`, `gear`, `f_group`, `nom_active` (kW·days) |
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/fisheries/calibration_catch_histsoc_*.parquet` | parquet | FishMIP catch — `saup`, `sector`, `f_group`, `reported`, `iuu`, `discards` |
| `fg_catch_timeseries_<FAO_GFCM_DATASET_VERSION>.csv` | csv | Script 2's own output, read unchanged |
| `FISHMIP_FG_CROSSWALK_PATH` (optional) | csv | User-supplied `fishmip_f_group` → `FG_num` crosswalk — without it, FishMIP is informational-only |

Each source **degrades gracefully** if its input is missing — a clear
console message explains what to run first, the script does not hard-fail.

## Processing steps

1. Configuration — `TARGET_ISO3` (the 6-country Western Med default: Spain,
   France, Italy, Tunisia, Algeria, Morocco), `START_YEAR`/`END_YEAR`
   (1994–2019, matching SAU's public reconstruction cutoff), `N_TOP_GEARS`
   (top 8 gears + "Other gear" bucketing, same convention as the attached
   SAU/FishMIP analysis scripts, to avoid a combinatorial explosion of
   near-empty FG × Fleet × Year rows).
2. `DATA_SOURCE <- "SAU" | "SAU_no_unreported" | "FishMIP" | "FAO_GFCM"` —
   which source drives Script 4's Yield/Fmort/PB = M+F chain. Does **not**
   change this script's own output — every available source is still
   written to `DataSources_Catch`/`Catches_by_Fleet`/`Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet`
   regardless.
2b. `AUTO_RUN_MISSING_SOURCES <- TRUE` (default) — when SAU's raw extract or
   Script 2's `fg_catch_timeseries_<FAO_GFCM_DATASET_VERSION>.csv` is missing,
   this script now **runs the upstream step itself** instead of just telling
   you to go run something else by hand: SAU auto-downloads
   `sau_raw_combined_west_med.csv` straight from SAU's API (per-EEZ, via the
   new `lib_sau_extraction.R` — the same extraction logic as
   `sau_west_med_analysis.Rmd`'s own "Part 0" chunk, lifted out so it doesn't
   require knitting that Rmd by hand every time), and FAO-GFCM auto-sources
   Script 2 (`02_fao_catches.R`) itself, first setting a global
   `DATASET_VERSION` to match this script's own `FAO_GFCM_DATASET_VERSION` so
   the auto-run can't silently regenerate the *wrong* dataset version's file.
   Both attempts are wrapped so a failure (offline, upstream file genuinely
   missing, etc.) degrades to the same "not found, skipping" message as
   before — auto-run is a shortcut, not a new failure mode. **FishMIP is
   deliberately excluded** — its two parquet files are a manual download
   from the FishMIP Input Explorer website, not something any R script here
   can generate, so a missing FishMIP file always just skips with a message
   pointing at that download page. Set `AUTO_RUN_MISSING_SOURCES <- FALSE`
   to go back to the old "message + skip" behavior for every source.
3. Species → FG matching — same `match_species_to_fg()` →
   `match_nominate_subspecies_fg()` → `fallback_match_fg_by_taxonomy()`
   cascade Script 1/Script 2 use, so a species is matched (or left
   unresolved) consistently across the whole pipeline.
4. SAU — year/country filter, species→FG match, builds country-year totals
   (both "SAU total" and "SAU w/o unreported"), FG-year totals, species-year
   totals (for `landings_by_species_gsa_year_<SOURCE>.csv`), and two
   fleet-level (country × top-N-gears, "Other gear" bucket) sheets:
   `Catches_by_Fleet` — **wide**, one row per FG, **one column per Fleet**
   (e.g. `Spain - bottom trawl`, `France - longline`, ...), value = that
   FG × Fleet's catch (t) **averaged over `YEAR_ECOPATH`** (a config
   constant in this script that must match Step 1's own `YEAR_ECOPATH`) —
   same single-snapshot convention `add_catches_to_ecopath_workbook()`
   already uses for `Catches_Ecopath`, but built directly from SAU's real
   per-fleet annual values rather than a static proportion split, so each
   fleet's actual year-to-year share is reflected; and
   `Catches_by_Fleet_AllYears` — the same data in **long** form (one row per
   FG × Fleet × Year), kept for anyone who needs the full annual detail the
   wide sheet collapses out of. Both already include discards (+
   unreported + IUU) — see "Why four sources, not one" above; there is no
   separate landings-only vs. discards-only split at fleet resolution,
   only SAU's already-combined total.
5. FishMIP (if the `arrow` package and both parquet files are available) —
   builds `Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet` (the **only genuine effort data** in this
   whole pipeline — SAU has no effort variable at all) and country-level
   catch totals; FG-level totals only if `FISHMIP_FG_CROSSWALK_PATH` is
   supplied.
6. FAO-GFCM — reads Script 2's own `fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv` unchanged.
7. `DataSources_Catch` — every available source's country-level and FG-level
   Yield, stacked with a `source` column, for side-by-side comparison
   regardless of which one is selected as `DATA_SOURCE`.
8. Writes `Catches_by_Fleet`, `Catches_by_Fleet_AllYears`,
   `Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet`, `DataSources_Catch` into the shared workbook
   via `upsert_workbook_sheets()` (idempotent — re-running replaces these
   sheets, doesn't duplicate them).
9. Writes `fg_catch_timeseries_<DATA_SOURCE>.csv` (and, for
   SAU/SAU_no_unreported only, `landings_by_species_gsa_year_<DATA_SOURCE>.csv`)
   — the contract files Script 4's `FG_YIELD_SOURCE`/`YIELD_SOURCE` mechanism
   reads. **Wiring into Script 4:** Script 4 has its own
   `FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE` selector (see Script 4 below) which, when set to
   match this script's `DATA_SOURCE`, automatically points
   `FG_CATCH_CSV_PATH`/`LANDINGS_CSV_PATH` at these files — no manual path
   editing needed in Script 4 as long as the two `DATA_SOURCE`/
   `FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE` values match.

## Outputs

* **CSVs:** `fg_catch_timeseries_<DATA_SOURCE>.csv`, and (SAU/SAU_no_unreported
  only) `landings_by_species_gsa_year_<DATA_SOURCE>.csv`.
* **Workbook** `ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx`, sheets written: `Catches_by_Fleet`
  (wide — FG rows × Fleet columns, averaged over `YEAR_ECOPATH`) and
  `Catches_by_Fleet_AllYears` (the same data, long, one row per FG × Fleet ×
  Year) — both SAU only, the only source with real gear × country × species
  resolution; `Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet` (FishMIP only — genuinely different
  fleet resolution than `Catches_by_Fleet`, never to be joined directly to
  it), `DataSources_Catch` (every available source, country- and FG-level,
  side by side).

---

# Script 3 — `03b_ecobase.R`

**Role:** Pipeline Step 3 of 4. Independent of Steps 1/2 — can run anytime
before Step 4. **Optional** input to Step 4 for literature-based PB/QB
gap-filling and comparison against the empirical formula estimates.

Queries **EcoBase** (ecobase.ecopath.org), an open-access repository of
published Ecopath models, via its SOAP/XML web service — the official
EcoBase R example ported from RCurl/XML to httr/xml2. The script author
notes this was **not tested against the live service** from within the
authoring sandbox (no network access to `sirs.agrocampus-ouest.fr`) — the
diagnostics throughout are there specifically to surface an endpoint change,
not just fail silently.

**Fixed: `merge(mediterranean_models, all_status, ...)` — "x has duplicated
column name [comments_objectives]".** Root cause traced to
`xml_node_to_dt_row()` (formerly an inline anonymous function, at two call
sites — the model-list parse and, in `fetch_model_inputs()`, the per-model
group parse): when a single `<model>` (or `<group>`) XML node repeats a
child tag — confirmed EcoBase's own XML does this, e.g. a duplicated
`<comments_objectives>` — `setNames(as.list(xml_text(fields)),
xml_name(fields))` produced a list with two entries sharing one name, and
`as.data.table()` on that silently creates a data.table with two
same-named columns. data.table allows that at creation time; it only
rejects it the moment something needs unique names — which is why the
error surfaced three functions and one `rbindlist()` away from where the
duplicate actually originated, at the `merge()` call. Fix: both parse
sites now go through one shared `xml_node_to_dt_row()` that detects a
repeated tag name and collapses its values into ONE column, joined with
`"; "` — nothing is dropped, there's just no longer two columns fighting
over one name.

## Inputs (network)

| Endpoint | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `sirs.agrocampus-ouest.fr/EcoBase/php/webser/soap-client_3.php` | Full model list |
| `sirs.agrocampus-ouest.fr/EcoBase/php/webser/soap-client.php?no_model=<id>` | Per-model input values (Biomass/PB/QB per group) |

`pcloud_dir`/`git_dir` are resolved for consistency with the other scripts
but not actually used — this script is self-contained.

## Processing steps

1. Fetch the full EcoBase model list; save `ecobase_model_list_full.csv`.
2. Filter geographically to the Mediterranean, using each model's
   `geographic_extent` bounding box (not text/keyword matching on
   country/ecosystem names — more robust). Save
   `ecobase_mediterranean_models.csv`.
3. Filter further to `dissemination_allow == "true"` — EcoBase's own flag for
   which models actually have downloadable input data (per the site, only
   ~233 of ~500 listed models do; the rest are metadata/reference-only).
   `KNOWN_RELEVANT_MODEL_IDS` is the resulting query list.
4. Fetch per-model group-level Biomass/PB/QB for each disseminable model
   (`fetch_model_inputs()`), explicitly detecting the "data not available
   despite the flag" case (confirmed against a real response for model 418).
   `-9999` (Ecopath's own sentinel for "not provided") is converted to `NA`.
5. Save a full status audit (`ecobase_mediterranean_models_status.csv`) and
   the failure-only subset (`ecobase_models_without_data.csv`).
6. Compile the final summary table: `FG_name, PB, QB, EwE_model, year,
   authors` (model metadata columns matched by best-guess candidate names
   against `model_list`, since EcoBase's exact field names aren't confirmed).

## Outputs

* `ecobase_model_list_full.csv`, `ecobase_mediterranean_models.csv`,
  `ecobase_mediterranean_models_status.csv`, `ecobase_models_without_data.csv`,
  `ecobase_literature_pb_qb_raw.csv`,
  **`ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv`** (the file `03_pbqb-traits.R` reads).
* No workbook writes from this script directly — `03_pbqb-traits.R` is what
  merges this CSV into the workbook's `Ecobase` sheet.

### Column reference — `ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv`

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `FG_name` | EcoBase group name (matched to your own `FG_name` by text in `03_pbqb-traits.R` — group naming won't line up automatically across different published models) |
| `PB` | Literature Production/Biomass (`-9999`, EcoBase's own "not provided" sentinel, converted to `NA`) |
| `QB` | Literature Consumption/Biomass (same `-9999`→`NA` handling) |
| `EwE_model` | Source model name |
| `year` | Source model's reference year |
| `authors` | Source model's authors |

---

# Script 4 — `03_pbqb-traits.R`

**Role:** Pipeline Step 4 of 4 — run **last**. Requires Step 1's
`species_density_regional_combined.csv`. Optionally uses Step 2's
`fg_catch_timeseries_<DATASET_VERSION>.csv` (`FG_YIELD_SOURCE` toggle) and
Step 3's `ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv` — both degrade gracefully with
a clear message if missing, they don't hard-fail this script.

Estimates Production/Biomass (PB) and Consumption/Biomass (QB) for every EwE
Functional Group, following the taxon-specific methods in *"Quick guide on
how to calculate P/B and Q/B for EwE models"* (Vilas, Coll, Piroddi,
Steenbeek). Growth parameters, length-weight a/b, maturity, body weight,
longevity, trophic level, temperature, depth and aspect ratio are all fetched
automatically from FishBase + SeaLifeBase (`rfishbase` ≥ 4.0.0 required — the
script hard-stops with install instructions if an old, broken pre-4.0 CRAN
build, or a `duckdbfs` install missing `duckdb_config`, is detected).

## Inputs

| File | Format | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `species_density_regional_combined.csv` | csv | **From Step 1.** Reshaped via `lib_build_species_df_from_survey.R::build_species_df_from_survey()` into `species_df` (Species, FG, Biomass density, Yield=NA) |
| `fg_catch_timeseries_GFCM_2025.csv` (or `fg_catch_timeseries_<FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE>.csv`) | csv (optional) | **From Step 2, or Step 2a if `FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE` is set.** FG-level catch, used to compute a per-FG fishing-mortality rate `Fmort_FG` (`FG_YIELD_SOURCE = "fg_catch_csv"`) |
| `strata_area_by_area.csv` | csv | **From Step 1.** Study area, needed to convert FG catch totals into a density comparable to Biomass |
| `ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv` | csv (optional) | **From Step 3.** Literature PB/QB for gap-filling/comparison |
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/FG_WMed.xlsx`, sheet `fg_wmed_95` | xlsx | FG reference (loaded by explicit sheet **name**, since the workbook also has an unrelated `fg_ebro delta_21` sheet) |
| `landings_by_species_gsa_year.csv` (or `landings_by_species_gsa_year_<FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE>.csv`) | csv (optional; placeholder path when `FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE` is `NULL`) | Species-level landings, for a species-level Fmort path (`YIELD_SOURCE = "landings_csv"`) — real data when Step 2a's `FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE` is set to `"SAU"`/`"SAU_no_unreported"` |
| FishBase / SeaLifeBase (`rfishbase`) | web API | Growth, length-weight, maturity, ecology/trophic level, swimming/aspect-ratio traits |
| WoRMS (`worrms`, via `lib_worms_taxonomy_lookup.R`) | web API | Taxonomic classification (Class → dispatch group: fish/mammal/seabird/phytoplankton/invertebrate) |
| FishLife R package | local DB (`FishBase_and_RAM$ParentChild_gz`) | Phylogenetically-imputed Loo/K/M/Winf, walking up the taxonomic tree when a direct match is missing |
| TropFishR R package | local | `M_empirical()` — Hoenig (1983), Then et al. (2015), Alverson & Carney (1975) natural-mortality estimators |

### Column reference — key input files

**`species_density_regional_combined.csv`** (from Step 1 — required columns,
enforced by `build_species_df_from_survey()`):

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Year` | Survey year |
| `FG_num`, `FG_name` | Functional Group id/name |
| `ScientificName` | Species scientific name |
| `mean_density` | Species density, t/km² |

**`fg_catch_timeseries_GFCM_2025.csv`** (from Step 2, optional):

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Year` | Catch year |
| `FG_num`, `FG_name` | Functional Group id/name |
| `Catch_t` | Total landed catch, tonnes (region-wide, landings-only) |

**`strata_area_by_area.csv`** (from Step 1):

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `AreaID` | GSA number (or `1` for a custom-region run) |
| `Stratum` | Depth-stratum number |
| `area_km2` | Stratum area within that GSA/region, km² |
| `prop` | That stratum's proportion of the area's total |

**`FG_WMed.xlsx`, sheet `fg_wmed_95`** — loaded by explicit sheet **name**
(not position), since the same workbook also has an unrelated
`fg_ebro delta_21` sheet. `readxl` disambiguates duplicate-looking headers
as `FG_name...2`/`FG_name...7` — handled with a flagged best-guess in the
code rather than assumed.

**`landings_by_species_gsa_year.csv`** (placeholder path — not yet a real
data source; schema below is what `attach_yield_from_landings()` expects if
this is ever wired up):

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `ScientificName` | Species scientific name |
| `Year` | Landings year |
| `AreaID` | GSA number |
| `catch_t` | Landed catch, tonnes |

```{r pbqb-comparison-fig, echo=FALSE, eval=TRUE, results="asis", fig.cap="fish_PB_methods_comparison.png — all 6 fish PB/M methods per species, chosen method highlighted."}
safe_include_graphics(
  "figures/fish_PB_methods_comparison.png",
  script = "03_pbqb-traits.R",
  out_dir_note = "<out_dir>/plots/fish_PB_methods_comparison.png"
)
```

## Processing steps

1. **Setup** — package loading, `rfishbase`/`duckdbfs` version guard, GUI or
   console progress tracker across 13 named pipeline stages.
2. **Load `species_df`** (`SPECIES_DF_SOURCE = "survey"`, the real path) via
   `lib_build_species_df_from_survey.R`.
3. **Fishing mortality attachment** — two independent attachment points,
   both driven by a single **`FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE`** selector (set near
   `out_dir`/`pcloud_dir`/`git_dir` at the top of this script):
   `NULL | "SAU" | "SAU_no_unreported" | "FishMIP" | "FAO_GFCM"`, matching
   Script 2a's own `DATA_SOURCE` values one-to-one. When set, it
   automatically points `LANDINGS_CSV_PATH`/`FG_CATCH_CSV_PATH` at the
   `_<FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE>.csv` files Script 2a writes, and flips
   `YIELD_SOURCE`/`FG_YIELD_SOURCE` on to match — no manual path editing
   needed as long as this matches Script 2a's `DATA_SOURCE` for the run that
   produced those files. **Defaults to `"SAU"`** (not `NULL`) — species-level
   resolution and discards/unreported/IUU catch are treated as always-wanted
   for this chain, matching `02_fao_catches.R`'s own `CATCHES_DATA_SOURCE`
   default (also `"SAU"`, same reasoning). Set to `NULL` yourself to restore
   the original hardcoded placeholder behavior from before Script 2a
   existed. Every source's own catch/effort still appears in the
   workbook's `DataSources_Catch`/`Catches_by_Fleet`/`Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet`
   sheets (written by Script 2a) regardless of this setting — only *this*
   script's own Fmort/PB = M+F chain follows whichever source is selected
   here.
   * (A) species-level, via `attach_yield_from_landings()` — active only for
     `FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE %in% c("SAU", "SAU_no_unreported")` (the only two
     sources with species-level resolution); inactive (`YIELD_SOURCE =
     "none"`) for `NULL`/`"FishMIP"`/`"FAO_GFCM"`.
   * (B) FG-level, via `fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv` — active
     (`FG_YIELD_SOURCE = "fg_catch_csv"`) for any non-`NULL`
     `FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE` — computed as `Yield_FG / Biomass_FG` per FG, applied to fish
     PB inside `calc_fish()` as a **fallback** when species-level Yield is
     absent (species-level always takes priority when both exist).
     **Known, unresolved caveat (same root cause as `Catches_Ecopath`/
     `Catches_Ecosim` below):** `Yield_FG`'s denominator (`area_total_km2`,
     summed from `strata_area_by_area.csv`) is the *survey's* GSA-filtered
     area, while its numerator (`fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv`'s `Catch_t`) is
     filtered by GFCM Division, which for the Western Med example covers
     GSA 1–11 **plus** GSA 12 — so `Yield_FG`, and therefore `Fmort_FG` and
     the fish PB it feeds, is a **slight overestimate** wherever this path
     is enabled. Not fixable from this side (GFCM's catch data can't be
     split below Division resolution); see the same caveat under
     [Script 2 — `02_fao_catches.R`] for the full explanation.
     **Also not implemented — no fleet or gear disaggregation:** both
     `Fmort_species` (path A, inactive) and `Fmort_FG` (path B) are a
     single Yield/Biomass ratio lumped across the whole region and every
     gear/fleet that landed that species/FG — neither the
     `landings_by_species_gsa_year.csv` placeholder schema nor
     `fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv` carries a fleet or gear column, so
     there's nothing here that could produce a fleet- or gear-specific F
     even once a real landings file is plugged in. See the matching note
     under [Script 2 — `02_fao_catches.R`] for what a real fix would
     require (GFCM's Operational-Unit data or STECF's FDI database).
     **`Fmort` itself stays single-fleet even where `Catches_` sheets are
     split:** unlike `Catches_Ecopath`/`Catches_Ecosim` (which can now be
     split by fleet via `fleet_structure` — see the "Single fleet by
     default; multi-fleet via an optional `fleet_structure` input" note
     under [Script 2 — `02_fao_catches.R`]), this script never sees
     `fleet_structure` and computes `Fmort_species`/`Fmort_FG` from the
     same un-split `fg_catch_timeseries_*.csv` regardless. A genuinely
     fleet-specific F would need Yield split by fleet *before* this ratio,
     not just the `Catches_` sheets split after the fact.
4. **Taxonomic classification** — `worms_taxonomy_lookup()` for every species
   in `species_df`, dispatch-classified into `fish` / `mammal` / `seabird` /
   `phytoplankton` / `invertebrate` (default) by WoRMS Class, with a
   synonym→accepted-name resolution fallback for species WoRMS returns no
   Class for.
5. **Trait fetching (Step 2a–2f)** — `select_best_rows()` picks the best
   population-specific study per species from FishBase/SeaLifeBase
   multi-record tables, tiered: Western-Med locality keywords → broader
   Mediterranean keywords → most recent year → first available.
   `extract_provenance()` captures Locality/Year/Reference citation for each
   selected row (used later for the `References` sheet/CSV).
6. **Gap-filling** — `fill_by_taxonomic_proximity()`: local within-dataset
   donors first (Genus→Family→Order→Class, restricted to directly-computed
   values), then, for methods in `EXTERNAL_FORMULA_REGISTRY` (fish/mammal/
   general Gascuel formulas plus raw-trait identity mappings), an **external**
   FishBase/SeaLifeBase donor lookup — querying other species sharing the
   same taxonomic rank, not limited to species already in `species_df`.
7. **Taxon-specific PB/QB calculation** (dispatch by group) — the diagram
   below shows the actual dispatch and method-cascade logic:

   ```{mermaid}
   %%| fig-cap: "PB/QB taxon dispatch and gap-fill cascade - which calc_*() function runs per taxonomic Class, and how results are aggregated to FG-level and gap-filled from EcoBase."
   %%{init: {'flowchart': {'padding': 20, 'nodeSpacing': 60, 'rankSpacing': 90}, 'themeVariables': {'fontSize': '18px'}}}%%
   flowchart LR
       T["worms_taxonomy_lookup()&lt;br/&gt;WoRMS Class"] --> F["calc_fish()&lt;br/&gt;PB: 6 M methods + Fmort&lt;br/&gt;QB: 4 methods"]
       T --> M["calc_mammal()&lt;br/&gt;PB: Siler surv. to Gascuel&lt;br/&gt;QB: Innes&amp;Trites to Q/P=3"]
       T --> S["calc_seabird()&lt;br/&gt;PB: Gascuel only&lt;br/&gt;QB: Nilsson-Nilsson (no Q/P=3)"]
       T --> I["calc_invertebrate() (default)&lt;br/&gt;PB: Tumbiolo/Brey to Gascuel&lt;br/&gt;QB: Q/P=3 only"]
       F --> D["fill_by_taxonomic_proximity()&lt;br/&gt;local then external donors"]
       M --> D
       S --> D
       I --> D
       D --> AGG["FG-level aggregation&lt;br/&gt;biomass-weighted mean"]
       AGG --> OUT["Ecobase gap-fill -&gt; Ecopath_PBQB&lt;br/&gt;(PB_QB/PB_QB_spp/Ecobase CSVs)"]

       classDef gray fill:#F1EFE8,stroke:#5F5E5A,color:#2C2C2A
       classDef purple fill:#EEEDFE,stroke:#534AB7,color:#26215C
       classDef teal fill:#E1F5EE,stroke:#0F6E56,color:#04342C
       classDef coral fill:#FAECE7,stroke:#993C1D,color:#4A1B0C
       class T gray
       class F,M,S,I purple
       class D teal
       class AGG,OUT coral
   ```

   *Phytoplankton is flagged by `worms_taxonomy_lookup()` but not computed
   (needs separate production-sampling data, out of scope for this
   pipeline) — not shown above.*

   * `calc_fish()` — PB from 6 independent M methods (Pauly 1980, FishLife,
     Gascuel 2008, Hoenig 1983, Then et al. 2015, Alverson & Carney 1975) +
     Fmort; QB from 4 methods (Palomares & Pauly 1998 Eq.27/Eq.26, Christensen
     & Pauly 1992, Q/P=3 fallback). Chosen value via priority order or mean
     (`PB_QB_SELECTION_MODE`).
   * `calc_mammal()` — PB via Siler-survivorship (Barlow & Boveng 1991),
     fallback Gascuel (2008); QB via Innes & Trites (1997), fallback Q/P=3.
   * `calc_seabird()` — PB via Gascuel (2008) only; QB via Nilsson & Nilsson
     (1976), **no** Q/P=3 fallback (that heuristic is fish/ectotherm-derived
     and would badly underestimate endotherm QB).
   * `calc_invertebrate()` — PB via Tumbiolo & Downing (1994), Brey (1999),
     fallback Gascuel (2008); QB via Q/P=3 only (Brey's 2012 ANN model
     flagged as non-automatable — top-10 highest-biomass invertebrates
     exported instead for manual cross-check).
   * Phytoplankton — flagged, not computed (needs separate production-
     sampling data, out of scope for this pipeline).
8. **FG-level aggregation** — biomass-weighted mean PB/QB per FG, with
   coverage/count audit columns (`n_species_with_PB`, `biomass_coverage_PB`,
   etc.).
9. **EcoBase literature merge** (if `ecobase_literature_pb_qb_simple.csv`
   exists) — averages multiple matching literature models per `FG_name`,
   flags >2×/<0.5× divergence from the empirical estimate, gap-fills only
   where the empirical value is `NA`. Writes sheet **`Ecobase`** (one row per
   FG, averaged PB/QB across every matching model, plus a `References`
   column listing each contributing model) **and `Ecobase_by_Model`** (one
   row per FG × individual published model — `FG_num`, `FG_name`,
   `EwE_model`, `year`, `authors`, `PB`, `QB` — the un-averaged detail behind
   `Ecobase`'s own per-FG mean, since a single FG typically has several
   different published models backing it and the averaged sheet alone loses
   which model said what).
10. **FG-level F top-up** (if `FG_YIELD_SOURCE = "fg_catch_csv"`) — applied
    only to FGs with no species-level F already baked in (mainly mammal/
    seabird/invertebrate FGs), to avoid double-counting against fish's own
    species-level Fmort.
11. **Workbook write** — `add_pbqb_to_ecopath_workbook()` writes sheet
    **`PB_QB`** (one row per FG — the biomass-weighted rollup), and, since
    `results` (the species-level table) is passed in as its
    `species_pb_qb` argument, also writes sheet **`PB_QB_spp`** (one row
    per species, mirroring `FG_spp_Ecopath`'s shape — every species that
    went into computing `PB_FG`/`QB_FG`, with three proportion columns:
    `prop_biomass_FG` — share of the FG's total biomass, regardless of
    whether that species got a PB/QB; `prop_biomass_PB`/`prop_biomass_QB`
    — that species' actual weight inside `PB_FG`'s/`QB_FG`'s own
    biomass-weighted mean, `NA` if that species' own PB/QB didn't
    compute. This is the sheet to check *which* species and *how much*
    of each FG's biomass a given `PB_FG`/`QB_FG` number was built from —
    without it, that detail only existed outside the workbook, in
    `species_pb_qb_by_taxon_group.csv`). If `01_survey_density_*.R` already
    wrote a `PB_QB_spp` **scaffold** into this workbook (Species/FG_num/
    Biomass/`prop_biomass_FG` only, no real PB/QB yet), this call replaces
    it wholesale with the full sheet — `upsert_workbook_sheets()` always
    fully overwrites a same-named sheet, so no separate merge step is
    needed. A separate `upsert_workbook_sheets()`
    call writes sheet **`References`** (later renamed `PB_QB_References_`
    by `finalize_workbook_sheet_order()`, run from Script 1).
12. **Diagnostic plots** — per-method PB/QB comparison plots for fish, plus
    paginated species-level and FG-level PB/QB comparison plots across every
    dispatch group (auto-paginated when a plot would exceed `ggsave`'s
    50-inch height limit).
13. **Ecopath-ready CSV export** — `ecopath_ready_PB_QB.csv`, formatted to
    match a real Ecopath Basic Input CSV's exact column layout and
    European comma-decimal number formatting (verified against a real
    exported file, `westernmed90s-Basic_input.csv`).

## Outputs

* **CSVs (2026-09-17: all under `output/pbqb-traits/`, not the top-level
  `output/`):** `invertebrates_for_brey_manual_check.csv`,
  `species_pb_qb_by_taxon_group.csv`, `fg_pb_qb_weighted.csv`,
  `phytoplankton_needs_separate_method.csv`,
  `fg_pb_qb_weighted_with_ecobase.csv`, `fg_pb_qb_weighted_with_F.csv`,
  `species_parameter_references.csv` (species-level parameter provenance —
  not the same table as the workbook's `FG_References` sheet, see below),
  `ecopath_ready_PB_QB.csv`, native `PB_QB.csv`/`PB_QB_spp.csv`.
* **Plots:** `fish_PB_methods_comparison.png`,
  `fish_QB_methods_comparison.png`,
  `species_PB_QB_comparison[_pageN].png`, `FG_PB_QB_comparison[_pageN].png`.
* **Workbook** `ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx`, final sheets written:
  `Ecopath_PBQB`, `Ecopath_traits`. (`PB_QB`/`PB_QB_spp`/`Ecobase` are
  native/intermediate CSVs only now, not workbook sheets — see "Excel
  workbook functions" in the shared-library section below.)

# Shared library — `lib_survey_fg_density_functions.R`

Sourced by all four numbered scripts; has **no top-level driver code** of its
own — every symbol below is a function definition only.

## FG-matching functions

Every observed species runs through this cascade, in order, until it
resolves to a Functional Group or falls through to manual review — the
diagram shows the actual fallback order implemented in the code below
(same style as `medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd`'s `@sec-fg-matching`
diagram; each stage only attempts species the previous one left
unmatched).

```{mermaid}
%%| fig-cap: "FG matching detail (see @sec-fg-matching in medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd) - each stage only attempts species the previous one left unmatched; species still unmatched after seed rules go to manual review."
%%{init: {'flowchart': {'padding': 20, 'nodeSpacing': 60, 'rankSpacing': 90}, 'themeVariables': {'fontSize': '18px'}}}%%
flowchart LR
    A1["match_species_to_fg()&lt;br/&gt;direct ScientificName match"] --> A2["match_nominate_subspecies_fg()&lt;br/&gt;strip repeated trinomial word, retry"]
    A2 --> A3["apply_seed_fg_rules()&lt;br/&gt;manual rules, zero-reference FGs"]
    A3 --> A4["fallback_match_fg_by_taxonomy()&lt;br/&gt;Genus to Phylum, exactly-one-FG rule"]
    A4 --> A5["summarize_unresolved_species()&lt;br/&gt;manual review"]

    classDef purple fill:#EEEDFE,stroke:#534AB7,color:#26215C
    classDef gray fill:#F1EFE8,stroke:#5F5E5A,color:#2C2C2A
    class A1,A2,A3,A4 purple
    class A5 gray
```

| Function | Purpose | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| `validate_survey_data(dataframe1, dataframe2, strata = TRUE)` | Fail-fast schema/uniqueness check on the two standardized input tables (required columns present, `SampleID` unique per survey) | `invisible(TRUE)` |
| `prepare_fg_lookup(dataframe2)` | Deduplicates species→FG reference; keeps genuine stanza splits (a species exclusively mapped to ≥2 FGs), excludes/flags ambiguous multi-FG species | deduped `data.table(ScientificName, FG_num, FG_name)` |
| `match_species_to_fg(dataframe1, fg_lookup_safe)` | Direct left-merge by `ScientificName` | merged `dt` |
| `match_nominate_subspecies_fg(dt, fg_lookup_safe)` | Strips a repeated trinomial word (e.g. "Diplodus sargus sargus" → "Diplodus sargus") and retries the match | `dt` with newly resolved rows |
| `fetch_taxonomy(species_names, taxonomy_source = "worms")` | Dispatches to `fetch_taxonomy_worms()`, `fetch_taxonomy_fishbase()`, or both | `data.table(ScientificName, Genus, Family, Order, Class, Phylum)` |
| `fallback_match_fg_by_taxonomy(dt, fg_lookup_safe, taxonomy_source = "worms", rank_levels = c("Genus","Family","Order","Class","Phylum"))` | Data-driven rank fallback — a rank value is used only if it maps to exactly one FG among reference species | `dt`, with `attr(dt,"fetched_taxonomy")` and `attr(dt,"still_unresolved_taxonomy")` attached |
| `apply_seed_fg_rules(dt, dataframe2, seed_rules, species_exceptions = NULL)` | Explicit small rule table for FGs with **zero** reference species (nothing for the automatic fallback to learn from); species-level exceptions applied first | `dt` |
| `summarize_unresolved_species(dt, taxonomy = NULL)` | Mean biomass + appearance count per still-unmatched species, sorted by appearance count | summary `data.table` |

## Area / stratum / density functions

| Function | Purpose | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| `match_samples_to_area(dt, area_shp, area_id_col)` | Spatial join (`sf::st_join`) of sample points to polygons; no-op if `AreaID` already fully populated | `dt` |
| `assign_depth_stratum(dt, strata_def)` | Bins `Depth` into strata via `findInterval()`; out-of-range depths → `Stratum = NA` | `dt` |
| `filter_samples_by_area(dt, area_filter, filter_type = c("shapefile","bbox"), points_crs = 4326)` | Custom sub-region filter, independent of GSA/`AreaID` | `dt` filtered to in-area `SampleID`s |
| `compute_sample_densities(dt)` | `Density := Biomass / Effort` | `dt` |
| `apply_catchability_correction(dt, catchability_table, default_q = 1, species_taxonomy = NULL, exempt_fg_names = NULL, exempt_species = NULL)` | 5-tier resolution: direct species → genus "spp" entries → explicit broad-rank entries → taxonomic-proximity fallback → `default_q`; exemptions override last. Divides `Density`/`Biomass` by resolved `q` | `dt` |
| `remove_sample_outliers(dt, threshold = 70, min_samples = 5, drop_outliers = TRUE, log_scale = TRUE)` | Per-species/per-area robust z-score (median/MAD) on `log1p(Density)`; sets flagged `Density`/`Biomass` to `NA` if `drop_outliers = TRUE` | `dt`, with `attr(dt,"flagged_outliers")` |
| `compute_fg_densities_by_stratum(dt, strata = TRUE)` | Sums species density within FG per sample, then across samples by area/year/[stratum]/FG | `per_group_fg` (`density_sum`) |
| `compute_strata_area_by_area(area_ids, area_shp, area_id_col, strata_def, cache_path = NULL, resolution = 1)` | Downloads NOAA bathymetry per area, bins cell area into depth strata (latitude-corrected); optional CSV cache | `strata_area_by_area` table |
| `weight_by_strata(per_group_fg, n_samples_by_stratum, strata_area_by_area)` | Core strata-weighted estimator: `D_area = Σ_h (density_sum_h/n_samples_h) · area_prop_h` | `fg_index`, with `attr(fg_index,"per_stratum")` |
| `simple_area_density(per_group_fg, n_samples_by_area)` | Unstratified path (`strata = FALSE`) | `data.table` |
| `weight_by_area(fg_index_stratified)` | Region-wide weighting from absolute stratum areas (`per_stratum` attribute) — requires the stratified path upstream | `fg_index_regional` |
| `compute_species_densities_by_stratum(dt, strata = TRUE)` / `weight_species_by_area(per_group_sp, n_samples_by_stratum, strata_area_by_area)` | Species-level analogs of the two functions above, feeding the `FG_spp` sheets | `data.table(Year, FG_num, FG_name, ScientificName, mean_density)` |
| `compute_cv_log_by_fg(value_dt, value_col, min_replicates = 2)` | `cv_log = sd(log1p(x))/mean(log1p(x))` per FG/Year, averaged across years — feeds the Ecosim "Weight" row | `data.table(FG_num, FG_name, cv_log, n_years)` |

## Plotting functions

| Function | Produces |
|---|---|
| `plot_sample_map(dt, area_shp, area_id_col, ..., by_year = FALSE, selected_areas = NULL, land_on_top = FALSE)` | Sample coverage map, points colored inside/outside the study area, polygons shaded by sample intensity |
| `plot_fg_timeseries_by_area(fg_index, ...)` / `plot_fg_timeseries_regional(fg_index_regional, ...)` | Faceted FG density time series, per-area vs. region-wide |
| `plot_strata_profile(fg_index_stratified, strata_def, ...)` | Depth-strata validation bar chart (pre-area-weighting, per-sample density by stratum) |

## Excel workbook functions (2026-09-17 revision)

As of the 2026-09-17 CSV-native refactor, `ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx`
carries **only the 10 final target sheets** — every native/intermediate
table each block used to also keep as a workbook sheet is now CSV-only
(see "Per-block output CSV subfolders" and "Final target sheet order"
below). The table below reflects the current function signatures, not the
older ones this document used to describe.

| Function | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `upsert_workbook_sheets(sheets, out_path)` | Adds/replaces the named sheets in `out_path`, **leaves every other sheet untouched**. Creates the workbook if it doesn't exist yet. Safe to call repeatedly, from any script, in any order. |
| `write_native_sheet_csv(dt, name, out_dir)` / `write_native_sheets_csv(sheets, out_dir)` / `read_native_sheet_csv(name, out_dir)` | Generic CSV read/write for a block's own native/intermediate tables — `out_dir` is always passed explicitly by the caller (the block's own subfolder), never assumed |
| `read_existing_ts_years(out_path, sheet_name, csv_dir = NULL)` | Reads year values from column 1 of an existing native CSV sheet (e.g. `Ecosim`), so `Catches_Ecosim`'s years stay aligned with it. `csv_dir` defaults to `dirname(out_path)` for back-compat; the numbered scripts pass the correct block's subfolder explicitly |
| `read_full_fg_reference(out_path, csv_dir = NULL)` | Reads `FG_lookup.csv` (a biomass-block output) — the full FG_num/FG_name reference, used to expand PB_QB/FG_References/etc. to every model FG, not just the ones with data this run |
| `export_ecopath_ecosim_excel(fg_index_regional, species_density_regional, n_samples_by_area_year, dataframe2, year_ecopath, ts_years, out_path, species_taxonomy = NULL, fg_cv_log = NULL, normalize_ts = TRUE, csv_out_dir = NULL)` | Writes the final `Ecopath_B` sheet directly to the workbook, plus native/intermediate CSVs (`FG_spp_Ecopath`, `FG_lookup`, `Ecosim`, `FG_spp_Ecosim`, a `PB_QB_spp` scaffold) into `csv_out_dir` (01_biomass.R's own `output/biomass/` subfolder) |
| `add_catches_to_ecopath_workbook(fg_catch, fg_lookup, out_path, year_ecopath, area_km2, ts_years = NULL, fleet_structure = NULL, csv_out_dir = NULL, biomass_csv_dir = NULL)` | Converts raw catch tonnes to t/km² density; writes native CSVs `Catches_Ecopath`/`Catches_Ecosim`/`Fleet_Structure` into `csv_out_dir` (02_fisheries.R's `output/fisheries/`). Reads `Ecosim.csv` from `biomass_csv_dir` when `ts_years` isn't passed explicitly |
| `add_pbqb_to_ecopath_workbook(fg_weighted, out_path, species_pb_qb = NULL, csv_out_dir = NULL, biomass_csv_dir = NULL)` | Writes native CSVs `PB_QB`/`PB_QB_spp` into `csv_out_dir` (03_pbqb-traits.R's `output/pbqb-traits/`). Reads `FG_lookup.csv` from `biomass_csv_dir` to expand to every model FG |
| `finalize_ecopath_ecosim_summary_sheets(out_path, year_ecopath, biomass_csv_dir = NULL, fisheries_csv_dir = NULL)` | Builds the final `Ecosim_ts` sheet by combining native CSVs from **two** blocks — `Ecosim` (biomass) plus `Catches_Discards_FG_ts`/`Catches_Ecosim`/`Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet` (fisheries) — hence the two separate directory arguments |
| `build_fg_references_sheet(out_path, biomass_csv_dir = NULL, fisheries_csv_dir = NULL, pbqb_csv_dir = NULL, diet_csv_dir = NULL)` | Builds the final `FG_References` sheet — one row per FG, consolidating which data source fed each block (see "The FG_References sheet" below) |
| `trim_workbook_to_final_sheets(out_path)` | **Run last**, by every numbered script. Reduces the workbook to exactly the 10 final target sheets (see below), dropping any native/intermediate sheet a much older run of this workbook might still be carrying |

The diagram below shows which script/function writes which sheet into the
one shared workbook, and the final trim that runs after each script.

```{mermaid}
%%| fig-cap: "Excel workbook assembly - which script/function writes which native CSV or final sheet into the one shared workbook, and the final trim that runs after each script."
%%{init: {'flowchart': {'padding': 20, 'nodeSpacing': 60, 'rankSpacing': 90}, 'themeVariables': {'fontSize': '18px'}}}%%
flowchart LR
    S1["01_biomass.R&lt;br/&gt;export_ecopath_ecosim_excel()"] --> W["ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx&lt;br/&gt;(shared workbook, top-level out_dir)"]

    S5["build_fg_references_sheet()&lt;br/&gt;(run by 04_diets.R, last)"] --> W
    W --> F["trim_workbook_to_final_sheets()&lt;br/&gt;run after every script"]
    F --> O["output/ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx&lt;br/&gt;(10 final sheets only)"]
```
