## Per-block output CSV subfolders (2026-09-17)

Every native/intermediate CSV a block writes now lands in that block's own
subfolder under the shared `out_dir`, instead of a single flat directory
shared by all four scripts:

* `01_biomass.R` → `output/biomass/`
* `02_fisheries.R` → `output/fisheries/`
* `03_pbqb-traits.R` → `output/pbqb-traits/`
* `04_diets.R` → `output/diet/`

`ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx` itself stays at the top-level `output/` — all
four scripts read and write it, so it has no single owning block. Each
script defines its own `csv_out_dir` near the top and passes it (or a
`*_csv_dir` pointing at a *different* block's subfolder, for the handful of
cross-block reads — species density, strata area, `FG_lookup.csv`,
`Ecosim.csv`, `biomass_proportion_by_species_fg.csv`, catch time series)
into the library functions above. Every one of those new directory
arguments defaults to `dirname(out_path)` (the workbook's own top-level
folder) when omitted, so nothing breaks if some other caller doesn't pass
them.

## Final target sheet order (10 sheets)

`target_order`, used by `trim_workbook_to_final_sheets()` and run after
every numbered script:

1. `info` — run metadata (areas, countries, YEAR_ECOPATH, TS_YEARS, ...)
2. `FG_spp` — FG × species × taxonomy list, written by `01_biomass.R`
3. `Ecopath_B` — FG-level biomass (t/km²), `YEAR_ECOPATH`-averaged
4. `Ecopath_L` — FG × Fleet landings, written by `02_fisheries.R`
5. `Ecopath_Di` — FG × Fleet discards, written by `02_fisheries.R`
6. `Ecopath_PBQB` — FG-level PB/QB, written by `03_pbqb-traits.R`
7. `Ecopath_traits` — FG_name/species-level traits, written by `01_biomass.R`
8. `Ecopath_diet` — predator FG × prey FG diet matrix, written by `04_diets.R`
9. `Ecosim_ts` — combined B/L/Di/Effort time series (see `finalize_ecopath_ecosim_summary_sheets()`)
10. `FG_References` — one row per FG, the data-source/citation summary (see below) — **added 2026-09-17**

Every native/intermediate table (`Ecopath`, `Catches_Ecopath`, `PB_QB`,
`Ecosim`, `Catches_Ecosim`, `Fishing_Effort_by_Fleet`, `FG_spp_Ecopath`,
`FG_spp_Ecosim`, `FG_lookup`, `traits_ewe`, `fg_traits_weighted`,
`PB_QB_spp`, `References` [species-parameter-level, `03_pbqb-traits.R`'s
own audit CSV — a different table from the `FG_References` workbook sheet
above, despite the similar name], `Ecobase`, `Catches_Discards_FG_ts`,
`diet_references_by_fg`, ...) lives only as CSV, in its own block's
subfolder, never as a workbook sheet — `trim_workbook_to_final_sheets()`
drops anything else it finds regardless.

## The FG_References sheet

Added 2026-09-17, built by `build_fg_references_sheet()`
(`lib_survey_fg_density_functions.R`), run by `04_diets.R` after every other
script has had a chance to run. One row per FG, with columns:

| Column | What it summarizes | Read from |
|---|---|---|
| `FG_num`, `FG_name` | The full FG reference | `FG_lookup.csv` (`output/biomass/`) |
| `ref_B` | Which biomass source (MEDITS/MEDIAS survey vs. stock assessment) fed `Ecopath_B`/`Ecosim_ts`'s B block for this FG | `biomass_source` in `survey_fg_annual_index_regional_combined.csv` (`output/biomass/`) |
| `ref_fisheries` | Which catch/discard source (GFCM/FDI/SAU-derived vs. stock assessment) fed `Ecopath_L`/`Ecopath_Di`/`Ecosim_ts`'s catch time series for this FG | `catch_source` in `Catches_Discards_FG_ts.csv` (`output/fisheries/`) |
| `ref_pbqb_traits` | Whether `PB_FG`/`QB_FG` came from the empirical species-level calculation or an EcoBase literature gap-fill | `PB_source`/`QB_source` in `PB_QB.csv` (`output/pbqb-traits/`), falling back to a generic "empirical" note when EcoBase gap-filling didn't run |
| `ref_diet` | Which diet-study citations (the metaweb's own `Reference` column) fed this FG's row/columns in `Ecopath_diet` | `diet_references_by_fg.csv` (`output/diet/`), rolled up from `build_species_diet()`'s per-predator citations via the same species→FG mapping the diet matrix itself uses |
| `ref_methods` | The calculation METHOD/literature behind `PB_FG`/`QB_FG` (as opposed to `ref_pbqb_traits`, which is about the *data*, not the method) | `dispatch_group` in `PB_QB_spp.csv` (`output/pbqb-traits/`), mapped to a literature citation |

:::: {.callout-important}
## This is a summary, not a full audit trail

Each column is a best-effort summary (distinct values `" | "`-joined when a
FG drew on more than one source across years/species/studies) — the
individual block's own CSV named in the table above has the complete
detail. A column is left blank for a FG when the block behind it hasn't
run yet against this workbook, with a message saying so rather than an
error; running `build_fg_references_sheet()` again later (as `04_diets.R`
does every run) fills in whatever is available by then.
::::

---

# Other library files

## `lib_worms_taxonomy_lookup.R`

Library file, no top-level driver code — `source()`'d automatically by the
numbered scripts. Defines a single function:

**`worms_taxonomy_lookup(names_vector)`** — the single taxonomy source used
everywhere in the pipeline (so genus/family comparisons are always
apples-to-apples, never a FishBase-derived family compared against a
WoRMS-derived one for the same actual family). Strips `"Genus spp."`
suffixes so genus-level placeholders still resolve, chunks the query list
(50 names/chunk) against `worrms::wm_records_names()`, picks the best
(`status == "accepted"` preferred) match per name, and returns
`data.table(original_name, search_term, AphiaID, matched_name, rank, status,
phylum, class, order, family, genus)`. Explicitly namespaces `purrr::map()`
rather than calling `map()` bare — the `maps` package (loaded elsewhere in
this pipeline for basemap plotting) also exports an unrelated `map()`, and
whichever package attached most recently silently wins an unqualified call.

## `lib_build_species_df_from_survey.R`

Library file — bridges `01_biomass.R`'s survey output and
`03_pbqb-traits.R`'s PB/QB estimation. Defines:

**`build_species_df_from_survey(survey_csv_path, year_ecopath_range,
out_rds_path = NULL)`** — reads `species_density_regional_combined.csv`,
restricts to `YEAR_ECOPATH`'s snapshot years (the same years used for the
`Ecopath` sheet's Biomass column, so PB/QB weighting and the reported Biomass
describe the same time snapshot), collapses to one mean-density `Biomass`
value per species (stops with an error if any species maps to more than one
FG within that year range — shouldn't happen given upstream FG-matching
logic), sets `Yield := NA` (this survey pipeline has no catch/landings data
of its own — F = Yield/Biomass isn't computable from it directly). Optionally
saves the result as an `.rds`. Returns
`species_df(Species, FG, FG_name, Biomass, Yield)`.

---

# Standalone cartography utilities (not part of the numbered pipeline)

## `export_gsa_boundaries.R`

Standalone — does not need `01_biomass.R` or the shared
library sourced first. Downloads the same GFCM GSA shapefile via the same URL
and GSA-numbering fix (`SMU_CODE` 111/112 → 11). **Western-Med-specific as
delivered:** restricts to GSA 1–11 — change the `gsa_num %in% 1:11` filter to
any other GSA range to export a different region's boundaries. Simplifies
the result for web embedding via
`rmapshaper::ms_simplify(keep = 0.05, keep_shapes = TRUE)` (keeps small
polygons like Alboran Island, GSA 2, from being deleted outright), and writes
`gsa_boundaries.geojson` — the file embedded in
`medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd`'s interactive Leaflet map.

## `fig_WMed_basemap.R`

Standalone map-generation script (not sourced by anything else), purely
cartographic — not required by the numbered pipeline at all.
**Western-Med-specific as delivered:** downloads the GFCM GSA shapefile,
splits polygons into Western Med (GSA 1–11, the target study area) vs.
context GSAs (12, 16 — Northern Tunisia, South of Sicily) and draws the
Sicily Channel divide (Cape Bon–Cape Lilibeo) as the Western/Central
Mediterranean boundary; all three of those choices would need editing to
produce an equivalent basemap for a different region. The general
technique — label placement via the "pole of inaccessibility" algorithm
(`polylabelr`, more robust than `st_point_on_surface()` for narrow,
coastline-hugging GSAs) plus a high-resolution (`rnaturalearthhires`)
coastline — is reusable as-is for any region. Output:
`output/plots/westmed_gsa_map.png`.

---

# Adapting this pipeline to a different region

Everything below applies whether the target region is a single GSA, an
arbitrary GSA subset, the whole Mediterranean GSA-by-GSA, or a fully
custom (non-GSA) boundary.

## Preferred method: source the scripts with pre-set options

`01_biomass.R`, `02_fisheries.R`,
`03_pbqb-traits.R`, and `04_diets.R` are **source-able**: every region/year/method knob each
one uses (`FILTER_AREAS`, `TARGET_COUNTRIES`, `YEAR_ECOPATH`,
`START_YEAR`/`END_YEAR`, `TS_YEARS`, `DATASET_VERSION`,
`FISHERIES_DATA_SOURCE`, `OUTLIER_METHOD`, `PB_QB_SELECTION_MODE`,
`out_dir`/`pcloud_dir`/`git_dir`, and a handful more — each script's own
"Configuration" section, under a comment block headed `SOURCE-ABLE
SCRIPT`, lists exactly which variables it recognizes) is wrapped in
`if (!exists("VAR", envir = .GlobalEnv, inherits = FALSE)) VAR <- <default>`.
That means:

* If a variable is **already set** in the calling environment before the
  script is `source()`-d, the script uses that value and skips its own
  default entirely.
* If it is **not set**, the script falls back to its original Western Med
  default (GSA 1–11, 1994–2023) — running any of the three scripts on its
  own, exactly as before, is completely unchanged.

`run_pipeline_demo.R` is a driver script demonstrating this: it sets
`out_dir`/`pcloud_dir`/`git_dir` once, then `source()`s
`01_biomass.R` → `02_fisheries.R` →
`03_pbqb-traits.R` → `04_diets.R` in that dependency order (01 must run
first — see [Adapting this pipeline to a different region] below and each
script's own header comment for why; 04 specifically needs 01's
`FG_spp_Ecopath` sheet in the shared workbook for its species'
share-of-FG-biomass values, and is otherwise optional — diet composition
isn't needed for every run). It includes two ready-to-run examples,
switched via a `RUN_MODE` flag at the top:

* `RUN_MODE <- "westmed_default"` — sets nothing beyond the three paths;
  every script uses its built-in West Med default.
* `RUN_MODE <- "custom_example"` — sets `FILTER_AREAS <- 17:18`,
  `TARGET_COUNTRIES <- c("Italy", "Croatia", "Slovenia")`,
  `YEAR_ECOPATH <- 2005:2007`, `TS_YEARS <- 2000:2020`, and
  `START_YEAR`/`END_YEAR <- 2000`/`2020` as a worked Adriatic-GSA example
  — copy and adapt this block for any other region/year range, setting
  only the variables that need to differ from the West Med default.

`YEAR_ECOPATH` in particular is read by all three scripts and must stay
consistent across them (it defines the single Biomass snapshot the PB/QB
weights are computed against) — setting it once in the driver script
before any of the three are sourced keeps them in sync automatically,
which is easy to forget when editing each script's hardcoded copy by hand.

This sourcing approach and the manual per-script editing approach
described in the rest of this section are not mutually exclusive — the
`if (!exists(...))` guards mean a variable can still be hardcoded by
editing the script directly (equivalent to always setting it), or left
alone to pick up a caller's override, on a variable-by-variable basis.

**To model a different GSA or set of GSAs** (Adriatic, Ionian, Aegean,
Levant, or the whole basin), starting from `01_biomass.R` as
a template:

* `FILTER_AREAS` — set to the target GSA number(s). No other GSA-based code
  in `lib_survey_fg_density_functions.R` hardcodes a Western-Med-only
  assumption — `compute_strata_area_by_area()`, `weight_by_strata()`,
  `weight_by_area()` etc. are all region-agnostic and operate on whatever
  `area_ids`/`area_shp` they're given.
* `ALBORAN_GSAS`/`fix_alboran_longitude_sign()` — a Western-Med-only
  correction (GSA 1–3); harmless no-op elsewhere, delete if not needed.
* `MEDIAS_HANDBOOK_AREAS`/`GSA_AREA_CROSSWALK` — only populated for Western
  Med GSAs; add the target region's own rows from the MEDIAS Handbook
  (or rely entirely on the bathymetry-derived 10–200 m fallback if MEDIAS
  data or handbook-reported areas aren't available for that region).
* `out_dir`/`pcloud_dir`/`git_dir` paths, and the raw MEDITS/MEDIAS/FG-
  reference file paths under them — point at the target region's own data.
* `export_gsa_boundaries.R`/`fig_WMed_basemap.R` — optional cartography
  only; edit the GSA-range filters if you want an equivalent map for the
  new region, otherwise skip both entirely (nothing downstream needs them).

**To model a fully custom (non-GSA) region anywhere in the Mediterranean**,
use `01_survey_density_custom.R` directly: set `AREA_NAME`, `CUSTOM_AREA_TYPE`
(`"bbox"` or `"shapefile"`), and `CUSTOM_BBOX`/`CUSTOM_SHAPEFILE_PATH` to the
target boundary — no GSA-specific code path is involved at all in this
script (the Alboran-sign fix is present but is a documented no-op outside
GSA 1–3).

Everything downstream of Step 1 — `02_fao_catches.R`, `03b_ecobase.R`,
`03_pbqb-traits.R`, and the shared library's Excel-export/PB-QB functions — is
already region-agnostic: they operate on whatever `strata_area_by_area.csv`
and `species_density_regional_combined.csv` Step 1 produced, with no
Western-Med-specific logic beyond `02_fao_catches.R`'s `area_filter_values`
(the FAO/GFCM division codes 37.1.1–37.1.3, which likewise need changing to
match the target region's own FAO division codes) and `03b_ecobase.R`'s
`MED_LON_MIN/MAX`/`MED_LAT_MIN/MAX` bounding box (already covers the **full**
Mediterranean by default — Gibraltar to the Levant — so it does not need
editing for a sub-region; narrow it only if you specifically want to exclude
literature models from elsewhere in the basin).

---

# Script 5 — `04_diets.R`

**Role:** Pipeline Step 5, optional — builds an EwE diet-composition
matrix (predator FG × prey FG) from the Mediterranean trophic
metaweb database (`data_entry_metaweb_empty.xlsx` - currently an empty template, no DATA_ENTRY rows yet), collapsed from species-level
predator-prey records up to the same Functional Group scheme used
everywhere else in the pipeline. Auto-runs on `source()`, same convention
as `01_biomass.R`/`02_fisheries.R`/`03_pbqb-traits.R` — no separate
function call needed afterward.

**Dependency:** must run **after** `01_biomass.R` has written to the same
`ECOPATH_WORKBOOK_PATH` — it reads `biomass_proportion_by_species_fg.csv`
(01_biomass.R's own native CSV output, `output/biomass/` — **not** a
workbook sheet) for each species' share of its Functional Group's total
biomass ("proportion B"), which weights that species' own diet more heavily
within its FG's combined diet the larger a share of the FG's biomass it
represents. If that CSV isn't there yet (01 hasn't run against this
workbook), it falls back to `SPECIES_BIOMASS_IN_FG_CSV_PATH` (a small manual
CSV, same schema: species/fg_name/biomass_proportion) for whichever
species×FG pairs are still missing, and finally to an equal split among an
FG's remaining mapped species for anything neither source covers.

## Inputs

| File | Format | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/Complementary data/data_entry_metaweb_empty.xlsx` | xlsx | Mediterranean trophic metaweb — predator-prey diet records (`DATA_ENTRY`), taxonomic code lookup (`Taxonomic_codes`), generic/non-taxonomic prey-category lookup (`Non_taxonomic_groups`) |
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/FG_WMed.xlsx`, sheet 4 | xlsx | Species → Functional Group reference — same file `01_biomass.R` uses, so every species already assigned an FG there needs no manual entry here |
| `output/biomass/biomass_proportion_by_species_fg.csv` | csv | Written by `01_biomass.R` — species' share of biomass within their FG ("proportion B"), the preferred source |
| `output/biomass/FG_lookup.csv` | csv | Full FG_num/FG_name reference, used to resolve `FG_num` for the new `diet_references_by_fg.csv` output below |
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/species_to_fg_missing.csv` | csv | Manual species→FG fallback, only for species this run needs that aren't in `FG_WMed.xlsx` at all |
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/species_biomass_in_fg_missing.csv` | csv | Manual biomass-share fallback, only for species×FG pairs missing from `biomass_proportion_by_species_fg.csv` |
| `<pcloud_dir>/data/ewe_group_table.csv` | csv | EwE group numbering (group_number/group_name/is_predator) — defines row/column order and which FGs get a predator column in the exported matrix |

## Outputs

* `output/diet/diet_composition_ewe.csv` (2026-09-17: under `output/diet/`,
  not the top-level `output/`) — EwE's own plain-text diet-composition
  import format: blank + "Prey \\ predator" header, predator columns by
  group number (`is_predator` groups only), prey rows by group number +
  name (full group list), decimal-comma **quoted** values (unquoted would
  silently split into extra columns, since the delimiter is also a comma),
  `Import`/`Sum`/`1-Sum` rows at the bottom.
* `output/diet/diet_references_by_fg.csv` — **added 2026-09-17** — one row
  per FG, the distinct diet-study citations (the metaweb's own `Reference`
  column) behind every species that maps into that FG as a predator. Read
  back by `build_fg_references_sheet()` to populate the final workbook's
  `FG_References` sheet's `ref_diet` column (see the shared-library section
  below).
* `output/ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx`, sheet **`Ecopath_diet`** — the same
  predator-FG × prey-FG matrix, written into the shared workbook as plain
  numeric values (no decimal-comma/quoting — that convention is specific
  to EwE's plain-text import, not needed inside an Excel sheet). Columns:
  `Prey_FG_num`, `Prey_FG_name`, then one `Predator_<num>_<name>` column per
  predator FG.
* `output/ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx`, sheet **`FG_References`** — built
  here too (04_diets.R runs last), after `Ecopath_diet` — see "The
  FG_References sheet" in the shared-library section below.

## Key functions

| Function | Role |
|---|---|
| `read_metaweb(path)` | Reads `DATA_ENTRY`/`Taxonomic_codes`/`Non_taxonomic_groups` from the metaweb workbook |
| `build_code_lookup(tax_codes, nontax_groups)` | Builds one code→name lookup from both tables (Taxonomic_codes wins on a code collision) |
| `resolve_codes(codes, lookup)` | Predator/prey code → scientific name or generic-category label |
| `pick_diet_metric(dt)` | Picks one diet-proportion value per `DATA_ENTRY` row via `DIET_METRIC_PRIORITY` (IRI preferred; Presence-only records get an equal split among a study's present prey) |
| `build_species_diet(data_entry, lookup)` | Species-level predator→prey diet proportions, weighted by study sample size and normalized per predator |
| `read_species_to_fg_from_reference(path, sheet)` | Reads `FG_WMed.xlsx`'s species→FG assignment |
| `build_species_to_fg(needed_species, reference_path, missing_csv_path)` | FG_WMed.xlsx first, manual CSV fallback only for genuinely missing species |
| `read_biomass_proportion_from_workbook(workbook_path, sheet = "FG_spp_Ecopath")` | Reads species' share of FG biomass from `01_biomass.R`'s own output — the preferred "proportion B" source |
| `build_biomass_in_fg(needed_species_fg, workbook_path, fallback_csv_path)` | Combines the workbook source with the manual fallback CSV, reporting coverage counts at each tier |
| `build_fg_diet(species_diet, species_to_fg, biomass_in_fg)` | Expands species-level diet to FG×FG, biomass-weighting each predator species' contribution within its FG, then collapses to one number per (predator FG, prey FG) |
| `export_ewe_matrix_csv(fg_diet, group_table, out_path)` | Writes the EwE plain-text import CSV |
| `build_ecopath_diet_sheet(fg_diet, group_table)` | Builds the same matrix as a plain numeric data.table for the `Ecopath_diet` sheet |
| `run_pipeline(...)` | Runs all of the above end to end; called unconditionally at the bottom of the script |

---

# Methodology document — `medbs_pipeline_methodology.qmd`

A Quarto methodology write-up (`format: html`, all R chunks `eval: false` —
illustrative, not executed at render time) documenting
`lib_survey_fg_density_functions.R` and the two Step-1 driver scripts. Key
features:

* **Embedded interactive map** — the actual `gsa_boundaries.geojson` (from
  `export_gsa_boundaries.R`) is inlined as a JSON blob and rendered via
  Leaflet.js. Lets a reader toggle between the GSA-based and custom-region
  workflows, live-preview `FILTER_AREAS`/`CUSTOM_BBOX`, and generate a
  ready-to-paste R config block — all client-side JavaScript, no R executes
  behind it.
* **Section structure:** Overview (with mermaid diagrams for the pipeline,
  FG-matching sub-flow, and density/weighting sub-flow) → Input files →
  Interactive configuration → Functional Group matching (worked examples of
  each matching stage) → Study-area definition (GSA vs. custom) → Per-haul
  density and corrections (swept-area formula, depth strata, catchability,
  outliers) → Strata-weighted density → Region-wide aggregation → MEDIAS
  acoustic survey → Validation plots → Sample coverage map → Outputs → a
  full `ecopath_ecosim_inputs.xlsx` deep-dive, including the same final
  sheet-order table reproduced above.
* Real output files are copied into `figures/`/`downloads/` folders next to
  the rendered document (via `safe_include_graphics()`/`safe_download_link()`
  helpers) so the rendered HTML is portable off the author's own machine,
  since `out_dir` is otherwise an absolute local path.
